${\mathchoice{\OOp\left(2^{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{3}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{3})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{3})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{3})}}\right)}{\OOp(2^{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{3}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{3})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{3})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{3})}})}{\OOp(2^{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{3}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{3})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{3})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{3})}})}{\OOp(2^{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{3}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{3})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{3})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{3})}})}}=2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{3}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{3})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{3})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{3})}}$ as well.
The number of different $\assignOp$ functions in any configuration can be bounded by $\left(2^{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\right)^{{\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{T}_{S}\right|}{{|\mathcal{T}_{S}|}}{{|\mathcal{T}_{S}|}}{{|\mathcal{T}_{S}|}}}\cdot 2{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\cdot{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}}\leq\left(2^{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\right)^{{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\cdot 2{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\cdot{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}}=2^{{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}}}$ and enumerated in time ${\mathchoice{\OOp\left(2^{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}}\right)}{\OOp(2^{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}})}{\OOp(2^{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}})}{\OOp(2^{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}})}}=2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}}$ as well.
Therefore, the number of different viable configurations and by extension ${\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\signatureOp\left(C\right)}{\signatureOp(C)}{\signatureOp(C)}{\signatureOp(C)}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\signatureOp\left(C\right)}{\signatureOp(C)}{\signatureOp(C)}{\signatureOp(C)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\signatureOp\left(C\right)}{\signatureOp(C)}{\signatureOp(C)}{\signatureOp(C)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\signatureOp\left(C\right)}{\signatureOp(C)}{\signatureOp(C)}{\signatureOp(C)}}|}}}$ is bounded by $2^{{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}}}$ and can be enumerated in this running time as well.
\qed\end@proof
\par Finally, we exploit this fact, to show, that the signature of a component can be computed reasonably fast.
\par\begin{lemma}Let $C\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S\right)}{\compOp({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S)}{\compOp({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S)}{\compOp({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S)}}$.
We can compute ${\mathchoice{\signatureOp\left(C\right)}{\signatureOp(C)}{\signatureOp(C)}{\signatureOp(C)}}$ with running time $2^{{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}}}$.
\end{lemma}
\@proof First note, that all viable configurations—a super-set of ${\mathchoice{\signatureOp\left(C\right)}{\signatureOp(C)}{\signatureOp(C)}{\signatureOp(C)}}$—can be enumerated in running time $2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}}$.
Consider any viable configuration $\gamma$.
Given some $(\sigma,\mathcal{F},\pi)$ it is possible to check in linear time, whether $(\sigma,\mathcal{F},\pi)$ gives rise to $\gamma$ on $C$.
Denote with $\mathcal{E}\coloneqq E(\mathcal{F})$ the edge sets of the disjoint trees of the possible solution.
As all $F\in\mathcal{F}$ intersect with $E(C^{+})$ and since ${\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{F}\right|}{{|\mathcal{F}|}}{{|\mathcal{F}|}}{{|\mathcal{F}|}}}\leq u$, we have that $\mathcal{E}\cup\left\{E({\mathchoice{\confInstOp\left(C,\gamma,\sigma\right)}{\confInstOp(C,\gamma,\sigma)}{\confInstOp(C,\gamma,\sigma)}{\confInstOp(C,\gamma\sigma)}})\setminus\bigcup\mathcal{E})\right\}$ partitions $E({\mathchoice{\confInstOp\left(C,\gamma,\sigma\right)}{\confInstOp(C,\gamma,\sigma)}{\confInstOp(C,\gamma,\sigma)}{\confInstOp(C,\gamma\sigma)}})$ into at most $u+1$ parts.
So, it is easy to enumerate a superset $\mathscr{X}$ of all $(\sigma,\mathcal{F},\pi)$ that solve ${\mathchoice{\confInstOp\left(C,\gamma,\sigma\right)}{\confInstOp(C,\gamma,\sigma)}{\confInstOp(C,\gamma,\sigma)}{\confInstOp(C,\gamma\sigma)}}$ and therefore gives rise to $\gamma$ on $C$.
We set $\mathscr{X}$ to be the set of all surjections $\sigma\colon{\mathchoice{\left[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\right]}{[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}]}}\to C$, all partitions of $E({\mathchoice{\confInstOp\left(C,\gamma,\sigma\right)}{\confInstOp(C,\gamma,\sigma)}{\confInstOp(C,\gamma,\sigma)}{\confInstOp(C,\gamma\sigma)}})$ into at most $u+1$ parts and functions from the current $\mathcal{F}$ to the set of all terminal sets $\mathcal{U}$.
\par The number of surjections is bounded by ${\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}=2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}}$.
Since $${\mathchoice{\left|E({\mathchoice{\confInstOp\left(C,\gamma,\sigma\right)}{\confInstOp(C,\gamma,\sigma)}{\confInstOp(C,\gamma,\sigma)}{\confInstOp(C,\gamma\sigma)}})\right|}{{|E({\mathchoice{\confInstOp\left(C,\gamma,\sigma\right)}{\confInstOp(C,\gamma,\sigma)}{\confInstOp(C,\gamma,\sigma)}{\confInstOp(C,\gamma\sigma)}})|}}{{|E({\mathchoice{\confInstOp\left(C,\gamma,\sigma\right)}{\confInstOp(C,\gamma,\sigma)}{\confInstOp(C,\gamma,\sigma)}{\confInstOp(C,\gamma\sigma)}})|}}{{|E({\mathchoice{\confInstOp\left(C,\gamma,\sigma\right)}{\confInstOp(C,\gamma,\sigma)}{\confInstOp(C,\gamma,\sigma)}{\confInstOp(C,\gamma\sigma)}})|}}}\leq u+\sum_{U\subseteq S}{\mathchoice{\left|U\right|}{{|U|}}{{|U|}}{{|U|}}}{\mathchoice{\demandOp\left(U\right)}{\demandOp(U)}{\demandOp(U)}{\demandOp(U)}}\leq u+u{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{2}={\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}},$$ the number of partitions of $E({\mathchoice{\confInstOp\left(C,\gamma,\sigma\right)}{\confInstOp(C,\gamma,\sigma)}{\confInstOp(C,\gamma,\sigma)}{\confInstOp(C,\gamma\sigma)}})$ into at most $u+1$ parts is bounded by $\sum_{i=1}^{u+1}i^{{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}}}\leq(u+1)\left(u+1\right)^{{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}}}=2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}}$.
Additionally, we have ${\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{U}\right|}{{|\mathcal{U}|}}{{|\mathcal{U}|}}{{|\mathcal{U}|}}}\leq{\mathchoice{\left|V(C)\right|}{{|V(C)|}}{{|V(C)|}}{{|V(C)|}}}+u+{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}={\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{2}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{2})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{2})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{2})}}$.
Therefore, the number of different possible $\pi$ is bounded by $\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{2}\right)^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{2}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{2})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{2})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{2})}}=2^{{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{2}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{2}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{2}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{2}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}}}$ and so ${\mathchoice{\left|\mathscr{X}\right|}{{|\mathscr{X}|}}{{|\mathscr{X}|}}{{|\mathscr{X}|}}}\leq 2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}}2^{{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}}}2^{{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{2}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{2}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{2}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{2}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}}}=2^{{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}}}$.
Thus, the running time to check whether $C$ admits $\gamma$ is bounded by $2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}}{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}}=2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}}$.
Since we need to check $2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}}$ different configurations, the running time to compute ${\mathchoice{\signatureOp\left(C\right)}{\signatureOp(C)}{\signatureOp(C)}{\signatureOp(C)}}$ is in $2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\log{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}}$ as well.
\qed\end@proof
\par\par\@@numbered@section{subsection}{toc}{Integer Linear Program Representation}
In this Section, we finally provide a linear programming representation of the instance of $\gstp$.
We first create separate integer-linear-program representations for \lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{item:valid-selector-rho-connected} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{item:valid-selector-assign-connected} in \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:valid-selector}.
Finally, we create a single ILP combining those such that this ILP has a feasible assignment if and only if the considered instance is positive.
\par To construct our ILPs, assume that $\Gamma$ is a configuration selector.
First we construct an ILP to check \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:valid-selector-rho-connected} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:valid-selector}.
For this, we assume that for all $U\subseteq S\cap V(G)$, ${\bm{s}}_{U}$ corresponds to $r_{U}$ of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:valid-selector}.
\begin{definition}For all $U\subseteq S\cap V(G)$, denote with $\mathcal{M}_{U}$ all minimally connected hypergraphs on the vertex set $U$.
Let ${\bm{s}}$ be indexed by $U\subseteq S\cap V(G)$.
Then, $\bm{\rho\text{-}linRep}({\bm{s}})$ is the ILP
\lx@equationgroup@subnumbering@begin
\@@amsalign
\lx@equationgroup@subnumbering@end
\end{definition}
\par For all $T\in\mathcal{T}^{\star}$, $T\subseteq U\subseteq S\cap V(G)$, and $H\in\mathcal{M}_{U}$, the variable ${\bm{p}}_{T,H}$ denotes how often the hypergraph $H$ is used to fulfill requirements of $T$.
For all $R\in E(H)$, the variable ${\bm{q}}_{T,H,R}$ denotes how often the hyperedge $R$ gets used to construct the hypergraph $H$ that gets assigned to $T$.
We first check, that enough hypergraphs are assigned to $T$.
Then, we check that each assigned hypergraph has enough edges available.
Finally, we check that each edge is not used more often globally than allowed.
\par Now we show, that indeed $\bm{\rho\text{-}linRep}({\bm{s}})$ fully captures \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:valid-selector-rho-connected} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:valid-selector} with few variables.
\begin{lemma}Assume that \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{rr:sensible-terminal-sets} is applied exhaustively and let ${\bm{s}}$ be a vector indexed by $U\subseteq S\cap V(G)$.
Then, $\bm{\rho\text{-}linRep}({\bm{s}})$ has
\begin{enumerate}\enumerate@item@$2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{2}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{2})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{2})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{2})}}$ variables,
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@a feasible assignment if and only if there is a function ${\rho\colon\mathcal{T}^{\star}\times\N\to 2^{2^{S\cap V(G)}}}$ that satisfies \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:valid-selector-rho-connected} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:valid-selector} and for all $U\subseteq S\cap V(G)$, it holds that $r_{U}\leq{\bm{s}}_{U}$.\end{enumerate}\end{lemma}
\@proof First, we bound the number of variables needed.
The number of ${\bm{s}}$ variables is bounded by $2^{{\mathchoice{\left|S\cap V(G)\right|}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}}}$.
Consider $T\in\mathcal{T}^{\star}$, $T\subseteq U\subseteq S\cap V(G)$ and $H\in\mathcal{M}_{U}$.
As $H$ is minimally-connected, ${\mathchoice{\left|E(H)\right|}{{|E(H)|}}{{|E(H)|}}{{|E(H)|}}}\leq{\mathchoice{\left|U\right|}{{|U|}}{{|U|}}{{|U|}}}-1$.
Any $F\in E(H)$ is chosen from $2^{{\mathchoice{\left|S\cap V(G)\right|}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}}}$ many possibilities.
As hypergraphs in $\mathcal{M}_{U}$ only differ by their hyperedges, we have ${\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{M}_{U}\right|}{{|\mathcal{M}_{U}|}}{{|\mathcal{M}_{U}|}}{{|\mathcal{M}_{U}|}}}\leq\binom{2^{{\mathchoice{\left|S\cap V(G)\right|}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|S\cap V(G)\right|}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}}}{{\mathchoice{\left|S\cap V(G)\right|}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}}}=2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\cap V(G)\right|}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}}^{2}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\cap V(G)\right|}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}}^{2})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\cap V(G)\right|}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}}^{2})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\cap V(G)\right|}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}}^{2})}}$.
Since ${\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{T}^{\star}\right|}{{|\mathcal{T}^{\star}|}}{{|\mathcal{T}^{\star}|}}{{|\mathcal{T}^{\star}|}}}\leq 2^{{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}}$, the number of ${\bm{p}}$ variables is bounded by ${\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{T}^{\star}\right|}{{|\mathcal{T}^{\star}|}}{{|\mathcal{T}^{\star}|}}{{|\mathcal{T}^{\star}|}}}2^{\mathchoice{\left|S\cap V(G)\right|}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}}2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\cap V(G)\right|}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}}^{2}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\cap V(G)\right|}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}}^{2})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\cap V(G)\right|}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}}^{2})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\cap V(G)\right|}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}}^{2})}}=2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{2}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{2})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{2})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{2})}}$.
For each ${\bm{p}}$ variable, the number of ${\bm{q}}$ variables is bounded by ${\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}-1$.
So, the number of ${\bm{q}}$ variables is bounded by $2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{2}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{2})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{2})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{2})}}$ as well.
\par Let ${\bm{s}}$ be given and let ${\bm{p}}$ and ${\bm{q}}$ be chosen such that the assignment is feasible.
By \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eq:rho-lin-rep-exact}, it is possible to choose $\rho$ such that for all $T\in\mathcal{T}^{\star}$, $T\subseteq U\subseteq S\cap V(G)$, and $H\in\mathcal{M}_{U}$ the number of $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[d(T)\right]}{[d(T)]}{[d(T)]}{[d(T)]}}$ with $\rho(T,i)=E(H)$ is exactly ${\bm{p}}_{H}$.
For all $T\in\mathcal{T}^{\star}$, we have ${\mathchoice{\left|T\right|}{{|T|}}{{|T|}}{{|T|}}}\geq 2$.
So, for all $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[d(T)\right]}{[d(T)]}{[d(T)]}{[d(T)]}}$, we have $T\subseteq\bigcup\rho(T,i)$.
As the hypergraph $(T\cup\bigcup\rho(T,i),\rho(T,i))=(\bigcup\rho(T,i),\rho(T,i))$ is contained in $\mathcal{M}_{\bigcup\rho(T,i)}$, it is minimally connected and $\rho$ satisfies \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:valid-selector-rho-connected} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:valid-selector}.
Additionally, let any $R\subseteq S\cap V(G)$ be given.
By \lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{eq:rho-lin-rep-enough-edges} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{eq:rho-lin-rep-enough-supply}, we have that
$$r_{R}={\mathchoice{\left|\{(T,i)\in\mathcal{T}^{\star}\times\N\mid R\in\rho(T,i)\}\right|}{{|\{(T,i)\in\mathcal{T}^{\star}\times\N\mid R\in\rho(T,i)\}|}}{{|\{(T,i)\in\mathcal{T}^{\star}\times\N\mid R\in\rho(T,i)\}|}}{{|\{(T,i)\in\mathcal{T}^{\star}\times\N\mid R\in\rho(T,i)\}|}}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}T\in\mathcal{T}^{\star},\\
T\subseteq U\subseteq S\cap V(G),\\
H\in\mathcal{M}_{U}\!\colon\\
R\in E(H)\end{subarray}}{\bm{p}}_{T,H}\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}T\in\mathcal{T}^{\star},\\
T\subseteq U\subseteq S\cap V(G),\\
H\in\mathcal{M}_{U}\!\colon\\
R\in E(H)\end{subarray}}{\bm{q}}_{T,H,U}\leq{\bm{s}}_{R}.$$
\par Now let $\rho$ satisfy \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:valid-selector-rho-connected} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:valid-selector} and for all $R\subseteq S\cap V(G)$ assume that $r_{R}\leq{\bm{s}}_{R}$.
For $T\in\mathcal{T}^{\star}$, $T\subseteq U\subseteq S\cap V(G)$ and $H\in\mathcal{M}_{U}$, we set ${\bm{p}}_{T,H}={\mathchoice{\left|\{i\in{\mathchoice{\left[d(T)\right]}{[d(T)]}{[d(T)]}{[d(T)]}}\mid E(H)=\rho(T,i)\}\right|}{{|\{i\in{\mathchoice{\left[d(T)\right]}{[d(T)]}{[d(T)]}{[d(T)]}}\mid E(H)=\rho(T,i)\}|}}{{|\{i\in{\mathchoice{\left[d(T)\right]}{[d(T)]}{[d(T)]}{[d(T)]}}\mid E(H)=\rho(T,i)\}|}}{{|\{i\in{\mathchoice{\left[d(T)\right]}{[d(T)]}{[d(T)]}{[d(T)]}}\mid E(H)=\rho(T,i)\}|}}}$ and for all $R\in E(H)$, we set ${\bm{q}}_{T,H,R}={\bm{p}}_{T,H}$.
By definition, \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eq:rho-lin-rep-enough-edges} holds.
Since ${\mathchoice{\left|T\right|}{{|T|}}{{|T|}}{{|T|}}}\geq 2$, for all $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[d(T)\right]}{[d(T)]}{[d(T)]}{[d(T)]}}$, we have $T\subseteq\bigcup\rho(T,i)$.
So, the hypergraph $(\bigcup\rho(T,i),\rho(T,i))$ is well-defined and minimally connected.
Therefore, $\sum_{T\subseteq U\subseteq S\cap V(G),H\in\mathcal{M}_{U}}{\bm{p}}_{T,H}=d(T)$, satisfying \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eq:rho-lin-rep-exact}.
Finally consider any $R\subseteq S\cap V(G)$.
By choice of ${\bm{p}}$ and ${\bm{q}}$, we have that
$$\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}T\in\mathcal{T}^{\star},\\
T\subseteq U\subseteq S\cap V(G),\\
H\in\mathcal{M}_{U}\!\colon\\
R\in E(H)\end{subarray}}{\bm{q}}_{T,H,U}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}T\in\mathcal{T}^{\star},\\
T\subseteq U\subseteq S\cap V(G),\\
H\in\mathcal{M}_{U}\!\colon\\
R\in E(H)\end{subarray}}{\bm{p}}_{T,H}={\mathchoice{\left|\{(T,i)\in\mathcal{T}^{\star}\times\N\mid R\in\rho(T,i)\}\right|}{{|\{(T,i)\in\mathcal{T}^{\star}\times\N\mid R\in\rho(T,i)\}|}}{{|\{(T,i)\in\mathcal{T}^{\star}\times\N\mid R\in\rho(T,i)\}|}}{{|\{(T,i)\in\mathcal{T}^{\star}\times\N\mid R\in\rho(T,i)\}|}}}=r_{R}\leq{\bm{s}}_{R},$$
showing that \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eq:rho-lin-rep-enough-supply} is satisfied as well.
\qed\end@proof
\par Now, we construct an ILP to check \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:valid-selector-assign-connected} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:valid-selector}.
For this, we assume that for all $T\in\mathcal{T}_{S}$, $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[d(T)\right]}{[d(T)]}{[d(T)]}{[d(T)]}}$, and $U\subseteq S\cap V(G)$, the value ${\bm{a}}_{T,i,U}$ corresponds to the indicator variable whether there is a $C\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S\right)}{\compOp({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S)}{\compOp({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S)}{\compOp({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S)}}$, and $j\in{\mathchoice{\left[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\right]}{[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}]}}$ with $U={\mathchoice{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}\left(T,i,j\right)}{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}(T,i,j)}{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}(T,i,j)}{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}(T,i,j)}}$.
\par\begin{definition}Let ${\bm{a}}$ be indexed by $T\in\mathcal{T}_{S}$, $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[d(T)\right]}{[d(T)]}{[d(T)]}{[d(T)]}}$, and $U\subseteq S\cap V(G)$.
Then, $\bm{assign\text{-}linRep}({\bm{a}})$ is the ILP
\lx@equationgroup@subnumbering@begin
\@@amsalign
\lx@equationgroup@subnumbering@end
\end{definition}
\par In the above definition, for all $T\in\mathcal{T}_{S}$ and $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[d(T)\right]}{[d(T)]}{[d(T)]}{[d(T)]}}$, we essentially choose one $T\subseteq U\subseteq S\cap V(G)$, where $U$ is the vertex set of the hypergraph considered in \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:valid-selector-assign-connected} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:valid-selector}.
This is the unique $U$ with ${\bm{b}}_{T,i,U}=1$.
First, we encode that at least one hyperedge is present in the considered hypergraph.
Then, we ensure that the hypergraph is well formed.
To do so, we check that all sets $Y$ that get assigned to (i.e., ${\bm{a}}_{T,i,Y}=1$) actually only use vertices of $U$.
Finally, we ensure that each cut of $U$ is crossed by at least one edge.
\par Now we show, that indeed $\bm{assign\text{-}linRep}({\bm{a}})$ fully captures \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:valid-selector-assign-connected} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:valid-selector} with few variables.
\par\begin{lemma}Let ${\bm{a}}$ be a vector indexed by $T\in\mathcal{T}_{S}$, $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[d(T)\right]}{[d(T)]}{[d(T)]}{[d(T)]}}$, and $U\subseteq S\cap V(G)$. Then,
\begin{enumerate}\enumerate@item@$\bm{assign\text{-}linRep}({\bm{a}})$ has $2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}}$ variables,
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@assuming that for all $T\in\mathcal{T}_{S}$, $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[d(T)\right]}{[d(T)]}{[d(T)]}{[d(T)]}}$, $U\subseteq S\cap V(G)$ we have $${\bm{a}}_{T,i,U}=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if }\exists C\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S\right)}{\compOp({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S)}{\compOp({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S)}{\compOp({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S)}},j\in{\mathchoice{\left[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\right]}{[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}]}}\colon{\mathchoice{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}\left(T,i,j\right)}{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}(T,i,j)}{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}(T,i,j)}{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}(T,i,j)}}=U\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}$$Then, $\bm{assign\text{-}linRep}({\bm{a}})$ has a feasible assignment if and only if $\Gamma$ satisfies \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:valid-selector-assign-connected} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:valid-selector}.\end{enumerate}\end{lemma}
\@proof Since $S$ is nice, we have $\mathcal{T}_{S}\cap\mathcal{T}^{\star}=\emptyset$.
Therefore, by \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:terminal-sets-few-requirements}, we need at most ${\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}2^{{\mathchoice{\left|S\cap V(G)\right|}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}}}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}2^{{\mathchoice{\left|S\cap V(G)\right|}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}2^{{\mathchoice{\left|S\cap V(G)\right|}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}2^{{\mathchoice{\left|S\cap V(G)\right|}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}{{|S\cap V(G)|}}}})}}=2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}}$ variables.
\par Let $T\in\mathcal{T}_{S}$ and $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[d(T)\right]}{[d(T)]}{[d(T)]}{[d(T)]}}$.
For all $Y\subseteq S\cap V(G)$, if there is a $C\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S\right)}{\compOp({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S)}{\compOp({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S)}{\compOp({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S)}}$ and $j\in{\mathchoice{\left[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\right]}{[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}]}}$ with ${\mathchoice{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}\left(T,i,j\right)}{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}(T,i,j)}{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}(T,i,j)}{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}(T,i,j)}}=Y$, we assume that ${\bm{a}}_{T,i,Y}=1$ and otherwise that ${\bm{a}}_{T,i,Y}=0$.
Additionally, denote with $H$ the hypergraph considered in \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:valid-selector-assign-connected} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:valid-selector}
\par First, assume that $\bm{assign\text{-}linRep}({\bm{a}})$ is feasible and let ${\bm{b}}$ be chosen accordingly.
Let $T\cap S\subseteq Z\subseteq S\cap V(G)$ be the unique set with ${\bm{b}}_{T,i,Z}=1$.
By \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eq:assign-lin-rep-well-formed}, we know that for all $Y\subseteq S\cap V(G)$ with ${\bm{a}}_{T,i,Y}=1$, we have $Y\subseteq Z$.
So, the hypergraph $J\coloneqq(Z,\{F\subseteq S\cap V(G)\mid{\bm{a}}_{T,i,F}=1\})$ is well-defined.
Notice that $E(J)=E(H)$.
By \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eq:assign-lin-rep-cut}, $J$ is connected.
We now show $Z=V(H)$, which shows that $J=H$ and that $H$ is connected.
If ${\mathchoice{\left|Z\right|}{{|Z|}}{{|Z|}}{{|Z|}}}\geq 2$, we have that $Z\subseteq\bigcup_{F\in E(J)}F=\bigcup_{F\in E(H)}F\subseteq V(H)$.
Now, assume ${\mathchoice{\left|Z\right|}{{|Z|}}{{|Z|}}{{|Z|}}}\leq 1$.
Since $S$ is nice, there is a $C\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S\right)}{\compOp({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S)}{\compOp({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S)}{\compOp({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S)}}$ and $j\in{\mathchoice{\left[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\right]}{[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}]}}$ with ${\mathchoice{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}\left(T,i,j\right)}{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}(T,i,j)}{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}(T,i,j)}{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}(T,i,j)}}\neq\emptyset$.
Let $U\coloneqq{\mathchoice{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}\left(T,i,j\right)}{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}(T,i,j)}{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}(T,i,j)}{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}(T,i,j)}}\in E(J)$.
Thus, $U\subseteq Z$, combined with ${\mathchoice{\left|Z\right|}{{|Z|}}{{|Z|}}{{|Z|}}}\leq 1$, we have $U=Z$.
As $U\in E(H)$, we have $U=Z\subseteq V(H)$.
Now, let $v\in V(H)$.
If $v\in T\cap S$, we have by definition of $Z$ that $v\in Z$.
Otherwise, if $\{v\}\neq Z$, by \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eq:assign-lin-rep-cut}, there is a $Y\subseteq S\cap V(G)$ with $v\in Y$ and ${\bm{a}}_{T,i,Y}=1$.
So, there is a $C\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S\right)}{\compOp({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S)}{\compOp({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S)}{\compOp({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S)}}$ and a $j\in{\mathchoice{\left[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\right]}{[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}]}}$ with ${\mathchoice{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}\left(T,i,j\right)}{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}(T,i,j)}{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}(T,i,j)}{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}(T,i,j)}}=Y$ and $Y\in E(J)$.
Thus, $v\in\bigcup_{Y\in E(J)}Y\subseteq Z$.
If $\{v\}=Z$, by \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eq:assign-lin-rep-at-least-one-edge}, we have ${\bm{a}}_{T,i,Z}\geq{\bm{b}}_{T,i,Z}=1$ and by the same argument as above $v\in\bigcup_{Y\in E(J)}Y\subseteq Z$ as well.
\par Second, assume that $\Gamma$ satisfies \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:valid-selector-assign-connected} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:valid-selector}.
Denote with $Z\coloneqq(T\cap S)\cup\allowbreak\bigcup_{Y\subseteq S\cap V(G)\colon{\bm{a}}_{T,i,Y}=1}Y$.
Set ${\bm{b}}_{T,i,Z}\coloneqq 1$ and for all $Z\neq W\subseteq S\cap V(G)$ we set ${\bm{b}}_{T,i,W}\coloneqq 0$.
We notice immediately that \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eq:assign-lin-rep-exact} is satisfied.
For all $T\cap S\subseteq U\subseteq S\cap V(G)$ with $U\neq Z$, \lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{eq:assign-lin-rep-at-least-one-edge} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{eq:assign-lin-rep-cut} is satisfied.
Since $S$ is nice fracture modulator, there is a $C\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S\right)}{\compOp({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S)}{\compOp({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S)}{\compOp({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S)}}$ with $V(C)\cap T\neq\emptyset$.
Consider $(\sigma,\mathcal{F},\pi)$ that gives rise to $\Gamma(C)$ on $C$.
Let $j\in\sigma^{-1}(V(C)\cap T)$, then ${\mathchoice{\assignOp_{\Gamma}\left(T,i,j\right)}{\assignOp_{\Gamma}(T,i,j)}{\assignOp_{\Gamma}(T,i,j)}{\assignOp_{\Gamma}(T,i,j)}}\neq\emptyset$.
So, we have $\sum_{\emptyset\neq Y\subseteq Z}{\bm{a}}_{T,i,Y}\geq{\bm{a}}_{T,i,{\mathchoice{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}\left(T,i,j\right)}{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}(T,i,j)}{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}(T,i,j)}{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}(T,i,j)}}}=1={\bm{b}}_{T,i,Z}$, meaning that \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eq:assign-lin-rep-at-least-one-edge} is satisfied for $U=Z$.
Consider any $\emptyset\subset X\subset U$.
As $Z=V(H)$, there is a hyperedge $F\in E(H)$ with $X\cap F\neq\emptyset$ and $X\setminus F\neq\emptyset$.
Thus, $\sum_{Y\subseteq U\colon Y\cap X\neq\emptyset,Y\setminus X\neq\emptyset}{\bm{a}}_{T,i,Y}\geq{\bm{a}}_{T,i,F}=1\geq{\bm{b}}_{T,i,U}$ and \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eq:assign-lin-rep-cut} is satisfied for $X=Z$ as well.
Finally, consider any $Y\subseteq S\cap V(G)$.
If ${\bm{a}}_{T,i,Y}=0$, \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eq:assign-lin-rep-well-formed} is trivially satisfied.
Otherwise, ${\bm{a}}_{T,i,Y}=1$ and $Y\subseteq Z$, which means that $\sum_{Y\subseteq U\subseteq S\cap V(G)}{\bm{b}}_{T,i,U}\geq{\bm{b}}_{T,i,Z}=1\geq{\bm{a}}_{T,i,Y}$.
Thus, \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eq:assign-lin-rep-well-formed} is satisfied as well.
\qed\end@proof
\par Now, we combine all the pieces to obtain an ILP that fully captures whether $\Gamma$ is valid.
For this, denote with $\mathfrak{C}$ the set of non-empty equivalence classes of ${\mathchoice{\compOp\left({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S\right)}{\compOp({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S)}{\compOp({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S)}{\compOp({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S)}}$ and extend $\signatureOp$ to $\mathfrak{C}$ to be defined as the signature of any component in the corresponding equivalence class.
For all $\mathcal{X}\in\mathfrak{C}$, we denote with $n_{\mathcal{X}}$ the number of components in $\mathcal{X}$ (i.e., ${\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{X}\right|}{{|\mathcal{X}|}}{{|\mathcal{X}|}}{{|\mathcal{X}|}}}$).
\par\begin{definition}Let $N\coloneqq\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathfrak{C}}n_{\mathcal{X}}$. We denote with $\bm{selector\text{-}linRep}$ the ILP
\lx@equationgroup@subnumbering@begin
\@@amsalign
\lx@equationgroup@subnumbering@end
\end{definition}
\par In this definition, we basically represent a configuration selector.
For all $\mathcal{X}\in\mathfrak{C}$ and $\gamma\in{\mathchoice{\signatureOp\left(\mathcal{X}\right)}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}}$, the variable ${\bm{d}}_{\mathcal{X},\gamma}$ denotes how many components in the equivalence class $\mathcal{X}$ take the configuration $\gamma$ in our solution.
We then use auxiliary variables ${\bm{s}}$ and ${\bm{a}}$ together with the previously analyzed ILPs $\bm{\rho\text{-}linRep}({\bm{s}})$ and $\bm{assign\text{-}linRep}({\bm{a}})$ to ensure that this solution corresponds to a valid configuration selector.
\par\begin{lemma}The ILP $\bm{selector\text{-}linRep}$ has
\begin{enumerate}\enumerate@item@$2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}}$ many variables,
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@a feasible assignment if and only if there is a valid configuration selector.\end{enumerate}\end{lemma}
\@proof The ILP $\bm{selector\text{-}linRep}$ itself uses $2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}}+{\mathchoice{\OOp\left(\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathfrak{C}}{\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\signatureOp\left(\mathcal{X}\right)}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\signatureOp\left(\mathcal{X}\right)}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\signatureOp\left(\mathcal{X}\right)}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\signatureOp\left(\mathcal{X}\right)}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}}|}}}\right)}{\OOp(\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathfrak{C}}{\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\signatureOp\left(\mathcal{X}\right)}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\signatureOp\left(\mathcal{X}\right)}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\signatureOp\left(\mathcal{X}\right)}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\signatureOp\left(\mathcal{X}\right)}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}}|}}})}{\OOp(\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathfrak{C}}{\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\signatureOp\left(\mathcal{X}\right)}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\signatureOp\left(\mathcal{X}\right)}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\signatureOp\left(\mathcal{X}\right)}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\signatureOp\left(\mathcal{X}\right)}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}}|}}})}{\OOp(\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathfrak{C}}{\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\signatureOp\left(\mathcal{X}\right)}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\signatureOp\left(\mathcal{X}\right)}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\signatureOp\left(\mathcal{X}\right)}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\signatureOp\left(\mathcal{X}\right)}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}}|}}})}}$ variables and the sub-ILPs use $2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{2}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{2})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{2})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{2})}}$ additional variables.
Using \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:frac-num-bounded-non-empty-equiv-classes} and \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:size-signature}, we observe that $\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathfrak{C}}{\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\signatureOp\left(\mathcal{X}\right)}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\signatureOp\left(\mathcal{X}\right)}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\signatureOp\left(\mathcal{X}\right)}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\signatureOp\left(\mathcal{X}\right)}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}}|}}}=2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}}$; so, in total the ILP $\bm{selector\text{-}linRep}$ uses $2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}^{4})}}$ variables.
\par Now consider any feasible assignment to the variables ${\bm{d}},{\bm{s}}$ and ${\bm{a}}$ and consider any configuration selector $\Gamma$ that assigns for all $\mathcal{X}$ and $\gamma\in{\mathchoice{\signatureOp\left(\mathcal{X}\right)}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}}$, exactly ${\bm{d}}_{\mathcal{X},\gamma}$ many components in this equivalence class the configuration $\gamma$.
This is possible by \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eq:selector-lin-rep-exact}.
By \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:rho-lin-rep-sensible}, there is a function $\rho\colon\mathcal{T}^{\star}\times\N\to 2^{2^{S\cap V(G)}}$ that satisfies \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:valid-selector-rho-connected} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:valid-selector} and for all $U\subseteq S\cap V(G)$, it holds that $r_{U}\leq{\bm{s}}_{U}$.
Notice that \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eq:selector-lin-rep-dem-sup} ensures that \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:valid-selector-enough-supply} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:valid-selector} is satisfied as well.
\par To conclude that $\Gamma$ is valid, we aim to apply \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:assign-lin-rep-sensible}.
Consider any $T\in\mathcal{T}_{S},i\in{\mathchoice{\left[d(T)\right]}{[d(T)]}{[d(T)]}{[d(T)]}}$, and $U\subseteq S\cap V(G)$.
Assume there is a $C\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S\right)}{\compOp({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S)}{\compOp({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S)}{\compOp({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S)}}$ and $j\in{\mathchoice{\left[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\right]}{[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}]}}$ such that ${\mathchoice{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}\left(T,i,j\right)}{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}(T,i,j)}{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}(T,i,j)}{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}(T,i,j)}}=U$ and denote with $\mathcal{X}$ the equivalence class of $C$.
By \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eq:selector-lin-rep-a-ub}, we have $1\leq{\bm{d}}_{\mathcal{X},\Gamma(C)}\leq N{\bm{a}}_{T,i,U}$; so, ${\bm{a}}_{T,i,U}=1$.
Now, assume that ${\bm{a}}_{T,i,U}=1$.
By \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eq:selector-lin-rep-a-lb} and since the maximum of a set of numbers is bounded below by their mean, there is a $\mathcal{X}\in\mathfrak{C}$ and $\gamma\in{\mathchoice{\signatureOp\left(\mathcal{X}\right)}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}}$ such that there is a $j\in{\mathchoice{\left[S\right]}{[S]}{[S]}{[S]}}$ with ${\mathchoice{\assignOp_{\gamma}\left(T,i,j\right)}{\assignOp_{\gamma}(T,i,j)}{\assignOp_{\gamma}(T,i,j)}{\assignOp_{\gamma}(T,i,j)}}=U$ and ${\bm{d}}_{\mathcal{X},\gamma}\geq\frac{{\bm{a}}_{T,i,U}}{\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathfrak{C}}{\mathchoice{\left|\{\gamma\in{\mathchoice{\signatureOp\left(\mathcal{X}\right)}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}}\mid\exists j\in{\mathchoice{\left[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\right]}{[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}]}}\colon{\mathchoice{\assignOp_{\gamma}\left(T,i,j\right)}{\assignOp_{\gamma}(T,i,j)}{\assignOp_{\gamma}(T,i,j)}{\assignOp_{\gamma}(T,i,j)}}=U\}\right|}{{|\{\gamma\in{\mathchoice{\signatureOp\left(\mathcal{X}\right)}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}}\mid\exists j\in{\mathchoice{\left[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\right]}{[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}]}}\colon{\mathchoice{\assignOp_{\gamma}\left(T,i,j\right)}{\assignOp_{\gamma}(T,i,j)}{\assignOp_{\gamma}(T,i,j)}{\assignOp_{\gamma}(T,i,j)}}=U\}|}}{{|\{\gamma\in{\mathchoice{\signatureOp\left(\mathcal{X}\right)}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}}\mid\exists j\in{\mathchoice{\left[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\right]}{[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}]}}\colon{\mathchoice{\assignOp_{\gamma}\left(T,i,j\right)}{\assignOp_{\gamma}(T,i,j)}{\assignOp_{\gamma}(T,i,j)}{\assignOp_{\gamma}(T,i,j)}}=U\}|}}{{|\{\gamma\in{\mathchoice{\signatureOp\left(\mathcal{X}\right)}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}}\mid\exists j\in{\mathchoice{\left[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\right]}{[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}]}}\colon{\mathchoice{\assignOp_{\gamma}\left(T,i,j\right)}{\assignOp_{\gamma}(T,i,j)}{\assignOp_{\gamma}(T,i,j)}{\assignOp_{\gamma}(T,i,j)}}=U\}|}}}}>0$.
So, ${\bm{d}}_{\mathcal{X},\gamma}\geq 1$ and there is a $C\in\mathcal{X}$ with $\Gamma(C)=\gamma$.
Thus, ${\mathchoice{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}\left(T,i,j\right)}{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}(T,i,j)}{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}(T,i,j)}{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}(T,i,j)}}=U$.
Now we know from \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:assign-lin-rep-sensible} that the configuration selector $\Gamma$ satisfies \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:valid-selector-assign-connected} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:valid-selector} and so $\Gamma$ is valid.
\par Let $\Gamma$ be a valid configuration selector.
We aim to provide a feasible solution to $\bm{selector\text{-}linRep}$.
First let $\mathcal{X}\in\mathfrak{C}$ and $\gamma\in{\mathchoice{\signatureOp\left(\mathcal{X}\right)}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}}$.
We set ${\bm{d}}_{\mathcal{X},\gamma}\coloneqq{\mathchoice{\left|\{C\in\mathcal{X}\mid\Gamma(C)=\gamma\}\right|}{{|\{C\in\mathcal{X}\mid\Gamma(C)=\gamma\}|}}{{|\{C\in\mathcal{X}\mid\Gamma(C)=\gamma\}|}}{{|\{C\in\mathcal{X}\mid\Gamma(C)=\gamma\}|}}}$ as the number of components in $\mathcal{X}$ that get assigned the configuration $\gamma$, satisfying \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eq:selector-lin-rep-exact}.
Let $\rho$ be a function witnessing validity of $\Gamma$.
We set for all $U\subseteq S\cap V(G)$, ${\bm{s}}_{U}=r_{U}$.
Since $\Gamma$ is valid, \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eq:selector-lin-rep-dem-sup} is satisfied.
By \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:rho-lin-rep-sensible}, the sub-ILP $\bm{\rho\text{-}linRep}({\bm{s}})$ is feasible.
\par Finally, consider any $T\in\mathcal{T}_{S}$, $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[d(T)\right]}{[d(T)]}{[d(T)]}{[d(T)]}}$, and $U\subseteq S\cap V(G)$.
If there is a $C\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S\right)}{\compOp({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S)}{\compOp({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S)}{\compOp({G}^{\mathcal{T}}-S)}}$ and $j\in{\mathchoice{\left[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\right]}{[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}]}}$ with ${\mathchoice{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}\left(T,i,j\right)}{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}(T,i,j)}{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}(T,i,j)}{\assignOp_{\Gamma(C)}(T,i,j)}}=U$, we set ${\bm{a}}_{T,i,U}\coloneqq 1$.
Otherwise, we set ${\bm{a}}_{T,i,U}\coloneqq 0$.
By \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:assign-lin-rep-sensible}, the sub-ILP $\bm{assign\text{-}linRep}({\bm{a}})$ is feasible.
If ${\bm{a}}_{T,i,U}=0$, \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eq:selector-lin-rep-a-lb} is trivially satisfied and \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eq:selector-lin-rep-a-ub} is satisfied by choice of ${\bm{a}}$.
If ${\bm{a}}_{T,i,U}=1$, \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eq:selector-lin-rep-a-lb} is satisfied by choice of ${\bm{a}}$.
Since $\sum_{\mathcal{X}\in\mathfrak{C},\gamma\in{\mathchoice{\signatureOp\left(\mathcal{X}\right)}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}{\signatureOp(\mathcal{X})}}}d_{\mathcal{X},\gamma}=N{\bm{a}}_{T,i,U}$, \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{eq:selector-lin-rep-a-ub} is satisfied as well.
\qed\end@proof
\par We know that deciding whether a feasible assignment for an ILP exists, is $\fpt$ by the number of variables~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Kannan87}{}{}]}.
Additionally, we know that deciding whether a fracture modulator of size $k$ exists, and possibly finding it, is $\fpt$ by $k$.
So, we can construct and evaluate $\bm{selector\text{-}linRep}$ reasonably fast, yielding the following theorem.
\par\begin{theorem}Let $\mathscr{P}=(G,\mathcal{T},d)$ be an instance of $\gstp$.
Denote the fracture number of ${G}^{\mathcal{T}}$ with $k$.
We can decide whether $\mathscr{P}$ is a positive instance in running time $2^{2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left(k^{4}\right)}{\OOp(k^{4})}{\OOp(k^{4})}{\OOp(k^{4})}}}{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}}+{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|\mathscr{P}\right|}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|\mathscr{P}\right|}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|\mathscr{P}\right|}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|\mathscr{P}\right|}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}})}}$.
\end{theorem}
\@proof First, we apply \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{rr:degree-negative-instance} in running time ${\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|\mathscr{P}\right|}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|\mathscr{P}\right|}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|\mathscr{P}\right|}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|\mathscr{P}\right|}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}})}}$.
After applying this reduction rule, for all $v\in V(G)$ the number of incident augmented edges is bounded by the number of non-augmented edges, yielding ${\mathchoice{\left|E({G}^{\mathcal{T}})\right|}{{|E({G}^{\mathcal{T}})|}}{{|E({G}^{\mathcal{T}})|}}{{|E({G}^{\mathcal{T}})|}}}\leq 2{\mathchoice{\left|E(G)\right|}{{|E(G)|}}{{|E(G)|}}{{|E(G)|}}}$.
Now, we find a fracture modulator $X$ of size $k$ in ${G}^{\mathcal{T}}$.
By \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:fracture-modulator-algo}, this can be done in time ${\mathchoice{\OOp\left((2k)^{k}{\mathchoice{\left|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}\right|}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}}\right)}{\OOp((2k)^{k}{\mathchoice{\left|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}\right|}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}})}{\OOp((2k)^{k}{\mathchoice{\left|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}\right|}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}})}{\OOp((2k)^{k}{\mathchoice{\left|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}\right|}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}})}}={\mathchoice{\OOp\left((2k)^{k}{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}}\right)}{\OOp((2k)^{k}{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}})}{\OOp((2k)^{k}{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}})}{\OOp((2k)^{k}{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}})}}$.
\par By \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:nice-fracture-modulator-exists}, we can find an equivalent instance $\mathscr{P}^{\prime}=(G^{\prime},\mathcal{T},d)$ and a nice fracture modulator $S$ of ${G^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{T}}$ with ${\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}={\mathchoice{\OOp\left(k\right)}{\OOp(k)}{\OOp(k)}{\OOp(k)}}$ and ${\mathchoice{\left|V({G^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{T}})\right|}{{|V({G^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{T}})|}}{{|V({G^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{T}})|}}{{|V({G^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{T}})|}}}={\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|V({G}^{\mathcal{T}})\right|}{{|V({G}^{\mathcal{T}})|}}{{|V({G}^{\mathcal{T}})|}}{{|V({G}^{\mathcal{T}})|}}}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|V({G}^{\mathcal{T}})\right|}{{|V({G}^{\mathcal{T}})|}}{{|V({G}^{\mathcal{T}})|}}{{|V({G}^{\mathcal{T}})|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|V({G}^{\mathcal{T}})\right|}{{|V({G}^{\mathcal{T}})|}}{{|V({G}^{\mathcal{T}})|}}{{|V({G}^{\mathcal{T}})|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|V({G}^{\mathcal{T}})\right|}{{|V({G}^{\mathcal{T}})|}}{{|V({G}^{\mathcal{T}})|}}{{|V({G}^{\mathcal{T}})|}}})}}$ in linear time.
Now, we again apply \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{rr:degree-negative-instance} to $\mathscr{P}^{\prime}$ in running time ${\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}})}}$.
If the reduction rule supplied a negative instance, we abort here and output that $\mathscr{P}$ is a negative instance.
Otherwise, for each of the components in ${G^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{T}}-S$ we compute the signature in time $2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left(k^{4}\log k\right)}{\OOp(k^{4}\log k)}{\OOp(k^{4}\log k)}{\OOp(k^{4}\log k)}}$, according to \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:compute-signature}.
Overall, this takes at most $2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left(k^{4}\log k\right)}{\OOp(k^{4}\log k)}{\OOp(k^{4}\log k)}{\OOp(k^{4}\log k)}}{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}$ time.
Next, we compute the size of the non-empty equivalence classes of the components.
This can be achieved in time ${\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}}\log{\mathchoice{\left|\!\allViable\!\right|}{{|\!\allViable\!|}}{{|\!\allViable\!|}}{{|\!\allViable\!|}}}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}}\log{\mathchoice{\left|\!\allViable\!\right|}{{|\!\allViable\!|}}{{|\!\allViable\!|}}{{|\!\allViable\!|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}}\log{\mathchoice{\left|\!\allViable\!\right|}{{|\!\allViable\!|}}{{|\!\allViable\!|}}{{|\!\allViable\!|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}}\log{\mathchoice{\left|\!\allViable\!\right|}{{|\!\allViable\!|}}{{|\!\allViable\!|}}{{|\!\allViable\!|}}})}}={\mathchoice{\OOp\left(k^{4}{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}}\right)}{\OOp(k^{4}{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}})}{\OOp(k^{4}{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}})}{\OOp(k^{4}{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}})}}$.
\par Now, it is straightforward to construct $\bm{selector\text{-}linRep}$ in time $2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left(k^{4}\right)}{\OOp(k^{4})}{\OOp(k^{4})}{\OOp(k^{4})}}$.
As all scalars in the ILP are bounded by ${\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}})}}$, we can check for feasibility in time $2^{2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left(k^{4}\right)}{\OOp(k^{4})}{\OOp(k^{4})}{\OOp(k^{4})}}}\log{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}}$~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Kannan87}{}{}]}.
We output that $\mathscr{P}$ is a positive instance if and only if $\bm{selector\text{-}linRep}$ is feasible.
By \lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{stmt:selector-lin-rep-sensible} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{stmt:valid-selector-if-positive}, this output is correct.
\qed\end@proof
\par\begin{corollary}$\gstp$ is $\fpt$ by the fracture number of the augmented graph.
\end{corollary}
\par
\par\@@numbered@section{section}{toc}{\texorpdfstring{$\gstp$}{GSTP} is \texorpdfstring{$\fpt$}{FPT} by the Augmented/Slim Tree-Cut Width}
This Chapter is devoted to showing, that $\gstp$ is fixed-parameter tractable by both the tree-cut width of the augmented graph and the slim tree-cut width of the original graph.
We later use the former fact to show, that $\stp$ itself is fixed-parameter tractable by the tree-cut width of the input graph.
This result does not follow immediately as augmentation—even of a single terminal set—might increase the tree-cut width arbitrarily.
\par Let $\mathscr{P}=(G,\mathcal{T},d)$ be an instance of $\gstp$.
The central part of deciding whether $\mathscr{P}$ is positive, is a dynamic program.
This dynamic program works on a tree-cut decomposition for $G$ with some additional assumptions and heavily uses the fact that the number of bold children of any node in the tree-cut decomposition is bounded by a function of its width.
\par Ganian et~al.~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{GanianKS22}{}{}]} claimed that in a nice tree-cut decomposition the number of bold children of any node is bounded by $w+1$.
However, we provide counter examples to this, showing that in a nice tree-cut decomposition, the number of bold children is actually not bounded by a function its width.
Then, we show how to transform a nice tree-cut decomposition of an arbitrary graph into a \emph{friendly} tree-cut decomposition in $\fpt$-linear time.
In fact, the running time is even at most polynomial in the size of the graph.
\par\begin{definition}We call a tree-cut decomposition $(S,\mathcal{X})$ of width $w$ \emph{friendly}, if it is nice and for all $s\in S$, we have ${\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|X_{s}\right|}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}}\leq w+2$.
\end{definition}
\par Based on this, we give the definition of a \emph{simple} tree-cut decomposition.
Given a simple tree-cut decomposition for $G$, we provide a dynamic program deciding whether $\mathscr{P}$ is a positive instance.
\par\begin{definition}Let $(G,\mathcal{T},d)$ be an instance of $\gstp$.
Consider a tree-cut decomposition $\mathcal{D}\coloneqq(S,\mathcal{X})$ of $G$ and let $s\in V(S)$.
Denote with ${\mathchoice{\crossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}}\coloneqq\{T\in\mathcal{T}\mid T\cap Y_{s}\neq\emptyset\land T\setminus Y_{s}\neq\emptyset\}$ the set of terminal sets crossing the link between $s$ and its parent.
We call $s$ \emph{simple} if it is thin, ${\mathchoice{\left|Y_{s}\right|}{{|Y_{s}|}}{{|Y_{s}|}}{{|Y_{s}|}}}=1$, ${\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(s\right)}{\adhesionOp(s)}{\adhesionOp(s)}{\adhesionOp(s)}}=2$, and ${\mathchoice{\crossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}}=\emptyset$.
We call $\mathcal{D}$ \emph{simple}, if it is friendly and all thin nodes are simple.
\end{definition}
\par This Chapter is structured as follows.
First, we provide reduction rules that are necessary for the whole Chapter and in particular for reducing the instance from augmented tree-cut width and slim tree-cut width to a simple tree-cut decomposition.
Then, we provide a dynamic program to solve $\gstp$ given a simple tree-cut decomposition parameterized by its width.
Building on this result, we show how to construct a simple tree-cut decomposition of width $w$ from a tree-cut decomposition with slim width $w$.
Finally, we show how to use the fact that we can decide instances with a simple tree-cut decomposition in $\fpt$-time by the width of this decomposition to decide an instance given a tree-cut decomposition of the augmented graph.
As an important ingredient, we show how to obtain a friendly tree-cut decomposition from a nice tree-cut decomposition in polynomial time, without considerably increasing its width.
\par\par\@@numbered@section{subsection}{toc}{General Techniques for \texorpdfstring{$\gstp$}{GSTP} Tree-Cut Decompositions \label{sec:tcw-red-rules}}
In this Section, we present the techniques, which we need across this chapter.
We start with the general reduction rules.
Then, we show that we can obtain a simple tree-cut decomposition from a tree-cut decomposition without increasing the width by much, if the given decomposition is almost friendly and for each node the number of non-simple, thin children is small.
\par Let $\mathcal{D}=(S,\mathcal{X})$ be a tree-cut decomposition of width $w$ for $G$.
Let $Q\subseteq V(G)$ be given.
If there is no $T\in\mathcal{T}$ with $T\cap Q\neq\emptyset$ and $T\setminus Q\neq\emptyset$ (i.e., $T$ crosses $Q$), we denote with $\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\coloneqq\{T\in\mathcal{T}\mid T\subseteq Q\}$ the terminals contained in $Q$ and with $\mathscr{P}[Q]$ the instance $(G[Q],\mathcal{T}^{\prime},d|_{\mathcal{T}^{\prime}})$ restricted to $Q$.
For every $C\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left(G\right)}{\compOp(G)}{\compOp(G)}{\compOp(G)}}$, we have that $\mathscr{P}$ is positive if and only if $\mathscr{P}[V(C)]$ and $\mathscr{P}[V(G)\setminus V(C)]$ are both positive, providing a way to consider connected components of an instance separately.
This immediately yields our first reduction rule.
\par\begin{reductionrule}If there is a $T\in\mathcal{T}$, such that there is no $C\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left(G\right)}{\compOp(G)}{\compOp(G)}{\compOp(G)}}$, with $T\subseteq V(C)$, output a trivial negative instance.
Otherwise, output that the instance is positive if and only if all $\{\mathscr{P}[V(C)]\}_{C\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left(G\right)}{\compOp(G)}{\compOp(G)}{\compOp(G)}}}$ are positive instances.
\end{reductionrule}
\par Denote with $r$ the root of $S$.
After applying \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{rr:connected-components}, all $s\in V(S)\setminus\{r\}$ with ${\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(s\right)}{\adhesionOp(s)}{\adhesionOp(s)}{\adhesionOp(s)}}=0$ are empty leaves, which we can remove from $\mathcal{D}$.
Thus, we assume from now on that ${\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(s\right)}{\adhesionOp(s)}{\adhesionOp(s)}{\adhesionOp(s)}}\geq 1$.
\par We now provide a reduction rule to limit the number of connections that need to be made across links.
In any positive instance the accumulated demands of all $T\in{\mathchoice{\crossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}}$ can not be very large as each such demand needs to be fulfilled by a tree crossing the link between $S_{s}$ and its parent, of which there can not be that many.
Denote this value by ${\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}\coloneqq\sum_{T\in{\mathchoice{\crossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}}}d(T)$.
If $\mathcal{D}$ would be a tree-cut decomposition for ${G}^{\mathcal{T}}$, denote with $\mathcal{U}$ the terminal sets inducing the augmented edges in ${\mathchoice{\cutEdgesOp_{{G}^{\mathcal{T}}}\left(Y_{s}\right)}{\cutEdgesOp_{{G}^{\mathcal{T}}}(Y_{s})}{\cutEdgesOp_{{G}^{\mathcal{T}}}(Y_{s})}{\cutEdgesOp_{{G}^{\mathcal{T}}}(Y_{s})}}$.
Initially, one might even think that ${\mathchoice{\crossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}}=\mathcal{U}$ holds, but this is not necessarily the case.
We note that ${\mathchoice{\crossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}}\subseteq\mathcal{U}$.
\par\begin{reductionrule}If there is a node $s\in V(S)$ with ${\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}>{\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(s\right)}{\adhesionOp(s)}{\adhesionOp(s)}{\adhesionOp(s)}}$, we output a trivial negative instance.
\end{reductionrule}
\@proof Consider any positive instance.
We show that for all $s\in V(S)$ we have ${\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}\leq{\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(s\right)}{\adhesionOp(s)}{\adhesionOp(s)}{\adhesionOp(s)}}$.
Let $(\mathcal{F},\pi)$ be a solution and set $\{F_{1},F_{2},\dots,F_{{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}}\}\coloneqq\pi^{-1}({\mathchoice{\crossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}})$.
As all terminal sets in ${\mathchoice{\crossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}}$ contain a terminal in $Y_{s}$ and $V(G)\setminus Y_{s}$, for each $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}\right]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}}$ there is a distinct $e_{i}\in E(F_{i})\cap{\mathchoice{\cutEdgesOp_{G}\left(Y_{s}\right)}{\cutEdgesOp_{G}(Y_{s})}{\cutEdgesOp_{G}(Y_{s})}{\cutEdgesOp_{G}(Y_{s})}}$.
As ${\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\cutEdgesOp_{G}\left(Y_{s}\right)}{\cutEdgesOp_{G}(Y_{s})}{\cutEdgesOp_{G}(Y_{s})}{\cutEdgesOp_{G}(Y_{s})}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\cutEdgesOp_{G}\left(Y_{s}\right)}{\cutEdgesOp_{G}(Y_{s})}{\cutEdgesOp_{G}(Y_{s})}{\cutEdgesOp_{G}(Y_{s})}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\cutEdgesOp_{G}\left(Y_{s}\right)}{\cutEdgesOp_{G}(Y_{s})}{\cutEdgesOp_{G}(Y_{s})}{\cutEdgesOp_{G}(Y_{s})}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\cutEdgesOp_{G}\left(Y_{s}\right)}{\cutEdgesOp_{G}(Y_{s})}{\cutEdgesOp_{G}(Y_{s})}{\cutEdgesOp_{G}(Y_{s})}}|}}}={\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(s\right)}{\adhesionOp(s)}{\adhesionOp(s)}{\adhesionOp(s)}}$, we have that ${\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}\leq{\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(s\right)}{\adhesionOp(s)}{\adhesionOp(s)}{\adhesionOp(s)}}$.
\qed\end@proof
\par Now, we consider thin nodes $s\in V(S)\setminus\{r\}$, with ${\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(s\right)}{\adhesionOp(s)}{\adhesionOp(s)}{\adhesionOp(s)}}=1$.
\par\begin{reductionrule}Assume \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{rr:cross-link-demand-large} has been applied exhaustively.
Let $s\in V(S)$ with ${\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(s\right)}{\adhesionOp(s)}{\adhesionOp(s)}{\adhesionOp(s)}}=1$ and consider $\{uv\}\coloneqq{\mathchoice{\cutEdgesOp\left(Y_{s}\right)}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{s})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{s})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{s})}}$ with $u\in Y_{s}$ and $v\notin Y_{s}$.
Then, remove $uv$ from $G$ and if there is a $T\in{\mathchoice{\crossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}}$, increase the demand of $(T\cap Y_{s})\cup\{u\}$ and $(T\setminus Y_{s})\cup\{v\}$ by 1 while removing $T$ from $\mathcal{T}$ (if necessary, add $(T\cap Y_{s})\cup\{u\}$ and $(T\setminus Y_{s})\cup\{v\}$ to $\mathcal{T}$).
\end{reductionrule}
\@proof As \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{rr:cross-link-demand-large} is applied, we have ${\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}\in\{0,1\}$.
First, assume that ${\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}=0$.
Then, for all $T\in\mathcal{T}$, we either have $T\subseteq Y_{s}$ or $T\subseteq V(G)\setminus Y_{s}$.
As restricting connected subgraphs to either $Y_{s}$ or $V(G)\setminus Y_{s}$ keeps them connected, the bridge $e$ in $G$ is not needed to connect terminal sets.
\par Now, assume ${\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}=1$ and let $\{T\}\coloneqq{\mathchoice{\crossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}}$.
Let $(\mathcal{F},\pi)$ be a solution to the original instance.
Consider $\{F\}\coloneqq\pi^{-1}(T)$.
Then, $uv\in E(F)$.
As $uv$ is a bridge in $G$, $F-uv$ has two connected components.
Denote the component contained in $Y_{s}$ with $P$ and the other with $Q$.
Then, $(T\cap Y_{s})\cup\{u\}\subseteq V(P)$ and $(T\setminus Y_{s})\cup\{v\}\subseteq V(Q)$.
So, in the reduced instance we assign all of $\mathcal{F}\setminus\{F\}$ to the same terminal sets and we additionally assign $P$ to $(T\cap Y_{s})\cup\{u\}$ and $Q$ to $(T\setminus Y_{s})\cup\{v\}$, solving the reduced instance.
\par Let $(\mathcal{F},\pi)$ now be a solution to the reduced instance and let $P\in\pi^{-1}((T\cap Y_{s})\cup\{u\})$ and $Q\in\pi^{-1}((T\setminus Y_{s})\cup\{v\})$ and set $F\coloneqq(P\cup Q)+uv$.
Note that $F$ is connected, edge-disjoint from all $\mathcal{F}\setminus\{P,Q\}$ and $V(F)\supseteq(T\cap Y_{s})\cup(T\setminus Y_{s})=T$.
To get a solution for the original instance, we assign all of $\mathcal{F}\setminus\{P,Q\}$ to the same terminal sets and $F$ to $T$, solving the instance.
\qed\end@proof
After applying \lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{rr:connected-components}, \lx@cref{refnum}{rr:cross-link-demand-large} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{rr:adh-1} exhaustively, we remove all empty leaves from $S$, which achieves that for all thin nodes $s\in V(S)\setminus\{r\}$ we have ${\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(s\right)}{\adhesionOp(s)}{\adhesionOp(s)}{\adhesionOp(s)}}=2$.
This already hints at how we solve GSTP parameterized by slim tree-cut width.
After applying these reduction rules, we show that in a nice tree-cut decomposition the number of children is bounded by a function of its slim width.
As a friendly tree-cut decomposition without thin nodes is simple, we just treat all children like bold children, yielding an $\fpt$ algorithm.
\par It is known that EDP—and therefore GSTP—is $\wonehard$ parameterized by tree-cut width~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{GanianOR20}{}{}]}.
We show, that GSTP can be solved in $\fpt$-time parameterized by the tree-cut width of a simple tree-cut decomposition.
Additionally, we show that we can transform any tree-cut decomposition into a friendly tree-cut decomposition in polynomial time.
Assume there exist general reduction rules, that make all thin nodes simple and only increases the tree-cut width by a computable function.
Then, we could transform the tree-cut decomposition to be friendly and without thin links that are not simple in polynomial time.
Thus, $\mathrm{GSTP}$ would be $\fpt$ by the tree-cut width, which is not possible unless $\fpt=\wone$.
Therefore, the existence of such a reduction rule is unlikely.
\par Finally, we show that if $\mathcal{D}$ is close to being simple, we can actually make $\mathcal{D}$ simple without increasing its width by much.
To formalize this idea, let $s\in V(S)$ and denote with $N_{s}\subseteq{\mathchoice{\thinChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\thinChildrenOp(s)}{\thinChildrenOp(s)}{\thinChildrenOp(s)}}$ the set of thin and non-simple children of $s$.
If for all $s$ both ${\mathchoice{\left|N_{s}\right|}{{|N_{s}|}}{{|N_{s}|}}{{|N_{s}|}}}$ and ${\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|X_{s}\right|}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}}-w$ are small, we obtain a simple tree-cut decomposition of width that is not increased by much.
\par\begin{lemma}Given a nice tree-cut decomposition $\mathcal{D}$ of $G$.
We can compute in linear time an equivalent instance $(G^{\prime},\mathcal{T},d)$ and a simple tree-cut decomposition $\mathcal{C}$ of $G^{\prime}$.
Let $\Delta_{s}\coloneqq{\mathchoice{\left|N_{s}\right|}{{|N_{s}|}}{{|N_{s}|}}{{|N_{s}|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|X_{s}\right|}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}}-w-1$, then the tree-cut width of $\mathcal{C}$ is $$w^{*}\coloneqq w+4+\max(0,\max_{s\in V(S)}\Delta_{s}).$$\end{lemma}
\@proof For each $s\in V(S)$ add vertices $t_{s}$ and $t^{\prime}_{s}$ to $G$ and $X_{s}$, and for each $r\in N_{s}$ the edges $\{t_{s},t^{\prime}_{s}\}\times\{t_{r},t^{\prime}_{r}\}$.
Now add new isolated vertices to the root vertex of the tree-cut decomposition until its width is at least $w^{*}$ and remove all empty leaves.
These operation can be applied in linear time.
We call the obtained decomposition $\mathcal{C}\coloneqq(S^{\prime},\mathcal{X^{\prime}})$.
Observe that as $\mathcal{D}$ is nice, so is $\mathcal{C}$.
Since all added vertices and edges are disconnected from $G$, this instance is solvable if and only if the original instance is solvable.
So, consider any $s\in V(S)$.
We have $${\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{C}}\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{C}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{C}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{C}}(s)}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{C}}\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{C}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{C}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{C}}(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{C}}\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{C}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{C}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{C}}(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{C}}\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{C}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{C}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{C}}(s)}}|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|X^{\prime}_{s}\right|}{{|X^{\prime}_{s}|}}{{|X^{\prime}_{s}|}}{{|X^{\prime}_{s}|}}}={\mathchoice{\left|N_{s}\right|}{{|N_{s}|}}{{|N_{s}|}}{{|N_{s}|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}}\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}}\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}}\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}}\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}}|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|X_{s}\right|}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}}+2\leq\Delta_{s}+w+3\leq w^{*}-1.$$ Thus, $\mathcal{C}$ is friendly and we see that all remaining thin nodes are simple; so, $\mathcal{C}$ is simple.
