\par Finally, we compute the width of $\mathcal{C}$.
By construction, we have ${\mathchoice{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{C}}\left(s\right)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{C}}(s)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{C}}(s)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{C}}(s)}}\leq{\mathchoice{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}\left(s\right)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}}+4\leq w+4\leq w^{*}$.
Consider the torso at $s$ with respect to $\mathcal{C}$.
If $s$ is not the root, the size of the 3-center of the torso at $s$ in $\mathcal{C}$ is bounded by $1+{\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{C}}\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{C}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{C}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{C}}(s)}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{C}}\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{C}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{C}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{C}}(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{C}}\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{C}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{C}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{C}}(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{C}}\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{C}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{C}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{C}}(s)}}|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|X^{\prime}_{s}\right|}{{|X^{\prime}_{s}|}}{{|X^{\prime}_{s}|}}{{|X^{\prime}_{s}|}}}\leq w^{*}$.
The same holds for the root, before we start adding isolated vertices.
So, the width of $\mathcal{C}$ is exactly $w^{*}$.
\qed\end@proof
\par\par\@@numbered@section{subsection}{toc}{\texorpdfstring{$\gstp$}{GSTP} is \texorpdfstring{$\fpt$}{FPT} by the Width of a Simple Tree-Cut Decomposition \label{sec:tcw-simple}}
In this Section, we assume that the given tree-cut decomposition $(S,\mathcal{X})$ is simple.
How to use this to solve the general problem parameterized by slim width or tree-cut width of the augmented graph, is left for the following Sections.
A simple tree-cut decomposition heavily restricts how vertices in thin bags can be connected with the rest of the graph.
This can be exploited to construct a dynamic program for this problem.
\par From now on, we assume that \lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{rr:cross-link-demand-large} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{rr:sensible-terminal-sets} are applied.
For each $s\in V(S)$ choose a function $\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}\colon{\mathchoice{\left[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}\right]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}}\to{\mathchoice{\crossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}}$ such that for all $T\in{\mathchoice{\crossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}}$ we have ${\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}^{-1}(T)}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}^{-1}(T)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}^{-1}(T)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}^{-1}(T)}}|}}}=d(T)$.
This function gives us the ability to identify the different trees crossing the link between this node and its parent by a number in the set ${\mathchoice{\left[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}\right]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}}\subseteq{\mathchoice{\left[w\right]}{[w]}{[w]}{[w]}}$.
\par To define the dynamic program, we often refer to the \emph{boundary} ${\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}}$ of a node, which is defined as the graph edge-induced by ${\mathchoice{\cutEdgesOp\left(Y_{s}\right)}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{s})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{s})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{s})}}$ on $G$, and the graph $G_{s}\coloneqq G[Y_{s}]\cup{\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}}$ which is the graph edge-induced by all edges with at least one endpoint in $Y_{s}$.
Note that there can be edges in $G[V({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})]$ that are not included in ${\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}}$; in particular, ${\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}}$ is bipartite with the bipartitions $V({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})\cap Y_{s}$ and $V({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})\setminus Y_{s}$.
We have ${\mathchoice{\left|E({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})\right|}{{|E({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})|}}{{|E({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})|}}{{|E({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})|}}}\leq w$; so, ${\mathchoice{\left|V({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})\right|}{{|V({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})|}}{{|V({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})|}}{{|V({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})|}}}\leq 2w$.
The boundary is a small separator between the vertices $Y_{s}\setminus V({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})$ and $V(G)\setminus(Y_{s}\cup V({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}}))$.
We use this fact to define our dynamic program.
\par The data-table $D(s)$ at $s$, is a set of tuples $\tau=(\pastPartBaseOp_{\tau},\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau},\futurePartBaseOp_{\tau})$ with $\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau}\colon\pastPartBaseOp_{\tau}\to{\mathchoice{\left[w\right]}{[w]}{[w]}{[w]}}$ where $\pastPartBaseOp_{\tau}$ and $\futurePartBaseOp_{\tau}$ are partitions of a (not necessarily proper) subset of $E({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})$.
If $\bigcup\pastPartBaseOp_{\tau}$ and $\bigcup\futurePartBaseOp_{\tau}$ are disjoint, we call $\tau$ a syntactically valid tuple at $s$.
Note that we do not assign the partitions in $\futurePartBaseOp_{\tau}$ to indices, since at this point we do not care, whether they are eventually used for the same terminal set.
\par Intuitively, a tuple $\tau$ gives almost complete information about the part of the final solution that crosses ${\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}}$.
Consider a solution $(\mathcal{F},\pi)$ to the whole instance and denote with $\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{F}$ the trees containing an edge of $E({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})$.
Set $\mathcal{F}^{\downarrow}\coloneqq\{F\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\mid\pi(F)\cap Y_{s}\neq\emptyset\}$ and $\mathcal{F}^{\uparrow}\coloneqq\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{F}^{\downarrow}$ as the subgraphs crossing the link between $s$ and its parent which are assigned to terminal set starting below this link and starting above this link respectively.
This solution corresponds to a tuple $\tau\in D(s)$ with $\pastPartBaseOp_{\tau}\coloneqq\{\{E(K)\cap E({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})\mid K\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left(F[E(G_{s})]\right)}{\compOp(F[E(G_{s})])}{\compOp(F[E(G_{s})])}{\compOp(F[E(G_{s})])}}\}\}_{F\in\mathcal{F}^{\downarrow}}$ and $\futurePartBaseOp_{\tau}\coloneqq\{\{E(K)\cap E({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})\mid K\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left(F[E(G_{s}))]\right)}{\compOp(F[E(G_{s}))])}{\compOp(F[E(G_{s}))])}{\compOp(F[E(G_{s}))])}}\}\}_{F\in\mathcal{F}^{\uparrow}}$ where we consider the edges in $E({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})$ per connected component of the trees in $\mathcal{F}^{\prime}$ restricted to the edges with at least one endpoint in $Y_{s}$.
For each $F\in\mathcal{F}^{\downarrow}$ we choose a distinct $\lambda_{F}\in{\mathchoice{\left[w\right]}{[w]}{[w]}{[w]}}$ such that if $\pi(F)\in{\mathchoice{\crossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}}$, we have $\lambda_{F}\in{\mathchoice{\left[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}\right]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}}$ and ${\mathchoice{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}\left(\lambda_{F}\right)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}(\lambda_{F})}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}(\lambda_{F})}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}(\lambda_{F})}}=\pi(F)$ and if $\pi(F)\notin{\mathchoice{\crossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}}$, we have $\lambda_{F}\in{\mathchoice{\left[w\right]}{[w]}{[w]}{[w]}}\setminus{\mathchoice{\left[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}\right]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}}$.
Now, we set $\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau}$ to $\lambda_{F}$ for each set induced by $F$.
\par However, when computing $D(s)$ we do not know enough about the whole instance to ensure that the remaining instance can actually be solved by a solution that is valid up to this node.
So, we cannot require that exactly the tuples induced by complete solutions are the members of $D(s)$.
Rather, we only require that the tuples in $D(s)$ correspond to solutions that are valid up to the considered node that could be extended to complete solutions assuming that partitions of $\pastPartBaseOp_{\tau}$ assigned to the same tree are connected in the whole solution and that the connections provided by $\futurePartBaseOp_{\tau}$ are enough to fulfill the requirements not intersecting $Y_{s}$.
\par To formally define what this means, consider a syntactically valid tuple $\tau$ and let $\mathcal{U}_{s}\coloneqq\{T\in\mathcal{T}\mid T\subseteq Y_{s}\}$.
Note that $\mathcal{U}_{s}$ and ${\mathchoice{\crossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}}$ are disjoint and their union is the set of all terminals $T\in\mathcal{T}$ that are not disjoint from $Y_{s}$.
We add $w$ vertices $\{q_{s,i}\}_{i\in{\mathchoice{\left[w\right]}{[w]}{[w]}{[w]}}}$ to $G_{s}$ such that each has the neighborhood $V({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})\setminus Y_{s}$.
Call this graph $G_{s}^{*}$.
These additional vertices can be used to simulate that a subgraph gets connected outside $G_{s}$.
Let $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}\right]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}}$ and denote with $Q_{s,i}\coloneqq({\mathchoice{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}\left(i\right)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}(i)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}(i)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}(i)}}\cap Y_{s})\cup\{q_{s,i}\}$ the vertices in $Y_{s}$ of the terminal set assigned to the $i$-th subgraph crossing ${\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}}$, which we called ${\mathchoice{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}\left(i\right)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}(i)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}(i)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}(i)}}$, combined with $q_{s,i}$.
Additionally, define for each $P\in\futurePartBaseOp_{\tau}$ the set $R_{s,P}\coloneqq V(G[P])\setminus Y_{s}$ to be all vertices of edges contained in $P$ that are not in $Y_{s}$.
Finally, define the instance $\mathscr{D}_{s,\tau}\coloneqq(G_{s}^{*},\mathcal{U}_{s}\cup\{Q_{s,i}\}_{i\in{\mathchoice{\left[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}\right]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}}}\cup\{R_{s,P}\}_{P\in\futurePartBaseOp_{\tau}},d^{\prime})$, where for all $T\in\mathcal{U}_{s}$ we have $d^{\prime}(T)=d(T)$, for all $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}\right]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}}$, we have $d^{\prime}(Q_{s,i})=1$, and for all $P\in\futurePartBaseOp_{\tau}$, we have $d^{\prime}(R_{s,P})={\mathchoice{\left|\{P^{\prime}\in\futurePartBaseOp_{\tau}\mid R_{s,P^{\prime}}=R_{s,P}\}\right|}{{|\{P^{\prime}\in\futurePartBaseOp_{\tau}\mid R_{s,P^{\prime}}=R_{s,P}\}|}}{{|\{P^{\prime}\in\futurePartBaseOp_{\tau}\mid R_{s,P^{\prime}}=R_{s,P}\}|}}{{|\{P^{\prime}\in\futurePartBaseOp_{\tau}\mid R_{s,P^{\prime}}=R_{s,P}\}|}}}$.
\par\begin{definition}For each $s\in V(S)$ the data-table $D(s)$ is exactly the set of syntactically valid tuples $\tau$ at $s$ where the instance $\mathscr{D}_{s,\tau}$ has a solution $(\mathcal{F},\pi)$ such that
\begin{enumerate}\enumerate@item@we have $E({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})\cap\bigcup E(\mathcal{F})\subseteq\bigcup\pastPartBaseOp_{\tau}\cup\bigcup\futurePartBaseOp_{\tau}$,
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@for all $P\in\futurePartBaseOp_{\tau}$ and $F\in\pi^{-1}(R_{s,P})$, the set $V(F)$ is disjoint from $\{q_{s,i}\}_{i\in{\mathchoice{\left[w\right]}{[w]}{[w]}{[w]}}}$,
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@for all $P\in\futurePartBaseOp_{\tau}$, there is a $F\in\pi^{-1}(R_{s,P})$ with $E(F)\cap E({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{S}}{\boundaryOp_{S}}{\boundaryOp_{S}}{\boundaryOp_{S}}})=P$,
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@for all $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[w\right]}{[w]}{[w]}{[w]}}$, let $\mathcal{P}_{i}\coloneqq{\mathchoice{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau}^{-1}\left(i\right)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau}^{-1}(i)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau}^{-1}(i)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau}^{-1}(i)}}$, then
\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@if $\mathcal{P}_{i}=\emptyset$, we have $q_{s,i}\notin\bigcup V(\mathcal{F})$,
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@otherwise, there is exactly one $F\in\mathcal{F}$ with $q_{s,i}\in V(F)$ and this $F$ additionally satisfies that $E(F-q_{s,i})$ can be partitioned into $\{E_{P}\}_{P\in\mathcal{P}_{i}}$ such that for all $P\in\mathcal{P}_{i}$, the graph $F[E_{P}]$ is connected and $E_{P}\cap E({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})=P$.\end{itemize}\end{enumerate}\end{definition}
\par With \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-edge-partitions} we ensure that the edges used in the solution are accounted for in $\pastPartBaseOp_{\tau}$ and $\futurePartBaseOp_{\tau}$.
We want to be able to assume that for every $P\in\futurePartBaseOp_{\tau}$ there is a subgraph completely contained in $G[Y_{s}]\cup{\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}}$ connecting all edges of $P$.
So, in \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-future-sensible} we ensure that the vertices $\{q_{s,i}\}_{i\in{\mathchoice{\left[w\right]}{[w]}{[w]}{[w]}}}$—that are used to simulate that a subgraph gets connected outside of this graph—are not included in the subgraphs connecting the edges in $P$.
These subgraphs should also use exactly the edge-set $P$ in $E({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})$, which we ensure with \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-future-exact}.
Finally, consider \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-past} and $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[w\right]}{[w]}{[w]}{[w]}}$.
If $\mathcal{P}_{i}=\emptyset$, that is, no edges are assigned to the $i$-th subgraph, the vertex $q_{s,i}$, which is used to mark the $i$-th subgraph crossing ${\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}}$, is not used in any subgraph.
Otherwise, we again ensure that for each $P\in\mathcal{P}_{i}$ there is a subgraph in this solution that connects the edges of $P$ inside $G_{s}$.
Notice that we can combine \lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-edge-partitions}, \lx@cref{refnum}{item:simple-tcd-future-exact} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{item:simple-tcd-past} to show, that for any $F\in\mathcal{F}$ with $E(F)\cap E({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})\neq\emptyset$ there is a $P\in\pastPartBaseOp_{\tau}\cup\futurePartBaseOp_{\tau}$ with $P\subseteq E(F)$.
\par Immediately, we observe that this dynamic program can indeed be used to determine whether $\mathscr{P}$ is a positive instance, if we assume that it is calculated correctly.
\par\begin{lemma}Let $r$ be the root of $S$.
Then, $\mathscr{P}$ is a positive instance if and only if $D(r)\neq\emptyset$.
\end{lemma}
\@proof We note that ${\mathchoice{\cutEdgesOp\left(r\right)}{\cutEdgesOp(r)}{\cutEdgesOp(r)}{\cutEdgesOp(r)}}=\emptyset$.
Therefore, ${\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{r}}{\boundaryOp_{r}}{\boundaryOp_{r}}{\boundaryOp_{r}}}$ is the empty graph, which means that there is exactly one syntactically valid tuple $\tau$ at $r$.
$\mathscr{D}_{r,\tau}$ is the same instance as $\mathscr{P}$ except that there are $w$ nodes added with neighborhood $V({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{r}}{\boundaryOp_{r}}{\boundaryOp_{r}}{\boundaryOp_{r}}})\setminus Y_{r}=\emptyset$.
So, every solution to $\mathscr{P}$ can be transformed into a valid solution of $\mathscr{D}_{r,\tau}$ and vice versa.
Additionally, every solution to $\mathscr{D}_{r,\tau}$ also satisfies the additional requirements of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcw-dp}, proving the statement.
\qed\end@proof
\par To compute this dynamic program, we consider that the size of the data-table is bounded by a function of the parameter.
Denote with $\mathcal{V}_{s}$ the set of all syntactically valid tuples at $s$.
As ${\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\crossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\crossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\crossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\crossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}}|}}}\leq w$ and as ${\mathchoice{\left|E({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})\right|}{{|E({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})|}}{{|E({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})|}}{{|E({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})|}}}\leq w$, we have ${\mathchoice{\left|D(s)\right|}{{|D(s)|}}{{|D(s)|}}{{|D(s)|}}}\leq{\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{V}_{s}\right|}{{|\mathcal{V}_{s}|}}{{|\mathcal{V}_{s}|}}{{|\mathcal{V}_{s}|}}}\leq 2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left(w\log w\right)}{\OOp(w\log w)}{\OOp(w\log w)}{\OOp(w\log w)}}$.
Now assume that for all bold children $b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}$ of $s$, we have already computed the data-table $D(b)$.
We now show how to compute $D(s)$ in $\fpt$-time.
To achieve this, it is enough to decide in $\fpt$-time for a given $\tau\in\mathcal{V}_{s}$ whether $\tau\in D(s)$.
For this we iterate over all simultaneous choices of $\tau_{b}\in D(b)$ for $b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}$ and check whether the solutions witnessing $\tau_{b}\in D(b)$ can be extended to solutions witnessing $\tau\in D(s)$.
Then, we show how to create instances of $\gstp$ with bounded fracture number of its augmented graph such that one of these instances is positive if and only if the aforementioned condition is met.
Combined with the results from \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{sec:gstp-fnS} this yields the result of this Section.
\par Assume for all $b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}$ a $\tau_{b}\in D(b)$ is fixed.
To combine the subgraphs of the sub-solutions witnessing $\tau_{b}\in D(b)$ to a solution witnessing $\tau\in D(s)$, we need to be able to translate the local numbering of the solution subgraphs into a numbering shared across all solution subgraphs not fully contained in one connected component of $S-s$.
When dealing with a shared mapping, we want to avoid that we map solution subgraphs assigned to different terminal sets to the same index.
For convenience denote with $A\coloneqq\{s\}\cup{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}$ the set of $s$ and all its bold children, with $\mathcal{T}^{\star}_{s}\coloneqq\{T\in\mathcal{T}\mid T\subseteq X_{s}\}$ the set of terminal sets completely contained in $X_{s}$, and with $\mathcal{X}\coloneqq\mathcal{T}^{\star}_{s}\cup\bigcup_{a\in A}{\mathchoice{\crossLinkOp\left(a\right)}{\crossLinkOp(a)}{\crossLinkOp(a)}{\crossLinkOp(a)}}$ the set of all terminal sets, which are not completely contained in one sub-tree of $S-s$.
As $s$ is simple it has at most $w+2$ bold children.
Since we applied \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{rr:cross-link-demand-large}, $w(w+3)$ is an upper bound on the cumulated demand of all terminal sets crossing the links between $s$ and its parent or any of its bold children.
It is also an upper bound on the number of edges crossing bold links adjacent to $s$.
So, we need at most $w(w+3)$ shared indices for the crossing subgraphs.
We set $u\coloneqq w(w+3)+w$ slightly larger than this upper bound to have spare indices.
\par\begin{definition}For all $a\in A$ let $\localSharedMapBaseOp_{a}\colon{\mathchoice{\left[w\right]}{[w]}{[w]}{[w]}}\to{\mathchoice{\left[u\right]}{[u]}{[u]}{[u]}}$ be an injective function and if $s\neq a$ let $\unknownTerminalInOp_{a}$ be a unique symbol.
Additionally, let $\blockedIndex$ be a another unique symbol.
We call $(\localSharedMapBaseOp_{a})_{a\in A}$ a \emph{local to shared mapping} if there is a function $\enumerateSharedOp\colon{\mathchoice{\left[u\right]}{[u]}{[u]}{[u]}}\to\mathcal{X}\cup\{\unknownTerminalInOp_{b}\}_{b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}}\cup\{\blockedIndex\}$, called a \emph{shared mapping enumerator}, such that
\begin{enumerate}\enumerate@item@for all $a\in A$ and $T\in{\mathchoice{\crossLinkOp\left(a\right)}{\crossLinkOp(a)}{\crossLinkOp(a)}{\crossLinkOp(a)}}$, we have ${\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}}={\mathchoice{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{a}\left({\mathchoice{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{a}^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{a}^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{a}^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{a}^{-1}(T)}}\right)}{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{a}({\mathchoice{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{a}^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{a}^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{a}^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{a}^{-1}(T)}})}{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{a}({\mathchoice{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{a}^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{a}^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{a}^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{a}^{-1}(T)}})}{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{a}({\mathchoice{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{a}^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{a}^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{a}^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{a}^{-1}(T)}})}}$,
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@for all $T\in\mathcal{T}^{\star}_{s}$, we have ${\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}}|}}}\leq d(T)$,
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@for all $b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}$, we have $\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\unknownTerminalInOp_{b})\subseteq{\mathchoice{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{b}\left({\mathchoice{\left[w\right]}{[w]}{[w]}{[w]}}\setminus{\mathchoice{\left[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(b\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}}\right]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(b\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(b\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(b\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}}]}}\right)}{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{b}({\mathchoice{\left[w\right]}{[w]}{[w]}{[w]}}\setminus{\mathchoice{\left[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(b\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}}\right]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(b\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(b\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(b\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}}]}})}{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{b}({\mathchoice{\left[w\right]}{[w]}{[w]}{[w]}}\setminus{\mathchoice{\left[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(b\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}}\right]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(b\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(b\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(b\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}}]}})}{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{b}({\mathchoice{\left[w\right]}{[w]}{[w]}{[w]}}\setminus{\mathchoice{\left[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(b\right)}{\demandCrossLi
