\par If ${\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{E}_{F}\right|}{{|\mathcal{E}_{F}|}}{{|\mathcal{E}_{F}|}}{{|\mathcal{E}_{F}|}}}=1$, the last argument shows that $\widetilde{F}$ is connected.
So, assume ${\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{E}_{F}\right|}{{|\mathcal{E}_{F}|}}{{|\mathcal{E}_{F}|}}{{|\mathcal{E}_{F}|}}}>1$.
This is only the case if there is an $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[w\right]}{[w]}{[w]}{[w]}}$ such that $q_{s,i}\in V(F)$.
Let $K\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left(\widetilde{F}\right)}{\compOp(\widetilde{F})}{\compOp(\widetilde{F})}{\compOp(\widetilde{F})}}$.
Since $E(K)\cap E(J_{s})\neq\emptyset$, there is a $D\in\mathcal{E}_{F}$ with $E(K)\cap D\neq\emptyset$.
By definition of $D$, there is an edge $uv\in E({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})\cap D$ with $v\notin Y_{s}$.
Note that, by construction, $vq_{s,i}\in E(\widetilde{F})$.
Since all vertices of $V(\widetilde{F}[D])$ are in the same component of $\widetilde{F}$, we have $v\in V(\widetilde{K})$ and by extension $q_{s,i}\in V(K)$, showing that $\widetilde{F}$ has one connected component.
\par To describe our solution consider a $F\in\mathcal{F}^{\downarrow}$ and choose a $v\in V(\widetilde{F})\cap V(J_{s})$.
Set $\widetilde{F_{\sigma(F)}}\coloneqq\widetilde{F}\cup\{vm_{\sigma(F)}\}$.
If ${\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp\left(\sigma(F)\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp(\sigma(F))}{\enumerateSharedOp(\sigma(F))}{\enumerateSharedOp(\sigma(F))}}\notin\mathcal{X}$, then $\widetilde{C_{\sigma(F)}}=\emptyset\subseteq V(\widetilde{F_{\sigma(F)}})$.
If ${\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp\left(\sigma(F)\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp(\sigma(F))}{\enumerateSharedOp(\sigma(F))}{\enumerateSharedOp(\sigma(F))}}\in\mathcal{X}$, then $\widetilde{C_{\sigma(F)}}={\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp\left(\sigma(F)\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp(\sigma(F))}{\enumerateSharedOp(\sigma(F))}{\enumerateSharedOp(\sigma(F))}}\cap X_{s}$.
We have $\pi(F)\cap Y_{s}={\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp\left(\sigma(F)\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp(\sigma(F))}{\enumerateSharedOp(\sigma(F))}{\enumerateSharedOp(\sigma(F))}}\cap Y_{s}$.
Therefore, ${\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp\left(\sigma(F)\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp(\sigma(F))}{\enumerateSharedOp(\sigma(F))}{\enumerateSharedOp(\sigma(F))}}\cap X_{s}\subseteq V(F)$ and, in particular, ${\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp\left(\sigma(F)\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp(\sigma(F))}{\enumerateSharedOp(\sigma(F))}{\enumerateSharedOp(\sigma(F))}}\cap X_{s}\subseteq V(\widetilde{F_{\sigma(F)}})$.
Since $m_{\sigma(F)}\in V(\widetilde{F_{\sigma(F)}})$, we have $\widetilde{M_{\sigma(F)}}\subseteq V(\widetilde{F_{\sigma(F)}})$; so, we assign $\widetilde{F_{\sigma(F)}}$ to $\widetilde{M_{\sigma(F)}}$ satisfying the demand of all $\{\widetilde{M_{i}}\}_{i\in{\mathchoice{\left[u\right]}{[u]}{[u]}{[u]}}\setminus{\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}\left(\blockedIndex\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}}}$.
\par Consider $F\in\mathcal{F}^{\uparrow}=\bigcup_{P\in\futurePartBaseOp_{\tau}}\pi^{-1}(R_{s,P})$.
We note that $\widetilde{F}$ is edge-disjoint from all already assigned subgraphs.
In our solution we assign $\widetilde{F}$ to $\pi(F)$ as well.
This satisfies the demand of all $\{R_{s,P}\}_{P\in\futurePartBaseOp_{\tau}}$.
\par Finally, consider $F\in F^{\star}=F^{\prime}\setminus(\mathcal{F}^{\uparrow}\cup\mathcal{F}^{\downarrow})$.
As $V(F)\subseteq X_{s}\cup\bigcup_{t\in{\mathchoice{\thinChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\thinChildrenOp(s)}{\thinChildrenOp(s)}{\thinChildrenOp(s)}}}X_{t}$, we have $F=\widetilde{F}$ and notice that $\widetilde{F}$ is edge-disjoint from all already assigned subgraphs.
So, we assign $\widetilde{F}$ to $\pi(F)$ as well.
We claim that this satisfies the demand of all $\mathcal{T}^{\star}_{s}$.
Let $T\in\mathcal{T}^{\star}_{s}$ and consider $\pi^{-1}(T)$.
Set $\mathcal{F}^{\boundaryOp}_{T}\coloneqq\sigma^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}})$ and notice that $\mathcal{F}^{\boundaryOp}_{T}\subseteq\pi^{-1}(T)$.
Let $F\in\pi^{-1}(T)\setminus\mathcal{F}^{\boundaryOp}_{T}$.
As $\pi(F)\subseteq X_{s}$, we have $V(F)\cap X_{s}\neq\emptyset$ and since $F\notin\mathcal{F}^{\boundaryOp}$, we have that $E(F)$ is disjoint from $\bigcup_{a\in A}E({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{a}}{\boundaryOp_{a}}{\boundaryOp_{a}}{\boundaryOp_{a}}})$.
Therefore, $V(F)\subseteq X_{s}\cup\bigcup_{t\in{\mathchoice{\thinChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\thinChildrenOp(s)}{\thinChildrenOp(s)}{\thinChildrenOp(s)}}}X_{t}$ and $F\in\mathcal{T}^{\star}_{s}$.
So, we assign ${\mathchoice{\left|\pi^{-1}(T)\right|}{{|\pi^{-1}(T)|}}{{|\pi^{-1}(T)|}}{{|\pi^{-1}(T)|}}}-{\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{F}^{\boundaryOp}_{T}\right|}{{|\mathcal{F}^{\boundaryOp}_{T}|}}{{|\mathcal{F}^{\boundaryOp}_{T}|}}{{|\mathcal{F}^{\boundaryOp}_{T}|}}}={\mathchoice{\left|\pi^{-1}(T)\right|}{{|\pi^{-1}(T)|}}{{|\pi^{-1}(T)|}}{{|\pi^{-1}(T)|}}}-{\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}}|}}}$ subgraphs to $T$ satisfying its demand.
Completing our solution of $\mathscr{C}(s,\tau,(\tau_{b})_{b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}},\enumerateSharedOp,(\localSharedMapBaseOp_{b})_{b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}})$.
\par To complete the prove that $(\tau_{b})_{b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}}$ witness $\tau\in D(s)$, we need to verify the additional requirements to our solution $(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}},\widetilde{\pi})$ posed in \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcd-witness}.
Notice that \lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-witness-edge-partitions}, \lx@cref{refnum}{item:simple-tcd-witness-future-sensible} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{item:simple-tcd-witness-future-exact} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcd-witness} directly follow from $\tau\in D(s)$ and we already argued \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-witness-sub-connected} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcd-witness}.
\par Consider $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[w\right]}{[w]}{[w]}{[w]}}$ and $\mathcal{P}_{i}={\mathchoice{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau}\left(i\right)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau}(i)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau}(i)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau}(i)}}$.
If $\mathcal{P}_{i}=\emptyset$, there is no $F\in\mathcal{F}$ with $q_{s,i}\in V(F)$.
As for all $\widetilde{F}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}$, we have $q_{s,i}\in\widetilde{F}$ if and only if $q_{s,i}\in F$, there also is no $\widetilde{F}^{\prime}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}$ with $q_{s,i}\in V(\widetilde{F}^{\prime})$.
Otherwise, there is exactly one $F\in\mathcal{F}$ with $q_{s,i}\in V(F)$.
For $P\in\widetilde{\mathcal{P}_{i}}$ consider $D\in\mathcal{E}_{F}$ with $P=D\cap E({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})$ and choose as the partition for $P$ the set $\widetilde{E}_{P}\coloneqq(D\cap E(J_{s}))\cup\widetilde{Z}^{E}_{D}$.
We know that $\widetilde{F}[D\cup\widetilde{Z}^{E}_{D}]=\widetilde{F}[\widetilde{E}_{P}]$ is connected.
Consider a $\tilde{p}_{b,P^{\prime}}\in\widetilde{Z}_{D}$.
By definition of $\{\tau_{b}\}_{b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}}$, we have $\emptyset\neq P^{\prime}\subseteq D\cap E(J_{s})\subseteq\widetilde{E}_{P}$.
So, there is no $D^{\prime}\in\mathcal{E}_{F}\setminus\{D\}$ with $\tilde{p}_{b,P^{\prime}}\in\widetilde{Z}_{D^{\prime}}$ implying that the partitions are actually disjoint and that any vertex of $\widetilde{Z}_{D}=V(\widetilde{F}[\widetilde{E}_{P}])\cap\widetilde{Z}$ is only a member of $V(\widetilde{F}[\widetilde{E}_{P}])$.
Additionally, we can verify that $\bigcup_{P\in\mathcal{P}_{i}}\widetilde{E}_{P}=E(\widetilde{F}-\{q_{s,i}\})$, showing that \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-witness-past} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcd-witness} is satisfied.
\par Let $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[u\right]}{[u]}{[u]}{[u]}}\setminus{\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}\left(\blockedIndex\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}}$ and $\{\widetilde{F}\}\coloneqq\widetilde{\pi}^{-1}(\widetilde{M_{i}})$.
By construction $\widetilde{F}$ is the unique $\widetilde{F}^{\prime}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}$ with $m_{i}\in V(\widetilde{F}^{\prime})$ and only one edge adjacent to $m_{i}$ is contained in $E(\widetilde{F})$.
Now, let $a\in A$ and $P\in\pastPartBaseOp_{\tau_{a}}$ be such that ${\mathchoice{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{a}\left({\mathchoice{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{a}}\left(P\right)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{a}}(P)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{a}}(P)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{a}}(P)}}\right)}{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{a}({\mathchoice{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{a}}\left(P\right)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{a}}(P)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{a}}(P)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{a}}(P)}})}{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{a}({\mathchoice{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{a}}\left(P\right)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{a}}(P)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{a}}(P)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{a}}(P)}})}{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{a}({\mathchoice{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{a}}\left(P\right)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{a}}(P)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{a}}(P)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{a}}(P)}})}}=i$.
Consider $F^{\prime}\coloneqq\sigma^{-1}({\mathchoice{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{a}\left({\mathchoice{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{a}}\left(P\right)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{a}}(P)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{a}}(P)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{a}}(P)}}\right)}{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{a}({\mathchoice{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{a}}\left(P\right)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{a}}(P)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{a}}(P)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{a}}(P)}})}{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{a}({\mathchoice{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{a}}\left(P\right)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{a}}(P)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{a}}(P)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{a}}(P)}})}{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{a}({\mathchoice{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{a}}\left(P\right)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{a}}(P)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{a}}(P)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{a}}(P)}})}})=\sigma^{-1}(i)$; so, by construction, we have $\widetilde{F^{\prime}}=\widetilde{F}$.
If $a=s$ by \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-past} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcw-dp} and otherwise by construction, we have $P\subseteq E(F^{\prime})\cap E({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{a}}{\boundaryOp_{a}}{\boundaryOp_{a}}{\boundaryOp_{a}}})=E(\widetilde{F})\cap E({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{a}}{\boundaryOp_{a}}{\boundaryOp_{a}}{\boundaryOp_{a}}})$.
If $a\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}$, then we add $\tilde{p}_{a,P}$ to $\widetilde{F}$ during construction showing that \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-witness-marked} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcd-witness}, and by extension \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcd-witness} itself, is satisfied.
\qed\end@proof
\par\begin{lemma}If for all $b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}$ there is a $\tau_{b}\in D(b)$ such that $(\tau_{b})_{b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}}$ witnesses $\tau\in D(s)$, then $\tau\in D(s)$ indeed holds.
\end{lemma}
\@proof Let $(\localSharedMapBaseOp_{b})_{b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}}$ and $\enumerateSharedOp$ be local to shared mappings and a shared mapping enumerator such that they fulfill the requirement of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcd-witness}.
Let $(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}},\tilde{\pi})$ be a solution to $\mathscr{C}(s,\tau,(\tau_{b})_{b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}},\enumerateSharedOp,(\localSharedMapBaseOp_{a})_{a\in A})$ satisfying the additional requirements of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcd-witness}.
For all $b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}$ let $(\mathcal{F}_{b},\pi_{b})$ be a solution to $\mathscr{D}_{b,\tau_{b}}$ satisfying the additional requirements of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcw-dp}.
Based on this, we now provide a solution to $\mathscr{D}_{s,\tau}$ satisfying the additional requirements of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcw-dp}.
\par For every $b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}$, set $\mathcal{F}^{\boundaryOp}_{b}\coloneqq\{F\in\mathcal{F}_{b}\mid E(F)\cap E({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{b}}{\boundaryOp_{b}}{\boundaryOp_{b}}{\boundaryOp_{b}}})\neq\emptyset\}$.
For all $F\in\mathcal{F}_{b}\setminus\mathcal{F}^{\boundaryOp}_{b}$, we know $\pi_{b}(F)\in\mathcal{U}_{b}\subseteq\mathcal{U}_{s}$ and that $F$ is contained in $G[Y_{b}]$, which in turn is a subgraph of $G^{*}_{s}$.
So, we assign $F$ to $\pi_{b}(F)$ in our solution of $\mathscr{D}_{s,\tau}$.
For all $U\in\mathcal{U}_{b}\subseteq\mathcal{U}_{s}$, this assigns $d(U)-{\mathchoice{\left|\pi^{-1}_{b}(U)\cap\mathcal{F}^{\boundaryOp}_{b}\right|}{{|\pi^{-1}_{b}(U)\cap\mathcal{F}^{\boundaryOp}_{b}|}}{{|\pi^{-1}_{b}(U)\cap\mathcal{F}^{\boundaryOp}_{b}|}}{{|\pi^{-1}_{b}(U)\cap\mathcal{F}^{\boundaryOp}_{b}|}}}$ many subgraphs to $U$.
\par Now, consider any $F\in\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}$.
Let $\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\star}\coloneqq\{F\in\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}\mid V(F)\subseteq X_{s}\cup\bigcup_{t\in{\mathchoice{\thinChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\thinChildrenOp(s)}{\thinChildrenOp(s)}{\thinChildrenOp(s)}}}X_{t}\}$ and set $\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\boundaryOp}\coloneq\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}\setminus\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\star}$.
All $F\in\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\star}$ are contained in $G^{*}_{s}$ and $\pi(F)\in\mathcal{T}^{\star}_{s}$.
As $F$ is disjoint from all previously assigned subgraphs, we assign $F$ to $\pi(F)$ in our solution.
For all $T\in\mathcal{T}^{\star}_{s}$, we assign $d^{\prime}(T)-{\mathchoice{\left|\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(T)\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\boundaryOp}\right|}{{|\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(T)\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\boundaryOp}|}}{{|\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(T)\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\boundaryOp}|}}{{|\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(T)\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\boundaryOp}|}}}=d(T)-{\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}}|}}}-{\mathchoice{\left|\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(T)\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\boundaryOp}\right|}{{|\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(T)\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\boundaryOp}|}}{{|\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(T)\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\boundaryOp}|}}{{|\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(T)\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\boundaryOp}|}}}$ subgraphs to $T$.
\par\par Finally, consider $\widetilde{F}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\boundaryOp}$.
The graph $\widetilde{F}$ might not be a subgraphs of $G^{*}_{s}$.
In particular, the vertices $\widetilde{Z}\coloneqq\{\tilde{p}_{b,P}\}_{b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}},P\in\pastPartBaseOp_{\tau_{b}}\cup\futurePartBaseOp_{\tau_{b}}}$ and $\widetilde{W}\coloneqq\{\tilde{m}_{i}\}_{i\in{\mathchoice{\left[u\right]}{[u]}{[u]}{[u]}}\setminus{\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}\left(\blockedIndex\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}}}$ are not contained in $G^{*}_{s}$.
We aim to create a connected subgraph $\psi(\widetilde{F})$ similar to $\widetilde{F}$ contained in $G^{*}_{s}$.
\par By \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-witness-marked} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcd-witness}, we can remove $\widetilde{W}$ from $\widetilde{F}$ without destroying connectedness.
For each $\tilde{p}_{b,P}\in\widetilde{Z}$, we now find edge-disjoint connected subgraphs $H_{b,P}$ completely contained in $G_{b}$ with $E(H_{b,P})\cap E({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{b}}{\boundaryOp_{b}}{\boundaryOp_{b}}{\boundaryOp_{b}}})=P$.
If $P\in\futurePartBaseOp_{\tau_{b}}$, choose a unique $F\in\pi^{-1}_{b}(R_{b,P})$ and set $H_{b,P}\coloneqq F$.
This works by choice of the demand of $R_{b,P}$ and \lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-future-sensible} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{item:simple-tcd-future-exact} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcw-dp}.
Otherwise $P\in\pastPartBaseOp_{\tau_{b}}$.
Set $i\coloneqq{{\mathchoice{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{b}}\left(P\right)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{b}}(P)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{b}}(P)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{b}}(P)}}}$ and let $F_{b}^{i}\in\mathcal{F}_{b}$ be the unique subgraph with $q_{b,i}\in V(F^{i}_{b})$.
By \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-past}, we can partition $E(F^{i}_{b}-q_{b,i})$ into $\{E_{P^{\prime}}\}_{P^{\prime}\in{\mathchoice{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{b}}^{-1}\left(i\right)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{b}}^{-1}(i)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{b}}^{-1}(i)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{b}}^{-1}(i)}}}$ such that for all $P^{\prime}\in{\mathchoice{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{b}}^{-1}\left(i\right)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{b}}^{-1}(i)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{b}}^{-1}(i)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{b}}^{-1}(i)}}$, the graph $F[E_{P^{\prime}}]$ is connected and $E_{P^{\prime}}\cap E({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})=P^{\prime}$.
Then, choose $H_{b,P}\coloneqq F^{i}_{b}[E_{P}]$ and note that $\bigcup_{P^{\prime}\in{\mathchoice{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{b}}\left(i\right)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{b}}(i)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{b}}(i)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{b}}(i)}}}F^{i}_{b}[E_{P^{\prime}}]=F^{i}_{b}-q_{b,i}$.
Now, set $\psi(\widetilde{F})$ equal to $\widetilde{F}$ where we replace all $\tilde{p}_{b,P}\in V(\widetilde{F})\cap\widetilde{Z}$ by $H_{b,P}$, that is $\psi(\widetilde{F})\coloneqq(\widetilde{F}-\widetilde{W}-\widetilde{Z})\cup\bigcup_{\tilde{p}_{b,P}\in V(\widetilde{F})\cap\widetilde{Z}}H_{b,P}$.
By \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-witness-sub-connected} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcd-witness}, all such graphs are edge-disjoint from each other and from all already assigned subgraphs.
To see that $\psi(\widetilde{F})$ is actually connected, notice that, by \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-witness-sub-connected} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcd-witness}, $N(\tilde{p}_{b,P})=V(F[P])\subseteq V(H_{b,P})$ and that $H_{b,P}$ is connected. So, any path in $F$ going over $\tilde{p}_{b,P}$ can be replaced by a path going through $H_{b,P}$.
\par Now, we aim to assign $\psi(\widetilde{F})$ to a terminal set.
If $\tilde{\pi}(\widetilde{F})\in\{R_{s,P}\}_{P\in\pastPartBaseOp_{\tau}\cup\futurePartBaseOp_{\tau}}\cup\mathcal{T}^{\star}_{s}$, we assign $\psi(\widetilde{F})$ to $\tilde{\pi}(\widetilde{F})\subseteq V(\widetilde{F}-\widetilde{W}-\widetilde{Z})\subseteq V(\psi(\widetilde{F}))$.
For $T\in\{R_{s,P}\}_{P\in\pastPartBaseOp_{\tau}\cup\futurePartBaseOp_{\tau}}$, we have $\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(T)\subseteq\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\boundaryOp}$.
Thus, this satisfies the demand of $T$ and \lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-future-sensible} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{item:simple-tcd-future-exact} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcw-dp}.
For $T\in\mathcal{T}^{\star}_{s}$, this newly assigns ${\mathchoice{\left|\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(T)\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\boundaryOp}\right|}{{|\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(T)\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\boundaryOp}|}}{{|\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(T)\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\boundaryOp}|}}{{|\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(T)\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\boundaryOp}|}}}$ subgraphs to $T$.
Otherwise, there is a unique $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[u\right]}{[u]}{[u]}{[u]}}\setminus{\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}\left(\blockedIndex\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}}$ with $\tilde{\pi}(\widetilde{F})=\widetilde{M_{i}}$.
Consider $b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}$ with ${\mathchoice{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{b}^{-1}\left(i\right)}{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{b}^{-1}(i)}{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{b}^{-1}(i)}{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{b}^{-1}(i)}}\neq\emptyset$.
Set $\ell\coloneqq{\mathchoice{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{b}^{-1}\left(i\right)}{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{b}^{-1}(i)}{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{b}^{-1}(i)}{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{b}^{-1}(i)}}$.
By \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-witness-marked} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcd-witness}, for all $P\in{\mathchoice{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{b}}^{-1}\left(\ell\right)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{b}}^{-1}(\ell)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{b}}^{-1}(\ell)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau_{b}}^{-1}(\ell)}}$ we have $\tilde{p}_{b,P}\in V(\widetilde{F})$.
Thus, $H_{b,P}$ is contained in $\psi(\widetilde{F})$ and in particular $V(F^{\ell}_{b}-q_{b,\ell})\subseteq V(\psi(\widetilde{F}))$.
If $\ell\in{\mathchoice{\left[w\right]}{[w]}{[w]}{[w]}}\setminus{\mathchoice{\left[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}\right]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}}$, by \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:local-shared-def-unknown-is-unnamend-or-blocked} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:local-shared-map} we have ${\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}}=\unknownTerminalInOp_{b}$.
In particular $\pi_{b}(F^{\ell}_{b})\in\mathcal{U}_{b}$.
Since $\pi_{b}(F^{\ell}_{b})\subseteq Y_{b}$, we have $\pi_{b}(F^{\ell}_{b})\subseteq V(F^{\ell}_{b}-q_{b,\ell})\subseteq V(\psi(\widetilde{F}))$ and we assign $\psi(\widetilde{F})$ to $\pi_{b}(F^{\ell}_{b})$.
Otherwise, we have by \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:local-shared-def-unnamed-sensible} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:local-shared-map} that ${\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}}\in\mathcal{X}$.
As ${\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}}\cap X_{s}\subseteq\widetilde{M_{i}}=\tilde{\pi}(\widetilde{F})$, we have ${\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}}\cap X_{s}\subseteq V(\psi(\widetilde{F}))$.
Now, let $b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}$ be such that ${\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}}\in{\mathchoice{\crossLinkOp\left(b\right)}{\crossLinkOp(b)}{\crossLinkOp(b)}{\crossLinkOp(b)}}$ and again set $\ell\coloneqq{\mathchoice{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{b}^{-1}\left(i\right)}{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{b}^{-1}(i)}{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{b}^{-1}(i)}{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{b}^{-1}(i)}}$.
Then, $\ell\in{\mathchoice{\left[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}\right]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}}$ and $\pi_{b}(F^{\ell}_{b})\setminus\{q_{b,\ell}\}=Q_{b,\ell}\setminus\{q_{b,\ell}\}={\mathchoice{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{b}\left(\ell\right)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{b}(\ell)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{b}(\ell)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{b}(\ell)}}\cap X_{b}={\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}}\cap Y_{b}$.
Additionally, $\pi_{b}(F^{\ell}_{b})\setminus\{q_{b,\ell}\}={\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}}\cap Y_{b}\subseteq V(F^{\ell}_{b}-q_{b,\ell})\subseteq V(\psi(\widetilde{F}))$.
For $b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}$ with ${\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}}\notin{\mathchoice{\crossLinkOp\left(b\right)}{\crossLinkOp(b)}{\crossLinkOp(b)}{\crossLinkOp(b)}}$, we have ${\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}}\cap Y_{b}=\emptyset\subseteq V(\psi(\widetilde{F}))$.
Thus, ${\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}}\cap Y_{s}={\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}}\cap(X_{s}\cup\bigcup_{b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}}Y_{b})\subseteq V(\psi(\widetilde{F}))$ and if ${\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}}\subseteq Y_{s}$, we assign $\psi(\widetilde{F})$ to ${\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}}$.
Otherwise, ${\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}}\in{\mathchoice{\crossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}}$ and by \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:local-shared-def-preserves-crossLink} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:local-shared-map} we have ${\mathchoice{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{s}^{-1}\left(i\right)}{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{s}^{-1}(i)}{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{s}^{-1}(i)}{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{s}^{-1}(i)}}\in{\mathchoice{\left[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}\right]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}}$.
Set $\ell\coloneqq{\mathchoice{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{s}^{-1}\left(i\right)}{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{s}^{-1}(i)}{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{s}^{-1}(i)}{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{s}^{-1}(i)}}$.
Thus, $q_{s,\ell}\in\pi(\widetilde{F})=\widetilde{M_{i}}\subseteq V(\psi(\widetilde{F}))$.
So, $Q_{s,\ell}=({\mathchoice{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}\left(\ell\right)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}(\ell)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}(\ell)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}(\ell)}}\cap Y_{s})\cup\{q_{s,\ell}\}=({\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}{\enumerateSharedOp(i)}}\cap Y_{s})\cup\{q_{s,\ell}\}\subseteq V(\psi(\widetilde{F}))$ and we assign $\psi(\widetilde{F})$ to $Q_{s,\ell}$.
\par We claim that now all demands are satisfied.
Recall, that we already proved this for all terminal sets in $\{R_{s,P}\}_{P\in\futurePartBaseOp_{\tau}}$.
For $T\in\mathcal{T}^{\star}_{s}$, before considering $F\in\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(\{\widetilde{M_{i}}\}_{i\in{\mathchoice{\left[u\right]}{[u]}{[u]}{[u]}}\setminus{\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}\left(\blockedIndex\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}}})$ we already assigned $d(T)-{\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}}|}}}$ subgraphs to $T$.
As $T\subseteq Y_{s}$, for all $i\in{\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}}$ the subgraph $\psi(\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(M_{i}))$ gets assigned to $T$ satisfying its demand.
Let $T\in\mathcal{X}\setminus(\mathcal{T}^{\star}_{s}\cup\{{\mathchoice{\crossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}}\})$.
By \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:local-shared-def-preserves-crossLink} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:local-shared-map}, ${\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}}|}}}=d(T)$ and by construction for all $i\in{\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(T)}}$, the subgraph $\psi(\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(\widetilde{M_{i}}))$ gets assigned to $T$ satisfying its demand.
Let $b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}$ and $T\in\mathcal{U}_{b}$.
Out of $\mathcal{F}_{b}\setminus\mathcal{F}^{\boundaryOp}_{b}$ we assign $d(T)-{\mathchoice{\left|\pi_{b}^{-1}(T)\cap\mathcal{F}^{\boundaryOp}_{b}\right|}{{|\pi_{b}^{-1}(T)\cap\mathcal{F}^{\boundaryOp}_{b}|}}{{|\pi_{b}^{-1}(T)\cap\mathcal{F}^{\boundaryOp}_{b}|}}{{|\pi_{b}^{-1}(T)\cap\mathcal{F}^{\boundaryOp}_{b}|}}}$ subgraphs to $T$.
Now, consider any $F\in\pi_{b}^{-1}(T)\cap\mathcal{F}^{\boundaryOp}_{b}$.
According to \lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-edge-partitions}, \lx@cref{refnum}{item:simple-tcd-future-exact} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{item:simple-tcd-past} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcw-dp}, there is a $P\in\pastPartBaseOp_{\tau_{b}}$ with $P\subseteq E(F)$.
So, there is a $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[w\right]}{[w]}{[w]}{[w]}}\setminus{\mathchoice{\left[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(b\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}}\right]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(b\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(b\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(b\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(b)}}]}}$ with $q_{b,i}\in V(F)$.
Consider $\widetilde{F}\in\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(\widetilde{M_{i}})$, and notice that $\psi(\widetilde{F})$ gets assigned to $\pi_{b}(F)=T$.
As for each $F\in\pi_{b}^{-1}(T)\cap\mathcal{F}^{\boundaryOp}_{b}$ the corresponding $\psi(\widetilde{F})$ is different, we additionally assign ${\mathchoice{\left|\pi_{b}^{-1}(T)\cap\mathcal{F}^{\boundaryOp}_{b}\right|}{{|\pi_{b}^{-1}(T)\cap\mathcal{F}^{\boundaryOp}_{b}|}}{{|\pi_{b}^{-1}(T)\cap\mathcal{F}^{\boundaryOp}_{b}|}}{{|\pi_{b}^{-1}(T)\cap\mathcal{F}^{\boundaryOp}_{b}|}}}$ subgraphs to $T$, satisfying its demand.
Therefore the demand of all $\widetilde{\mathcal{T}^{\star}_{s}}\cup(\mathcal{X}\setminus{\mathchoice{\crossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}})\cup\bigcup_{b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}}\mathcal{U}_{b}=\mathcal{U}_{s}$ is satisfied.
Finally, consider $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}\right]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}}$ and let $\widetilde{F}\coloneqq\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(\widetilde{M_{{\mathchoice{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{s}\left(i\right)}{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{s}(i)}{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{s}(i)}{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{s}(i)}}}})$.
We assign $\psi(\widetilde{F})$ to $Q_{s,i}$ satisfying its demand.
Showing that we have a solution $(\mathcal{F},\pi)$ for $\mathscr{D}_{s,\tau}$.
\par Now, we need to show that this solution satisfies the additional requirements of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcw-dp}.
Recall that we already argued \lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-future-exact} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{item:simple-tcd-future-sensible}.
Note that $E({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})\cap\bigcup E(\mathcal{F})=E({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})\cap\bigcup E(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}})$.
Thus, by \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-witness-edge-partitions} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcd-witness} we have \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-edge-partitions} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcw-dp}.
\par Finally, consider $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[w\right]}{[w]}{[w]}{[w]}}$ and let $\mathcal{P}_{i}\coloneqq{\mathchoice{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau}\left(i\right)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau}(i)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau}(i)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau}(i)}}$.
If $\mathcal{P}_{i}=\emptyset$, by \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-witness-past} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcd-witness} there is no $\widetilde{F}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}$ with $q_{s,i}\in V(\widetilde{F})$.
As we do not add $q_{s,i}$ to any solution subgraph, this shows that there is no $F\in\mathcal{F}$ with $q_{s,i}\in V(F)$.
Otherwise, let $\widetilde{F}\coloneqq\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(\widetilde{M_{{\mathchoice{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{s}\left(i\right)}{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{s}(i)}{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{s}(i)}{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{s}(i)}}}})$.
We have $q_{s,i}\in\widetilde{F}$ and $\{\psi(\widetilde{F})\}=\pi^{-1}(Q_{s,i})$ and note that $\psi(\widetilde{F})$ is the unique $F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}$ with $q_{s,i}\in V(F^{\prime})$.
\par According to \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-witness-past} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcd-witness}, there is a partitioning of $E(\widetilde{F}-q_{s,i})$ into $\{\widetilde{E}_{P}\}_{P\in\mathcal{P}_{i}}$ such that for every $P\in\mathcal{P}_{i}$ the graph $\widetilde{F}[\widetilde{E}_{P}]$ is connected and $\widetilde{E}_{P}\cap E({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})=P$ for all $\tilde{p}\in\widetilde{Z}\cap V(\widetilde{F})$ there is exactly one $P\in\mathcal{P}_{i}$ with $\tilde{p}\in V(\widetilde{F}[\widetilde{E}_{P}])$.
Now, let $P\in\mathcal{P}_{i}$ and set the partition required by \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-past} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcw-dp} to be $E_{P}\coloneqq E(\psi(\widetilde{F}[\widetilde{E}_{P}]))$.
That is $E_{P}=(\widetilde{E}_{P}\cap E(J_{s}))\cup\bigcup_{b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}},P^{\prime}\in\pastPartBaseOp_{\tau_{b}}\cup\futurePartBaseOp_{\tau_{b}}\colon P^{\prime}\subseteq\widetilde{E}_{P}}E(H_{b,P^{\prime}})=E(\psi(\widetilde{F})[E_{P}])$.
As $\widetilde{E}_{P}\cap E({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})=P$ and as $\widetilde{F}[\widetilde{E}_{P}]$ is connected, so is $E_{P}\cap E({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})=P$ and $\psi(\widetilde{F}[\widetilde{E}_{P}])=\psi(\widetilde{F})[E_{P}]$ is connected as well, as desired.
All $\{\widetilde{E}_{P}\}_{P\in\mathcal{P}_{i}}$ are pairwise disjoint; so, are $\{E_{P}\}_{P\in\mathcal{P}_{i}}$ as well.
For each $\tilde{p}_{b,P^{\prime}}\in\widetilde{Z}\cap V(\widetilde{F})$ there is exactly one $P\in\mathcal{P}_{i}$ with $P^{\prime}\subseteq P$.
So, $\bigcup_{P\in\mathcal{P}_{i}}E_{P}=E(\psi(\widetilde{F}-q_{s,i}))=E(\psi(\widetilde{F})-q_{s,i})$ and $\{E_{P}\}_{P\in\mathcal{P}_{i}}$ is a partitioning of the edges in $\psi(\widetilde{F})-q_{s,i}$ and \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-past} as well as \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcw-dp} itself is satisfied.
\qed\end@proof
\par We have shown, that it is enough to check for each syntactically valid tuple $\tau$ whether there are $(\tau_{b})_{b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}}$ such that for all $b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}$ we have $\tau_{b}\in D(b)$ and $(\tau_{b})_{b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}}$ witnesses $\tau\in D(s)$.
Now, we consider how to check for given $(\tau_{b})_{b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}}$ in $\fpt$-time whether $(\tau_{b})_{b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}}$ witness $\tau\in D(s)$ with respect to some local to shared mappings $(\localSharedMapBaseOp_{a})_{a\in A}$ and shared mapping enumerator $\enumerateSharedOp$.
\par For this, let $C\coloneqq V(\widetilde{J_{s}})\setminus\bigcup_{t\in{\mathchoice{\thinChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\thinChildrenOp(s)}{\thinChildrenOp(s)}{\thinChildrenOp(s)}}}X_{t}$.
For all $t\in{\mathchoice{\thinChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\thinChildrenOp(s)}{\thinChildrenOp(s)}{\thinChildrenOp(s)}}$, we have that ${\mathchoice{\left|X_{t}\right|}{{|X_{t}|}}{{|X_{t}|}}{{|X_{t}|}}}=1$ and $N(X_{t})\subseteq X_{s}\cup V({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})\subseteq C$.
Thus, $C$ is a vertex cover of $\widetilde{J_{s}}$.
As $C\neq\emptyset$ whenever $V(\widetilde{J_{s}})\setminus C\neq\emptyset$, it is a fracture modulator as well.
We see that $$C=V(X_{s})\cup V({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})\cup\{q_{s,i}\}_{i\in{\mathchoice{\left[w\right]}{[w]}{[w]}{[w]}}}\cup\bigcup_{b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}}\left(V({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{b}}{\boundaryOp_{b}}{\boundaryOp_{b}}{\boundaryOp_{b}}})\cup\bigcup_{P\in\pastPartBaseOp_{\tau_{b}}\cup\futurePartBaseOp_{\tau_{b}}}V(\tilde{p}_{b,P})\right).$$ Thus, ${\mathchoice{\left|C\right|}{{|C|}}{{|C|}}{{|C|}}}\leq w+2w+w+(w+2)(2w+w)={\mathchoice{\OOp\left(w^{2}\right)}{\OOp(w^{2})}{\OOp(w^{2})}{\OOp(w^{2})}}$.
As the tree-cut decomposition is simple, for all $T\in\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{s}$ we have $T\subseteq C$ and $C$ is a fracture modulator for ${\widetilde{J}_{s}}^{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{s}}$ (i.e.,~the augmented graph of $\mathscr{C}(s,\tau,(\tau_{b})_{b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}},\enumerateSharedOp,(\localSharedMapBaseOp_{a})_{a\in A})$) as well.
So, \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:augmented-frag-fpt} allows us to find a solution to the instance in $\fpt$ time.
This motivates us to construct gadgets to simulate the additional restrictions given by \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcd-witness}.
\par We can group the requirements of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcd-witness} into five categories.
\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@\lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-witness-edge-partitions} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{item:simple-tcd-witness-past} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcd-witness} require some predefined edges and vertices not be used in the solution.
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-witness-future-sensible} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcd-witness} requires some vertices not to be part of the solution subgraph assigned to specific terminal sets.
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@\lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-witness-future-exact}, \lx@cref{refnum}{item:simple-tcd-witness-past}, \lx@cref{refnum}{item:simple-tcd-witness-marked} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{item:simple-tcd-witness-sub-connected} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcd-witness} require some vertices and edges to be part of specific solution subgraphs.
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-witness-past} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcd-witness} requires that the solution subgraphs assigned to a specific terminal set can be partitioned into edge-disjoint connected subgraphs.
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-witness-marked} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcd-witness} requires that adjacent to a vertex at most one edge is chosen in any solution.
\end{itemize}
\par Ensuring that some vertices and edges are not used in any solution subgraph is quite easy.
We can just remove them.
Ensuring that some vertices are not used in a specific solution subgraph is more difficult and we leave this for later.
\par For the remaining gadgets it is very useful to sub-divide the edges contained in $\widetilde{J_{s}}$ with two vertices.
That is for an edge $e\in E(\widetilde{J_{s}}{})$ we replace $e$ by a $4$-path $\subDivOp_{P}(e)$.
Call the newly created vertices $\subDivOp_{1}(e)$ and $\subDivOp_{2}(e)$ respectively, set $\subDivOp_{V}(e)\coloneqq\{\subDivOp_{1}(e),\subDivOp_{2}(e)\}$ to be the inner vertices of $\subDivOp_{P}(e)$, set $\subDivOp_{e}(e)\coloneqq\subDivOp_{1}(a)\subDivOp_{2}(e)$ to be the edge between $\subDivOp_{1}(e)$ and $\subDivOp_{2}(e)$, and call the modified graph $\widetilde{J_{s}}{}^{\prime}$.
Note that if ${\mathchoice{\left|C\right|}{{|C|}}{{|C|}}{{|C|}}}\geq 5$, the set $C$ stays a fracture modulator of the augmented graph.
It is easy to transfer any solution of $\widetilde{J_{s}}$ to $\widetilde{J_{s}}{}^{\prime}$.
Now consider a solution $(\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\pi^{\prime})$ on $\widetilde{J_{s}}{}^{\prime}$, for all $F\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}$ let $L_{F}\coloneqq\{e\in E(\widetilde{J_{s}}{}[C])\mid\subDivOp_{e}(e)\in E(F)\}$ be the edges $e\in E(\widetilde{J_{s}}{})$ where $F^{\prime}$ contains the middle edge of the 4-path that replaced $e$.
Note that $\phi(F)\coloneqq\widetilde{J_{s}}{}[L_{F}]$ is connected, $V(\phi(F))=V(F)\cap V(\widetilde{J}_{s})$, and for every edge $e\in E(\widetilde{J_{s}})$ there is at most one $F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}$ with $e\in E(\phi(F^{\prime}))=L_{F^{\prime}}$.
Thus, we can assign $\phi(F)$ to $\pi^{\prime}(F^{\prime})$ to obtain a solution for the instance on the host graph $\widetilde{J_{s}}$.
\par Now, consider a set of edges $D\subseteq E(\widetilde{J_{s}}{})$ and a terminal set $T\in\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{s}$.
To ensure that there is a $F\in\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(T)$ with $D\subseteq E(F)$, we reduce the demand of $T$ by one and increase set the demand of $T\cup\bigcup\subDivOp_{V}(D)$ to 1.
Call the modified terminal set $\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{s}^{\prime}$ and the modified demand function $d^{\prime}$.
Note that $C\cup\aug{T \cup\bigcup\subDivOp_V(D)}$ is a fracture modulator of the augmented graph of the modified instance.
\par\begin{lemma}There is a solution $(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}},\tilde{\pi})$ to the instance $(\widetilde{J_{s}},\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{s},d)$ such that there is a $F\in\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(T)$ with $D\subseteq E(F)$ if and only if there is a solution $(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime},\tilde{\pi}^{\prime})$ to the instance $(\widetilde{J_{s}}{}^{\prime},\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{s}^{\prime},d^{\prime})$.
The fracture number of ${\widetilde{J_{s}}{}^{\prime}}^{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{s}^{\prime}}$ is at most ${\mathchoice{\left|C\right|}{{|C|}}{{|C|}}{{|C|}}}+1$.
\end{lemma}
\@proof The solution $(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}},\tilde{\pi})$ can be transferred canonically to the instance $(\widetilde{J_{s}}{}^{\prime},\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{s}^{\prime},d^{\prime})$.
Now, consider a solution $(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime},\tilde{\pi}^{\prime})$ to the instance $(\widetilde{J_{s}}{}^{\prime},\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{s}^{\prime},d^{\prime})$.
Let $\{F\}\coloneqq\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(T\cup\bigcup\subDivOp_{V}(D))$, $e\in D$, and let $F^{\prime}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\setminus\{F\}$.
Then, $F$ contains at-least two edges of $E(\subDivOp_{P}(e))$.
Therefore $F^{\prime}$, contains at most 1 edge of $E(\subDivOp_{P}(e))$ and as $F^{\prime}$ is connected and contains a vertex apart from $\subDivOp_{V}(e)$, the graph $F^{\prime}$ does not contain the edge $\subDivOp_{e}(e)$.
So, we assign $\phi(F\cup\bigcup_{e\in D}\subDivOp_{e}(e))$ to $T$ and $\phi(F^{\prime})$ to $\tilde{\pi}^{\prime}(F^{\prime})$, obtaining a solution to the original instance with the desired additional property.
\qed\end@proof
\par A similar idea also works, to ensure that for a terminal set $T\in\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{s}$ and a set $U\subseteq V(\widetilde{J_{s}}{})$, there is a $F\in\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(T)$ with $U\subseteq V(F)$ and $D\subseteq E(F)$.
We reduce the demand of $T$ by 1 and increase the demand of $T\cup U\cup\bigcup_{e\in D}\subDivOp_{V}(e)$ by 1.
Call the modified set of terminal sets $\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime\prime}$ and the demand function $d^{\prime\prime}$.
\par\begin{corollary}There is a solution $(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}},\tilde{\pi})$ to the instance $(\widetilde{J_{s}},\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{s},d)$ such that there is a $F\in\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(T)$ with $U\subseteq V(F)$ and $D\subseteq E(F)$ if and only if there is a solution $(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime},\tilde{\pi}^{\prime\prime})$ to the instance $(\widetilde{J_{s}}{}^{\prime},\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{s}^{\prime\prime},d^{\prime\prime})$.
The fracture number of ${\widetilde{J_{s}}{}^{\prime}}^{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{s}^{\prime\prime}}$ is at most ${\mathchoice{\left|C\right|}{{|C|}}{{|C|}}{{|C|}}}+1$.
\end{corollary}
\par To model \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-witness-future-exact} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcd-witness}, we first notice that according to \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-witness-past} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcd-witness} all edges in $\bigcup\pastPartBaseOp_{\tau}$ are used by solution subgraphs containing a vertex of $\{q_{s,i}\}_{i\in{\mathchoice{\left[w\right]}{[w]}{[w]}{[w]}}}$.
Now, consider a solution $(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}},\widetilde{\pi})$ to the instance $(\widetilde{J}_{s},\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{s},d)$ such that for all $P\in\futurePartBaseOp_{\tau}$, there is a distinct $F_{P}\in\widetilde{\pi}^{-1}(R_{s,P})$ with $P\subseteq E(F_{P})$ and $E(F_{P})\cap E({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})\subseteq\bigcup\futurePartBaseOp_{\tau}$.
Then, for each $P\in\futurePartBaseOp_{\tau}$ we notice that there is no edge in $\bigcup\futurePartBaseOp_{\tau}\setminus P$ that is not contained in a $\{F_{P^{\prime}}\}_{P^{\prime}\in\in\futurePartBaseOp_{\tau}\setminus\{P\}}$.
So, we even have $E(F_{P})\cap E({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})=P$ and \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:gadget-edges-and-vertices-contained-in-specific-solution} is enough to model \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-witness-future-exact} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcd-witness} completely.
\par Also \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-witness-sub-connected} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcd-witness} can almost be modeled using \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:gadget-edges-and-vertices-contained-in-specific-solution}.
But in this case we do not know to which terminal set the solution subgraph $F\in\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}$ with $\tilde{p}_{b,P}\in V(F)$ and $P\subseteq E(F)$ gets assigned.
This is needed to apply \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:gadget-edges-and-vertices-contained-in-specific-solution}.
To remedy this situation, we check each possible simultaneous assignment of $\tilde{p}_{b,P}$ to a terminal set $\pi(F)$.
There are ${\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\left[u\right]}{[u]}{[u]}{[u]}}\setminus{\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}\left(\blockedIndex\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\left[u\right]}{[u]}{[u]}{[u]}}\setminus{\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}\left(\blockedIndex\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\left[u\right]}{[u]}{[u]}{[u]}}\setminus{\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}\left(\blockedIndex\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\left[u\right]}{[u]}{[u]}{[u]}}\setminus{\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}\left(\blockedIndex\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}}|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|\futurePartBaseOp_{\tau}\right|}{{|\futurePartBaseOp_{\tau}|}}{{|\futurePartBaseOp_{\tau}|}}{{|\futurePartBaseOp_{\tau}|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|\widetilde{\mathcal{T}^{\star}_{s}}\right|}{{|\widetilde{\mathcal{T}^{\star}_{s}}|}}{{|\widetilde{\mathcal{T}^{\star}_{s}}|}}{{|\widetilde{\mathcal{T}^{\star}_{s}}|}}}\leq u+w+2^{w}=2^{{\mathchoice{\OOp\left(w\right)}{\OOp(w)}{\OOp(w)}{\OOp(w)}}}$ different terminal sets and at most $w(w+2)$ vertices $\tilde{p}_{b,P}$.
So, there are at most $\left(2^{{\mathchoice{\OOp\left(w\right)}{\OOp(w)}{\OOp(w)}{\OOp(w)}}}\right)^{w(w+2)}=2^{{\mathchoice{\OOp\left(w^{3}\right)}{\OOp(w^{3})}{\OOp(w^{3})}{\OOp(w^{3})}}}$ different simultaneous assignments, each of which we can check using the gadget from \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:gadget-edges-and-vertices-contained-in-specific-solution}.
The fracture number of the augmented graph increases by at most $w(w+2)$; so, it still bounded by ${\mathchoice{\OOp\left(w^{2}\right)}{\OOp(w^{2})}{\OOp(w^{2})}{\OOp(w^{2})}}$ and we can decide the resulting instances in $\fpt$-time using \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:augmented-frag-fpt}.
\par Now, consider an $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[w\right]}{[w]}{[w]}{[w]}}$ with $\mathcal{P}_{i}\coloneqq{\mathchoice{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau}^{-1}\left(i\right)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau}^{-1}(i)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau}^{-1}(i)}{\pastAssignBaseOp_{\tau}^{-1}(i)}}\neq\emptyset$.
First, we assume that $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}\right]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}}$.
This ensures that we know, which solution subgraph uses $q_{s,i}$ in $(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}},\tilde{\pi})$.
Let $j\coloneqq{\mathchoice{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{s}\left(i\right)}{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{s}(i)}{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{s}(i)}{\localSharedMapBaseOp_{s}(i)}}$ and set $\{F\}\coloneqq\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(\widetilde{M_{j}})$.
Notice that $\widetilde{M_{j}}=\{q_{s,i},m_{j}\}\cup({\mathchoice{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}\left(i\right)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}(i)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}(i)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}(i)}}\cap X_{s})$.
If $(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}},\tilde{\pi})$ satisfies \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-witness-past} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcd-witness}, we can partition $E(F-q_{s,i})$ into $\{\widetilde{E}_{P}\}_{P\in\mathcal{P}_{i}}$ such that for all $P\in\mathcal{P}_{i}$ the graph $F[\widetilde{E}_{P}]=\widetilde{J_{s}}{}[\widetilde{E}_{P}]$ is connected, $\widetilde{E}_{P}\cap E({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})=P$.
\par To model this, we remove $q_{s,i}$ from $\widetilde{J_{s}}{}^{\prime}$ and remove the terminal set $\widetilde{M_{j}}$.
Then, for all $P\in\mathcal{P}_{i}$, we increase the demand of $R_{s,P}$ by 1 and require the there is a $F_{P}\in\tilde{\pi}^{\prime-1}(R_{s,P})$ with $E(F_{P})\cap E({\mathchoice{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}{\boundaryOp_{s}}})=P$.
Additionally, we require for all $x\in\widetilde{M_{j}}\setminus\{q_{s,i}\}=\{m_{j}\}\cup({\mathchoice{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}\left(i\right)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}(i)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}(i)}{\enumerateCrossLinkBaseOp_{s}(i)}}\cap X_{s})$ there is a $P\in\mathcal{P}_{i}$ with $x\in V(\widetilde{J_{s}}{}^{\prime}[F_{P}])$ which we can ensure using the same approach as for \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-witness-sub-connected} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcd-witness}.
This approach also allows us to model \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-witness-past} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcd-witness} completely for $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}\right]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}}$.
For $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[w\right]}{[w]}{[w]}{[w]}}\setminus{\mathchoice{\left[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}\right]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}]}}$, we enumerate all choices of solution subgraphs that might contain a solution subgraph using $q_{s,i}$ and then check each simultaneous choice using the same approach.
We have to watch out to not use any solution subgraph assigned to a $\{R_{s,P}\}_{P\in\futurePartBaseOp_{\tau}}$, as this would violate \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-witness-future-sensible} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcd-witness}.
Given a solution to one of these this modified instance on $\widetilde{J_{s}}{}^{\prime}$, we can construct a solution to the original instance on $\widetilde{J_{s}}$ such that \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-witness-past} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:simple-tcd-witness} is satisfied.
Each such instance has a fracture modulator of its augmented graph of size ${\mathchoice{\OOp\left(w^{2}\right)}{\OOp(w^{2})}{\OOp(w^{2})}{\OOp(w^{2})}}$ and we need to check at most $\left(2^{{\mathchoice{\OOp\left(w\right)}{\OOp(w)}{\OOp(w)}{\OOp(w)}}}\right)^{w-{\mathchoice{\demandCrossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}{\demandCrossLinkOp(s)}}}=2^{{\mathchoice{\OOp\left(w^{2}\right)}{\OOp(w^{2})}{\OOp(w^{2})}{\OOp(w^{2})}}}$ such instances, which we can do in $\fpt$-time using \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:augmented-frag-fpt}.
\par\begin{remark}We can model \lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{item:simple-tcd-witness-edge-partitions}, \lx@cref{refnum}{item:simple-tcd-witness-future-sensible}, \lx@cref{refnum}{item:simple-tcd-witness-future-exact}, \lx@cref{refnum}{item:simple-tcd-witness-past} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{item:simple-tcd-witness-sub-connected} completely using $2^{{\mathchoice{\OOp\left(w^{3}\right)}{\OOp(w^{3})}{\OOp(w^{3})}{\OOp(w^{3})}}}$ instances of the $\gstp$-problem each with fracture number of the augmented graph at most ${\mathchoice{\OOp\left(w^{2}\right)}{\OOp(w^{2})}{\OOp(w^{2})}{\OOp(w^{2})}}$.
\end{remark}
\par It remains to design a gadget that at most one edge adjacent to any $\{m_{i}\}_{i\in{\mathchoice{\left[u\right]}{[u]}{[u]}{[u]}}\setminus{\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}\left(\blockedIndex\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}}}$ is used in a solution subgraph.
The easiest solution to this is to choose for each $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[u\right]}{[u]}{[u]}{[u]}}\setminus{\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}\left(\blockedIndex\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}}$ at most one edge adjacent to $m_{i}$ that should be used in the solution subgraph $\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(\widetilde{M_{i}})$ and then remove all other edges adjacent to $m_{i}$ from the graph.
Whether there is a simultaneous choice for all $i$ can be checked using \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:gadget-edges-contained-in-specific-solution}.
\par But there is a more elegant solution that allows to simulate this restriction within in single instance.
For this, let $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[u\right]}{[u]}{[u]}{[u]}}\setminus{\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}\left(\blockedIndex\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}}$ be given and order the neighbors $N(m_{i})$ arbitrarily and call them $u_{0},u_{1},\dots,u_{k-1}$ where $k+1\coloneqq{\mathchoice{\degOp\left(m_{i}\right)}{\degOp(m_{i})}{\degOp(m_{i})}{\degOp(m_{i})}}$.
For each $j\in{\mathchoice{\left[k-1\right]_{0}}{[k-1]_{0}}{[k-1]_{0}}{[k-1]_{0}}}$, let the vertex $t_{j,0}\in\subDivOp_{V}(m_{i}u_{j})$ be adjacent to $m_{i}$ and let $t_{j,3}\in\subDivOp_{V}(m_{i}u_{j})$ be adjacent to $u_{j}$.
Connect $t_{j,0}$ to $t_{(j+1)\xmod k,0}$ with a 4-path and call the two newly created vertices $t_{j,1}$ and $t_{j,2}$ respectively where $t_{j,1}$ is adjacent to $t_{j,0}$.
Analogously, connect $t_{j,3}$ to $t_{(j+1)\xmod k,3}$ calling the new vertices $t_{j,4}$ and $t_{j,5}$ where $t_{j,4}$ is adjacent to $t_{j,3}$.
Finally, connect $t_{k-1,2}$ and $t_{k-1,5}$ with an additional edge.
This is illustrated in \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{fig:gadget-at-most-one-adjacent}.
Additionally, we add for all $j\in{\mathchoice{\left[k-1\right]_{0}}{[k-1]_{0}}{[k-1]_{0}}{[k-1]_{0}}}$ the terminal set $T^{i}_{j}\coloneqq\{t_{j,\ell}\}_{\ell\in{\mathchoice{\left[5\right]_{0}}{[5]_{0}}{[5]_{0}}{[5]_{0}}}}$ with demand one.
\par\begin{figure}\centering\includegraphics[width=207.0021pt]{gadget-at-most-one-incident.pdf}
\@@toccaption{{\lx@tag[ ]{{6}}{ The gadget ensuring that at most one edge adjacent to $m_{i}$ is used in the solution.}}}\@@caption{{\lx@tag[: ]{{Figure 6}}{ The gadget ensuring that at most one edge adjacent to $m_{i}$ is used in the solution.}}}\@add@centering\end{figure}
\par\begin{lemma}The gadget depicted in \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{fig:gadget-at-most-one-adjacent} ensures that at most one edge adjacent to $m_{i}$ is used in a solution subgraph.
The fracture number of the augmented graph containing this gadget for all $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[u\right]}{[u]}{[u]}{[u]}}\setminus{\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}\left(\blockedIndex\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}}$ is at most ${\mathchoice{\OOp\left(w^{2}\right)}{\OOp(w^{2})}{\OOp(w^{2})}{\OOp(w^{2})}}$.
\end{lemma}
\@proof First, note that any solution to the original instance where at most one edge adjacent to $m_{i}$ is used can be transferred to a solution to the instance with the gadget.
Now, consider a solution $(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime},\tilde{\pi}^{\prime})$ to the instance with the gadget.
Let $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[u\right]}{[u]}{[u]}{[u]}}\setminus{\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}\left(\blockedIndex\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}}$ and denote with $D\coloneqq\bigcup_{j\in{\mathchoice{\left[k-1\right]_{0}}{[k-1]_{0}}{[k-1]_{0}}{[k-1]_{0}}}}E(\tilde{\pi}^{\prime-1}(T^{i}_{j}))$ all edges that are used to connect the new terminal sets for the gadget of this $i$.
Set $L\coloneqq\{\subDivOp_{e}(m_{i}u_{\ell})\}_{\ell\in{\mathchoice{\left[k-1\right]_{0}}{[k-1]_{0}}{[k-1]_{0}}{[k-1]_{0}}}}\cup\{t_{k-1,2}t_{k-1,5}\}$.
This set disconnects each terminal set in $\{T^{i}_{j}\}_{j\in{\mathchoice{\left[k-1\right]_{0}}{[k-1]_{0}}{[k-1]_{0}}{[k-1]_{0}}}}$ and ${\mathchoice{\left|L\right|}{{|L|}}{{|L|}}{{|L|}}}=k+1$.
So, there is at most one edge $e^{*}\in L\setminus D$.
Denote with $\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}$ the solution subgraphs in $\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}$ that are not assigned to any $\{T^{i}_{j}\}_{i\in{\mathchoice{\left[u\right]}{[u]}{[u]}{[u]}}\setminus{\mathchoice{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}\left(\blockedIndex\right)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}{\enumerateSharedOp^{-1}(\blockedIndex)}},j\in{\mathchoice{\left[k-1\right]_{0}}{[k-1]_{0}}{[k-1]_{0}}{[k-1]_{0}}}}$.
If there is a $\ell^{*}\in{\mathchoice{\left[k-1\right]_{0}}{[k-1]_{0}}{[k-1]_{0}}{[k-1]_{0}}}$ with $e^{*}=\subDivOp_{e}(m_{i}u_{\ell^{*}})$, we claim that $\phi(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime})$ with the canonical mapping is a solution to the original instance where at most one edge adjacent to $m_{i}$ is used.
Otherwise, we have $e^{*}=t_{k-1,2}t_{k-1,5}$.
Then, we replace the edge $t_{k-1,2}t_{k-1,5}$ by $\subDivOp_{e}(m_{i}u_{0})$ in the solution subgraphs in $\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}$.
We now again claim that after replacement $\phi(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime})$ with the canonical mapping is a solution to the original instance where at most one edge adjacent to $m_{i}$ is used.
\par First, we show that after removing the edges in $D$ from the gadget, the vertices $\{u_{j}\}_{j\in{\mathchoice{\left[k-1\right]_{0}}{[k-1]_{0}}{[k-1]_{0}}{[k-1]_{0}}}}$ are disconnected inside the gadget.
Showing that all $\phi(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime})\setminus\phi(\tilde{\pi}^{\prime-1}(\widetilde{M_{i}}))$ are connected.
Consider any path $P$ between $u_{\ell}$ and $u_{\ell^{\prime}}$ where $\ell\neq\ell^{\prime}$ that only uses edges introduced by the gadget and $\bigcup_{j\in{\mathchoice{\left[k-1\right]_{0}}{[k-1]_{0}}{[k-1]_{0}}{[k-1]_{0}}}}E(\subDivOp_{P}(m_{i}u_{j}))$ such that $E(P)$ is disjoint from $D$.
Then, $P$ either contains both edges adjacent to a $p_{x,1}$ for some $x\in{\mathchoice{\left[k-1\right]_{0}}{[k-1]_{0}}{[k-1]_{0}}{[k-1]_{0}}}$ or two edge of $L$.
The first case is not possible as at least one edge adjacent to $p_{x,1}$ is contained in $D$; the second case is not possible as ${\mathchoice{\left|L\setminus D\right|}{{|L\setminus D|}}{{|L\setminus D|}}{{|L\setminus D|}}}\leq 1$.
Additionally, all $\phi(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime})\setminus\phi(\tilde{\pi}^{\prime-1}(\widetilde{M_{i}}))$ do not use an edge adjacent to $m_{i}$.
\par Now, consider $\{F\}\coloneqq\tilde{\pi}^{\prime-1}(\widetilde{M_{i}})$.
The edge $e^{*}$ is reachable from all terminals in $\widetilde{M_{i}}$ using only edges from $F$.
If $e^{*}=t_{k-1,2}t_{k-1,5}$, we also have that $t_{0,0}$ or $t_{0,3}$ as not both edges adjacent to $t_{k-1,1}$ or $t_{k-1,4}$ can be past of $F$.
So, when we replace $e^{*}$ by $t_{0,0}t_{0,3}$ in $F$, it stays connected.
Thus, $\phi(F)$ is connected in any case.
This shows that the gadget works as expected.
\par Finally, consider $C$ and notice that $m_{i}$ and all $\{u_{j}\}_{j\in{\mathchoice{\left[k-1\right]_{0}}{[k-1]_{0}}{[k-1]_{0}}{[k-1]_{0}}}}$ are contained in $C$.
So, the connected component after removing $C$ created by the gadget has at most $6(k+1)={\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\degOp\left(m_{i}\right)}{\degOp(m_{i})}{\degOp(m_{i})}{\degOp(m_{i})}}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\degOp\left(m_{i}\right)}{\degOp(m_{i})}{\degOp(m_{i})}{\degOp(m_{i})}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\degOp\left(m_{i}\right)}{\degOp(m_{i})}{\degOp(m_{i})}{\degOp(m_{i})}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\degOp\left(m_{i}\right)}{\degOp(m_{i})}{\degOp(m_{i})}{\degOp(m_{i})}})}}$ vertices.
We have that $N(m_{i})\subseteq C$. So, $6(k+1)={\mathchoice{\OOp\left(w^{2}\right)}{\OOp(w^{2})}{\OOp(w^{2})}{\OOp(w^{2})}}$ and there is a fracture modulator of size ${\mathchoice{\OOp\left(w^{2}\right)}{\OOp(w^{2})}{\OOp(w^{2})}{\OOp(w^{2})}}$.
\qed\end@proof
\par So, given $(\tau_{b})_{b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}}$, $(\localSharedMapBaseOp_{a})_{a\in A}$, and $\enumerateSharedOp$, we can decide by solving $2^{{\mathchoice{\OOp\left(w^{3}\right)}{\OOp(w^{3})}{\OOp(w^{3})}{\OOp(w^{3})}}}$ $\gstp$-instances each with fracture number of the augmented graph at most ${\mathchoice{\OOp\left(w^{2}\right)}{\OOp(w^{2})}{\OOp(w^{2})}{\OOp(w^{2})}}$, whether $(\tau_{b})_{b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}}$ witness $\tau\in D(s)$ with respect to $(\localSharedMapBaseOp_{a})_{a\in A}$ and $\enumerateSharedOp$.
As there are at most $u^{w(w+3)}=2^{{\mathchoice{\OOp\left(w^{2}\log w\right)}{\OOp(w^{2}\log w)}{\OOp(w^{2}\log w)}{\OOp(w^{2}\log w)}}}$ local shared mappings and $\left(2^{w}+(w+3)w+w+2+1\right)^{u}=2^{{\mathchoice{\OOp\left(w^{3}\right)}{\OOp(w^{3})}{\OOp(w^{3})}{\OOp(w^{3})}}}$ shared mapping enumerators, we can check whether overall $(\tau_{b})_{b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}}$ witness $\tau\in D(s)$ by solving $2^{{\mathchoice{\OOp\left(w^{3}\right)}{\OOp(w^{3})}{\OOp(w^{3})}{\OOp(w^{3})}}}$ $\gstp$-instances each with fracture number of the augmented graph at most ${\mathchoice{\OOp\left(w^{2}\right)}{\OOp(w^{2})}{\OOp(w^{2})}{\OOp(w^{2})}}$
According to \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:augmented-frag-runtime} this takes time at most $2^{2^{{\mathchoice{\OOp\left(w^{8}\right)}{\OOp(w^{8})}{\OOp(w^{8})}{\OOp(w^{8})}}}}{\mathchoice{\left|\widetilde{J}_{s}\right|}{{|\widetilde{J}_{s}|}}{{|\widetilde{J}_{s}|}}{{|\widetilde{J}_{s}|}}}$.
Combined with \lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{stmt:simple-tcd-witness-implies-dp} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{stmt:simple-tcd-dp-implies-witness} this shows that the $D(s)$ can be computed in $\fpt$-time given that for all bold children the dynamic program is calculated correctly.
\par\begin{corollary}If for all $b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}$, we have computed $D(b)$, we can compute $D(s)$ in time $2^{2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left(w^{8}\right)}{\OOp(w^{8})}{\OOp(w^{8})}{\OOp(w^{8})}}}{\mathchoice{\left|\widetilde{J}_{s}\right|}{{|\widetilde{J}_{s}|}}{{|\widetilde{J}_{s}|}}{{|\widetilde{J}_{s}|}}}$.
\end{corollary}
\par Combined with \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:simple-tcw-dp-root-correct}, we get that $\gstp$ can be solved in $\fpt$-time given a simple tree-cut decomposition of appropriate width.
\par\begin{theorem}Given a simple tree-cut $(S,\mathcal{X})$ decomposition of width $w$, we can decide whether the instance is positive in time $2^{2^{{\mathchoice{\OOp\left(w^{8}\right)}{\OOp(w^{8})}{\OOp(w^{8})}{\OOp(w^{8})}}}}{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}$.
\end{theorem}
\@proof Given the simple tree-cut composition, we compute the dynamic program for all bold nodes and output whether the dynamic program of the root is empty.
By \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:simple-tcw-dp-root-correct}, this is correct.
It remains to calculate the running time.
According to \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:simple-dp-propagation-time}, we only need to compute upper bounds on $\sum_{s\in S\colon s\text{ is bold}}{\mathchoice{\left|\widetilde{J}_{s}\right|}{{|\widetilde{J}_{s}|}}{{|\widetilde{J}_{s}|}}{{|\widetilde{J}_{s}|}}}$.
\par Let $s\in V(S)$.
Then,
\@@amsalign
As every node is a child of at most one other node, we have $\sum_{s\in S}{\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\thinChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\thinChildrenOp(s)}{\thinChildrenOp(s)}{\thinChildrenOp(s)}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\thinChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\thinChildrenOp(s)}{\thinChildrenOp(s)}{\thinChildrenOp(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\thinChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\thinChildrenOp(s)}{\thinChildrenOp(s)}{\thinChildrenOp(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\thinChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\thinChildrenOp(s)}{\thinChildrenOp(s)}{\thinChildrenOp(s)}}|}}}\leq{\mathchoice{\left|V(S)\right|}{{|V(S)|}}{{|V(S)|}}{{|V(S)|}}}\leq 2{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}$; so, $\sum_{s\in S\colon s\text{ is bold}}{\mathchoice{\left|\widetilde{J}_{s}\right|}{{|\widetilde{J}_{s}|}}{{|\widetilde{J}_{s}|}}{{|\widetilde{J}_{s}|}}}\leq{\mathchoice{\OOp\left(w^{4}{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}}\right)}{\OOp(w^{4}{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}})}{\OOp(w^{4}{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}})}{\OOp(w^{4}{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}})}}$.
Therefore, the overall running time is as claimed.
\qed\end@proof
\par\par\@@numbered@section{subsection}{toc}{\texorpdfstring{$\gstp$}{GSTP} is \texorpdfstring{$\fpt$}{FPT} by the Slim Tree-Cut Width}
In this Section, we show that $\gstp$ is $\fpt$ by the slim tree-cut width of host graph.
For this, let $\mathscr{P}\coloneqq(G,\mathcal{T},d)$ be an instance of $\gstp$ and let $\mathcal{D}=(S,\mathcal{X})$ be a nice tree-cut decomposition of $G$ of slim width $\overline{w}$ and width $w\leq\overline{w}$.
To achieve our result, we transform $\mathcal{D}$ in such a way, that it becomes simple, while not increasing its tree-cut width beyond $\overline{w}+4$.
We do this, by adding vertices and edges such that all links are bold.
Then, we apply \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:fpt-simple-tcw} to decide whether the instance is positive.
\par Now, assume that the reduction rules presented in \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{sec:tcw-red-rules} are applied exhaustively to this instance.
In particular, \lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{rr:connected-components} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{rr:adh-1} together ensure that there are no links with adhesion one in the tree-cut decomposition.
Which allows to bound the number of children of each node in $S$ by a function of $\overline{w}$.
\par\begin{lemma}Assume \lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{rr:connected-components} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{rr:adh-1} are applied exhaustively and that all empty leaves are removed.
Let $s\in V(S)$.
Then, ${\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\childrenOp\left(s\right)}{\childrenOp(s)}{\childrenOp(s)}{\childrenOp(s)}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\childrenOp\left(s\right)}{\childrenOp(s)}{\childrenOp(s)}{\childrenOp(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\childrenOp\left(s\right)}{\childrenOp(s)}{\childrenOp(s)}{\childrenOp(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\childrenOp\left(s\right)}{\childrenOp(s)}{\childrenOp(s)}{\childrenOp(s)}}|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|X_{s}\right|}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}}\leq\overline{w}$.
\end{lemma}
\@proof Consider the torso $H_{s}$ at $s$.
The 2-center of $H_{s}$ with respect to $X_{s}$ is obtained by repeatedly suppressing vertices in $H_{s}$ of degree at most $1$.
For all $c\in{\mathchoice{\childrenOp\left(s\right)}{\childrenOp(s)}{\childrenOp(s)}{\childrenOp(s)}}$, denote with $z_{c}\in V(H_{s})$ the vertex in $H_{s}$ obtained by contracting the subgraph $G[Y_{c}]$.
As \lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{rr:connected-components} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{rr:adh-1} are applied exhaustively and all empty leaves are removed, we have ${\mathchoice{\degOp_{H_{s}}\left(z_{c}\right)}{\degOp_{H_{s}}(z_{c})}{\degOp_{H_{s}}(z_{c})}{\degOp_{H_{s}}(z_{c})}}\geq 2$.
Thus, for any of them to be suppressed a vertex in $V(H_{s})\setminus(X_{s}\cup\{z_{c}\}_{c\in{\mathchoice{\childrenOp\left(s\right)}{\childrenOp(s)}{\childrenOp(s)}{\childrenOp(s)}}})=\{z_{top}\}$ has to be suppressed first.
As ${\mathchoice{\degOp_{H_{s}}\left(z_{top}\right)}{\degOp_{H_{s}}(z_{top})}{\degOp_{H_{s}}(z_{top})}{\degOp_{H_{s}}(z_{top})}}={\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(s\right)}{\adhesionOp(s)}{\adhesionOp(s)}{\adhesionOp(s)}}\geq 2$, this will not happen.
Therefore, ${\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\childrenOp\left(s\right)}{\childrenOp(s)}{\childrenOp(s)}{\childrenOp(s)}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\childrenOp\left(s\right)}{\childrenOp(s)}{\childrenOp(s)}{\childrenOp(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\childrenOp\left(s\right)}{\childrenOp(s)}{\childrenOp(s)}{\childrenOp(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\childrenOp\left(s\right)}{\childrenOp(s)}{\childrenOp(s)}{\childrenOp(s)}}|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|X_{s}\right|}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}}\leq{\mathchoice{\left|\tilde{H}^{2}_{s}\right|}{{|\tilde{H}^{2}_{s}|}}{{|\tilde{H}^{2}_{s}|}}{{|\tilde{H}^{2}_{s}|}}}\leq\overline{w}$.
\qed\end@proof
\par This allows us to apply \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:almost-simple-tcd-made-simple} to $\mathcal{D}$ to obtain an equivalent instance with a simple tree-cut decomposition $\mathcal{C}$.
Let $s\in V(S)$ and consider $\Delta_{s}={\mathchoice{\left|N_{s}\right|}{{|N_{s}|}}{{|N_{s}|}}{{|N_{s}|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|X_{s}\right|}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}}-w-1$.
We have $N_{s}\subseteq{\mathchoice{\thinChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\thinChildrenOp(s)}{\thinChildrenOp(s)}{\thinChildrenOp(s)}}$.
Thus, by \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:slim-tcw-number-children}, $\Delta_{s}\leq{\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\childrenOp\left(s\right)}{\childrenOp(s)}{\childrenOp(s)}{\childrenOp(s)}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\childrenOp\left(s\right)}{\childrenOp(s)}{\childrenOp(s)}{\childrenOp(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\childrenOp\left(s\right)}{\childrenOp(s)}{\childrenOp(s)}{\childrenOp(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\childrenOp\left(s\right)}{\childrenOp(s)}{\childrenOp(s)}{\childrenOp(s)}}|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|X_{s}\right|}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}}-w-1\leq\overline{w}-w-1$ and we obtain a simple $\mathcal{C}$ tree-cut decomposition of width $\overline{w}+4$.
\par Let $k$ be the slim tree-cut width of $G$.
There is an algorithm that computes a nice tree-cut decomposition of slim width $6(k+1)^{3}$ in time $2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left(k^{2}\log k\right)}{\OOp(k^{2}\log k)}{\OOp(k^{2}\log k)}{\OOp(k^{2}\log k)}}{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}^{4}$~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{GanianK22}{}{}]}.
So, we obtain a simple tree-cut decomposition of tree-cut width $6(k+1)^{3}+4$.
Applying \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:fpt-simple-tcw}, we know that we can decide whether $\mathscr{P}$ is positive in time $2^{2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left(k^{24}\right)}{\OOp(k^{24})}{\OOp(k^{24})}{\OOp(k^{24})}}}{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}$.
\par\begin{corollary}Let $k$ be the slim tree-cut width of $G$.
We can decide whether $\mathscr{P}$ is positive in time $\OOp^{\ast}\left(2^{2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left(k^{24}\right)}{\OOp(k^{24})}{\OOp(k^{24})}{\OOp(k^{24})}}}\right)$.
So, $\gstp$ is $\fpt$ by the slim tree-cut width of $G$.
\end{corollary}
\par\par\@@numbered@section{subsection}{toc}{\texorpdfstring{$\gstp$}{GSTP} is \texorpdfstring{$\fpt$}{FPT} by the Augmented Tree-Cut Width}
In this Section, we show that GSTP is $\fpt$ by the tree-cut width of the augmented graph.
In contrast to the last Section, we actually need to treat thin and bold links differently.
In particular, we can not assume that the number of thin children is bounded by a function of the parameter.
Therefore, we first show how to compute a friendly tree-cut decomposition from a nice tree-cut decomposition in polynomial time.
Then, we use this to show how to reduce the problem of deciding GSTP by the tree-cut width of the augmented graph to solving multiple instances of GSTP with respect to simple tree-cut decompositions.
\par\@@numbered@section{subsubsection}{toc}{Friendly Tree-Cut Decompositions}
Let $G$ be a graph and $(S,\mathcal{X})$ be a nice tree-cut decomposition of width $w$.
Ganian et~al.~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{GanianKS22}{}{}]} claimed that for all $s\in V(S)$, we now have ${\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}|}}}\leq w+1$.
To be able to use dynamic programming on the tree-cut decomposition, this is a crucial fact.
