However, this is not true and we can find counter examples showing that in fact the number of bold-children is not bounded by a function of $w$.
So, we introduce the notion of a friendly tree-cut decomposition in \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:friendly-tcw}, where we add this as an additional requirement.
\par The goal of this Section is to show, that we can compute a friendly tree-cut decomposition from a nice tree-cut decomposition in FPT-linear and quartic time while not increasing its width beyond a constant.
Then, we extend this to show that we can always find a tree-cut decomposition of the same width that is friendly in polynomial time.
We achieve this by introducing the operation of \emph{blowing up} a node $s\in S$ that reduces the number of bold children of $s$ to at most $w+2-{\mathchoice{\left|X_{s}\right|}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}}$ while increasing the width of the tree-cut decomposition to at most 4 and keeping the tree-cut decomposition nice.
Afterwards we extend this result to not increase the tree-cut width at all.
\par\begin{figure}\subfigure{0.49}
\includegraphics[width=345.0pt]{unlimited-number-of-bold-children-decomp.pdf}
\@@toccaption{{\lx@tag[ ]{{7}}{ A graph family where the bags and links of the tree-cut decomposition are indicated in blue.}}}\@@caption{{\lx@tag[: ]{{Figure 7}}{ A graph family where the bags and links of the tree-cut decomposition are indicated in blue.}}}
\subfigure{0.49}
\includegraphics[width=345.0pt]{unlimited-number-of-bold-children-torso.pdf}
\@@toccaption{{\lx@tag[ ]{{8}}{ The torso at $m$. The 3-center is the graph induced by $z_{c_{1}}$ and $z_{c_{2}}$.}}}\@@caption{{\lx@tag[: ]{{Figure 8}}{ The torso at $m$. The 3-center is the graph induced by $z_{c_{1}}$ and $z_{c_{2}}$.}}}
\@@toccaption{{\lx@tag[ ]{{9}}{ A family of graphs with tree-cut width at most 5. In the depicted nice tree-cut decomposition the node $m$ has $\ell+2$ bold children, where $\ell$ can be chosen freely.}}}\@@caption{{\lx@tag[: ]{{Figure 9}}{ A family of graphs with tree-cut width at most 5. In the depicted nice tree-cut decomposition the node $m$ has $\ell+2$ bold children, where $\ell$ can be chosen freely.}}}\end{figure}
\par First, consider the family of graphs depicted in \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{fig:unlimited-bold-children-tcw}.
In \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{fig:unlimited-bold-children-tcw-graph}, we see a nice tree-cut decomposition for the graphs in this family.
Note that $X_{m}=\emptyset$ and that $m$ has $\ell+2$ bold children.
The adhesion of each node is bounded by $5$ and the size of the 3-center of the torso of any node apart from $m$ is bounded by 4.
Now consider the torso $H_{m}$ at $m$, which is depicted in \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{fig:unlimited-bold-children-tcw-torso}.
For each $c\in{\mathchoice{\childrenOp\left(m\right)}{\childrenOp(m)}{\childrenOp(m)}{\childrenOp(m)}}$, we denote with $z_{c}$ the vertex to which the sub-tree rooted at $c$ is contracted and with $z_{top}$ the vertex to which $G-Y_{m}$ was contracted.
As ${\mathchoice{\degOp\left(z_{top}\right)}{\degOp(z_{top})}{\degOp(z_{top})}{\degOp(z_{top})}}=2$, the vertex $z_{top}$ gets suppressed, which introduces a loop at $z_{p_{\ell}}$.
Loops get removed, so now ${\mathchoice{\degOp\left(z_{p_{\ell}}\right)}{\degOp(z_{p_{\ell}})}{\degOp(z_{p_{\ell}})}{\degOp(z_{p_{\ell}})}}=2$ and $z_{p_{\ell}}$ gets suppressed, reducing the degree of $z_{p_{\ell-1}}$ to 2.
This continues until only $z_{c_{1}}$ and $z_{c_{2}}$ remain.
Therefore, the 3-center of the torso at $m$ has 2 vertices and the width of this tree-cut decomposition is 5.
\par\begin{remark}For each $\ell\in\N$, there is a graph $G$ and nice tree-cut decomposition $(S,\mathcal{X})$ of width at most 5 such that there is a node $m\in S$ with ${\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(m\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(m)}{\boldChildrenOp(m)}{\boldChildrenOp(m)}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(m\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(m)}{\boldChildrenOp(m)}{\boldChildrenOp(m)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(m\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(m)}{\boldChildrenOp(m)}{\boldChildrenOp(m)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(m\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(m)}{\boldChildrenOp(m)}{\boldChildrenOp(m)}}|}}}\geq\ell$.
\end{remark}
\par To remove these additional bold children, we aim to understand their structure better.
Let $b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}$ such that $z_{b}$ is not part of the 3-center of the torso at $s$.
We call such a $b$ \emph{fake}.
We now show, that these fake nodes more or less have to look like the fake vertices in \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{fig:unlimited-bold-children-tcw-torso}.
That is, they form a narrow path from $z_{top}$ to the 3-center.
\par\begin{lemma}Let $\ell$ be the number of fake nodes in ${\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}$.
If $\ell\geq 1$, there is an induced path $P=z_{top}p_{1}p_{2}\dots p_{\ell}$ in $H_{s}$ such that
\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@$\{p_{i}\}_{i\in{\mathchoice{\left[\ell\right]}{[\ell]}{[\ell]}{[\ell]}}}=\{z_{f}\}_{f\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(f\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(f)}{\boldChildrenOp(f)}{\boldChildrenOp(f)}};f\text{ is fake}}$,
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@the multiplicity of each edge in $P$ is at most 2,
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@there is a suppression sequence that first suppresses all thin nodes, then $z_{top}$, and finally all $p_{1},p_{2},\dots,p_{\ell}$ in order,
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@for all $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[\ell-1\right]}{[\ell-1]}{[\ell-1]}{[\ell-1]}}$, we have $N(p_{i})\subseteq V(P)$, and when $p_{i}$ gets suppressed its only neighbor is $p_{i+1}$.\end{itemize}\end{lemma}
\@proof Assume $\ell\geq 1$ and consider the graph $H^{*}$ obtained from $H_{s}$ after suppressing all vertices in $\{z_{t}\}_{t\in{\mathchoice{\thinChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\thinChildrenOp(s)}{\thinChildrenOp(s)}{\thinChildrenOp(s)}}}$.
Note that for all $b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}$, the vertex $z_{b}$ is not adjacent to any $\{z_{t}\}_{t\in{\mathchoice{\thinChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\thinChildrenOp(s)}{\thinChildrenOp(s)}{\thinChildrenOp(s)}}}$ and we have ${\mathchoice{\degOp_{H^{*}}\left(z_{b}\right)}{\degOp_{H^{*}}(z_{b})}{\degOp_{H^{*}}(z_{b})}{\degOp_{H^{*}}(z_{b})}}={\mathchoice{\degOp_{H_{s}}\left(z_{b}\right)}{\degOp_{H_{s}}(z_{b})}{\degOp_{H_{s}}(z_{b})}{\degOp_{H_{s}}(z_{b})}}$.
Fix the fake nodes $f_{1},f_{2},\dots,f_{\ell}\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}$ such that they get suppressed in this order and set $Q=q_{0}q_{1}q_{2}\dots q_{\ell}\coloneqq z_{top}z_{f_{1}}z_{f_{2}}\dots z_{f_{\ell}}$.
To show that $Q$ is a valid assignment for $P$, we inductively prove that for all $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[\ell\right]_{0}}{[\ell]_{0}}{[\ell]_{0}}{[\ell]_{0}}}$
\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@let $H_{(i)}$ be the graph obtained from $H^{*}_{s}$ after suppressing all vertices of $\bigcup_{j\in{\mathchoice{\left[i-1\right]_{0}}{[i-1]_{0}}{[i-1]_{0}}{[i-1]_{0}}}}q_{j}$, then we have $H_{(i)}=H^{*}-(\bigcup_{j\in{\mathchoice{\left[i-1\right]_{0}}{[i-1]_{0}}{[i-1]_{0}}{[i-1]_{0}}}}q_{j})$,
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@${\mathchoice{\degOp_{H_{(i)}}\left(q_{i}\right)}{\degOp_{H_{(i)}}(q_{i})}{\degOp_{H_{(i)}}(q_{i})}{\degOp_{H_{(i)}}(q_{i})}}\leq 2$,
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@if $i<\ell$, we have $N_{H_{(i)}}(q_{i})=N_{H^{*}}(\{q_{j}\}_{j\in{\mathchoice{\left[i\right]_{0}}{[i]_{0}}{[i]_{0}}{[i]_{0}}}})=\{q_{i+1}\}$.
\end{itemize}
For all $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[\ell\right]}{[\ell]}{[\ell]}{[\ell]}}$ the edges incident to $z_{f_{i}}$ are equal in $H^{*}$ and $H_{s}$; so, the claim follows.
\par As noted above, for all $b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}$, we have ${\mathchoice{\degOp_{H^{*}}\left(z_{b}\right)}{\degOp_{H^{*}}(z_{b})}{\degOp_{H^{*}}(z_{b})}{\degOp_{H^{*}}(z_{b})}}={\mathchoice{\degOp_{H_{s}}\left(z_{b}\right)}{\degOp_{H_{s}}(z_{b})}{\degOp_{H_{s}}(z_{b})}{\degOp_{H_{s}}(z_{b})}}\geq 3$.
In particular, another vertex needs to be suppressed before $z_{f_{1}}$ can be suppressed.
As no vertex of $X_{s}$ or $\{z_{b}\}_{b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}}$ can be suppressed in $H^{*}$, the only choice for this is $z_{top}=q_{0}$.
If ${\mathchoice{\degOp_{H^{*}}\left(z_{top}\right)}{\degOp_{H^{*}}(z_{top})}{\degOp_{H^{*}}(z_{top})}{\degOp_{H^{*}}(z_{top})}}\geq 3$, it will not be suppressed.
Since $z_{f_{1}}$ gets suppressed, we have ${\mathchoice{\degOp_{H^{*}}\left(z_{top}\right)}{\degOp_{H^{*}}(z_{top})}{\degOp_{H^{*}}(z_{top})}{\degOp_{H^{*}}(z_{top})}}={\mathchoice{\degOp_{H_{(0)}}\left(q_{0}\right)}{\degOp_{H_{(0)}}(q_{0})}{\degOp_{H_{(0)}}(q_{0})}{\degOp_{H_{(0)}}(q_{0})}}\leq 2$.
Note that only degrees of adjacent vertices may change upon suppression.
Thus, ${\mathchoice{\left|N_{H^{*}}(z_{top})\right|}{{|N_{H^{*}}(z_{top})|}}{{|N_{H^{*}}(z_{top})|}}{{|N_{H^{*}}(z_{top})|}}}\geq 1$.
If ${\mathchoice{\left|N_{H^{*}}(z_{top})\right|}{{|N_{H^{*}}(z_{top})|}}{{|N_{H^{*}}(z_{top})|}}{{|N_{H^{*}}(z_{top})|}}}\geq 2$, suppressing $z_{top}$ does not decrease the degree of the adjacent vertices as direct edges get inserted between them.
So, ${\mathchoice{\left|N_{H^{*}}(z_{top})\right|}{{|N_{H^{*}}(z_{top})|}}{{|N_{H^{*}}(z_{top})|}}{{|N_{H^{*}}(z_{top})|}}}=1$.
More concretely, we have $N_{H^{*}}(z_{top})=\{z_{f_{1}}\}=\{q_{1}\}$ as otherwise the degree of $z_{f_{1}}$ after suppressing $z_{top}$ is still at least 3, so it can not be suppressed next, violating our assumption.
\par Now, let $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[\ell\right]}{[\ell]}{[\ell]}{[\ell]}}$ and assume that the claim holds for $i-1$.
So, we can suppress $q_{i-1}$ in $H_{(i-1)}$.
Since ${\mathchoice{\left|N_{H_{(i-1)}}(q_{i-1})\right|}{{|N_{H_{(i-1)}}(q_{i-1})|}}{{|N_{H_{(i-1)}}(q_{i-1})|}}{{|N_{H_{(i-1)}}(q_{i-1})|}}}=1$, this equates to deleting $q_{i-1}$; so, we have $H_{(i)}=H_{(i-1)}-q_{i-1}=H^{*}-(\bigcup_{j\in{\mathchoice{\left[i-1\right]_{0}}{[i-1]_{0}}{[i-1]_{0}}{[i-1]_{0}}}}q_{j})$.
By assumption, there is a suppression sequence suppressing $z_{f_{i}}=q_{i}$ next.
Thus, ${\mathchoice{\degOp_{H_{(i)}}\left(q_{i}\right)}{\degOp_{H_{(i)}}(q_{i})}{\degOp_{H_{(i)}}(q_{i})}{\degOp_{H_{(i)}}(q_{i})}}\leq 2$.
Now assume $i<\ell$.
By the induction hypothesis, the vertices $\{q_{j}\}_{j\in{\mathchoice{\left[i-1\right]_{0}}{[i-1]_{0}}{[i-1]_{0}}{[i-1]_{0}}}}$ are not adjacent to any $\{q_{j^{\prime}}\}_{j^{\prime}\in{\mathchoice{\left[\ell\right]_{0}}{[\ell]_{0}}{[\ell]_{0}}{[\ell]_{0}}}\setminus{\mathchoice{\left[i\right]_{0}}{[i]_{0}}{[i]_{0}}{[i]_{0}}}}$ in $H^{*}$.
Thus, for all $j^{\prime}\in{\mathchoice{\left[\ell\right]_{0}}{[\ell]_{0}}{[\ell]_{0}}{[\ell]_{0}}}\setminus{\mathchoice{\left[i\right]_{0}}{[i]_{0}}{[i]_{0}}{[i]_{0}}}$, we have ${\mathchoice{\degOp_{H_{(i)}}\left(q_{j^{\prime}}\right)}{\degOp_{H_{(i)}}(q_{j^{\prime}})}{\degOp_{H_{(i)}}(q_{j^{\prime}})}{\degOp_{H_{(i)}}(q_{j^{\prime}})}}={\mathchoice{\degOp_{H^{*}}\left(q_{j^{\prime}}\right)}{\degOp_{H^{*}}(q_{j^{\prime}})}{\degOp_{H^{*}}(q_{j^{\prime}})}{\degOp_{H^{*}}(q_{j^{\prime}})}}\geq 3$ and in particular ${\mathchoice{\degOp_{H_{(i)}}\left(q_{i+1}\right)}{\degOp_{H_{(i)}}(q_{i+1})}{\degOp_{H_{(i)}}(q_{i+1})}{\degOp_{H_{(i)}}(q_{i+1})}}\geq 3$.
For $q_{i+1}$ to be suppressible after suppressing $q_{i}$, its degree must decrease, implying $N_{H_{(i)}}(q_{i})=\{q_{i+1}\}$.
Combined with $N_{H^{*}}(\{q_{j}\}_{j\in{\mathchoice{\left[i-1\right]_{0}}{[i-1]_{0}}{[i-1]_{0}}{[i-1]_{0}}}})=\{q_{i}\}$, we obtain $N_{H^{*}}(\{q_{j}\}_{j\in{\mathchoice{\left[i\right]_{0}}{[i]_{0}}{[i]_{0}}{[i]_{0}}}})=\{q_{i+1}\}$, concluding the proof.
\qed\end@proof
\par We now describe the operation of \emph{expanding} a node $s\in V(S)$.
Recall that $H_{s}$ denotes the torso at $s$.
Let $a,b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}$ be fake and denote with $m$ the multiplicity of the edge $z_{a}z_{b}$ in $H_{s}$, that is the number of edges between $Y_{a}$ and $Y_{b}$.
If $m\geq 1$ and $3\leq{\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(a\right)}{\adhesionOp(a)}{\adhesionOp(a)}{\adhesionOp(a)}}+{\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(b\right)}{\adhesionOp(b)}{\adhesionOp(b)}{\adhesionOp(b)}}-2m$, we can expand the node $s$ with respect to $a$ and $b$ by introducing a new node $c$ associated with an empty bag as a child of $s$ and moving $a$ and $b$ to be children of $c$.
Expanding $s$ until this operation is not applicable any more is called \emph{blowing up} $s$.
We now show, that these operations keep $S$ nice while not increasing the width of the decomposition beyond 4.
\par\begin{lemma}Let $(S^{\prime},\mathcal{X}^{\prime})$ be the tree-cut decomposition after expanding $s\in S$ with respect to $a$ and $b$.
Then, $(S^{\prime},\mathcal{X}^{\prime})$ is nice and its width is at most $\max(4,w)$.
\end{lemma}
\@proof For all nodes $x\in V(S)\setminus\{s\}$, the children did not change and so the niceness property holds.
To show the niceness property for $s$, we show that $c$ is a bold child, where $c$ is the newly introduced node.
Notice that $3\leq{\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(a\right)}{\adhesionOp(a)}{\adhesionOp(a)}{\adhesionOp(a)}}+{\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(b\right)}{\adhesionOp(b)}{\adhesionOp(b)}{\adhesionOp(b)}}-2m={\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\cutEdgesOp\left(Y_{a}\right)}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{a})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{a})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{a})}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\cutEdgesOp\left(Y_{a}\right)}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{a})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{a})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{a})}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\cutEdgesOp\left(Y_{a}\right)}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{a})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{a})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{a})}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\cutEdgesOp\left(Y_{a}\right)}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{a})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{a})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{a})}}|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\cutEdgesOp\left(Y_{b}\right)}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{b})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{b})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{b})}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\cutEdgesOp\left(Y_{b}\right)}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{b})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{b})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{b})}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\cutEdgesOp\left(Y_{b}\right)}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{b})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{b})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{b})}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\cutEdgesOp\left(Y_{b}\right)}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{b})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{b})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{b})}}|}}}-2m$.
As $m$ is equal to the number of edges between $Y_{a}$ and $Y_{b}$ and since $Y_{c}=Y_{a}\cup Y_{b}$, we have ${\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\cutEdgesOp\left(Y_{a}\right)}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{a})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{a})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{a})}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\cutEdgesOp\left(Y_{a}\right)}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{a})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{a})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{a})}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\cutEdgesOp\left(Y_{a}\right)}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{a})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{a})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{a})}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\cutEdgesOp\left(Y_{a}\right)}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{a})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{a})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{a})}}|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\cutEdgesOp\left(Y_{b}\right)}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{b})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{b})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{b})}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\cutEdgesOp\left(Y_{b}\right)}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{b})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{b})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{b})}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\cutEdgesOp\left(Y_{b}\right)}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{b})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{b})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{b})}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\cutEdgesOp\left(Y_{b}\right)}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{b})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{b})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{b})}}|}}}-2m={\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\cutEdgesOp\left(Y_{a}\cup Y_{b}\right)}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{a}\cup Y_{b})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{a}\cup Y_{b})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{a}\cup Y_{b})}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\cutEdgesOp\left(Y_{a}\cup Y_{b}\right)}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{a}\cup Y_{b})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{a}\cup Y_{b})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{a}\cup Y_{b})}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\cutEdgesOp\left(Y_{a}\cup Y_{b}\right)}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{a}\cup Y_{b})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{a}\cup Y_{b})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{a}\cup Y_{b})}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\cutEdgesOp\left(Y_{a}\cup Y_{b}\right)}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{a}\cup Y_{b})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{a}\cup Y_{b})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y_{a}\cup Y_{b})}}|}}}={\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(c\right)}{\adhesionOp(c)}{\adhesionOp(c)}{\adhesionOp(c)}}$.
Therefore, $c$ is a bold child of $s$ and the niceness property at $s$ is satisfied as well.
As $a$ and $b$ are bold, the niceness at $c$ is also satisfied.
\par Now, we show that the width does not increase beyond $\max(4,w)$ by considering adhesion and the 3-center of the torsos separately.
For each $x\in V(S)$ the set $Y_{x}$ does not change and so ${\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(x\right)}{\adhesionOp(x)}{\adhesionOp(x)}{\adhesionOp(x)}}$ does not change.
Notice that ${\mathchoice{\degOp_{H_{s}}\left(z_{a}\right)}{\degOp_{H_{s}}(z_{a})}{\degOp_{H_{s}}(z_{a})}{\degOp_{H_{s}}(z_{a})}}={\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(a\right)}{\adhesionOp(a)}{\adhesionOp(a)}{\adhesionOp(a)}}$ and ${\mathchoice{\degOp_{H_{s}}\left(z_{b}\right)}{\degOp_{H_{s}}(z_{b})}{\degOp_{H_{s}}(z_{b})}{\degOp_{H_{s}}(z_{b})}}={\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(b\right)}{\adhesionOp(b)}{\adhesionOp(b)}{\adhesionOp(b)}}$.
So, we know from \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:tcw-fake-nodes-form-path} that ${\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(a\right)}{\adhesionOp(a)}{\adhesionOp(a)}{\adhesionOp(a)}},{\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(b\right)}{\adhesionOp(b)}{\adhesionOp(b)}{\adhesionOp(b)}}\in\{3,4\}$.
If ${\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(a\right)}{\adhesionOp(a)}{\adhesionOp(a)}{\adhesionOp(a)}}={\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(b\right)}{\adhesionOp(b)}{\adhesionOp(b)}{\adhesionOp(b)}}=3$, as ${\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(c\right)}{\adhesionOp(c)}{\adhesionOp(c)}{\adhesionOp(c)}}={\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(a\right)}{\adhesionOp(a)}{\adhesionOp(a)}{\adhesionOp(a)}}+{\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(b\right)}{\adhesionOp(b)}{\adhesionOp(b)}{\adhesionOp(b)}}-2m$, we have ${\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(c\right)}{\adhesionOp(c)}{\adhesionOp(c)}{\adhesionOp(c)}}\leq 4$.
Otherwise at least one of $a$ or $b$ has adhesion $4$, implying $m=2$.
In this case, ${\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(c\right)}{\adhesionOp(c)}{\adhesionOp(c)}{\adhesionOp(c)}}\leq 8-4\leq 4$ as well.
\par For all $x\in V(S)\setminus\{s\}$ the torso does not change and the torso at $c$ contains at most $3\leq 4$ vertices.
Finally, consider the torso at $s$ in $S$ and $S^{\prime}$, which we denote with $H$ and $H^{\prime}$ respectively.
Observe that $H^{\prime}=H/z_{a}z_{b}$, where $z_{c}$ is the vertex in $H^{\prime}$ to which $z_{a}$ and $z_{b}$ get contracted.
Let $P\coloneqq z_{top}p_{1}p_{2}\dots p_{\ell}$ be the path of vertices associated with the fake nodes in $H$ obtained from \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:tcw-fake-nodes-form-path} and let $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[\ell\right]}{[\ell]}{[\ell]}{[\ell]}}$ be such that, without loss of generality, $p_{i}=z_{a}$ and $p_{i+1}=z_{b}$.
Observe that in $H^{\prime}$ we can also suppress the vertices associated with thin nodes, then $z_{top}$, and then $p_{1},p_{2},\dots,p_{i-2}$ in this order as these nodes are not adjacent to $z_{a}$ or $z_{b}$ in $H$.
Call this graph $H_{(i-1)}^{\prime}$ and the graph after suppressing the same vertices in $H$ by $H_{(i-1)}$.
As nodes adjacent to either $z_{a}$ or $z_{b}$ are not suppressed yet, $H_{(i-1)}^{\prime}=H_{(i-1)}/z_{a}z_{b}$ and as $N_{H_{(i-1)}}(p_{i-1})=\{p_{i}\}=\{z_{a}\}$, we have $N_{H_{(i-1)}^{\prime}}(p_{i-1})=\{z_{c}\}$.
Additionally, the multiplicity of $p_{i-1}z_{c}$ is at most 2 in $H_{(i-1)}^{\prime}$ as well.
So, $p_{i-1}$ is suppressible in $H_{(i-1)}^{\prime}$, which equates to deleting $p_{i-1}$.
The same is true for $p_{i-1}$ in $H_{(i-1)}$, where we can then suppress $p_{i}=z_{a}$, which also equates to deleting it.
Thus, the graph obtained from $H_{(i-1)}^{\prime}$ after suppressing $p_{i-1}$ is $H_{(i-1)}^{\prime}-p_{i-1}=H_{(i-1)}/z_{a}z_{b}-p_{i-1}$.
The graph obtained from $H_{(i-1)}$ after suppressing $p_{i-1}$ and $z_{a}$ is $H_{(i-1)}-p_{i-1}-z_{a}$.
As $N_{H_{(i-1)}}(z_{a})=\{p_{i-1},z_{b}\}$, these two graphs are equal.
Thus, there are suppression sequences for $H$ and $H^{\prime}$ arriving at the same graph; so, their 3-centers are equal.
\qed\end@proof
\par Now, we show that after blowing up $s$, the number of its bold children is bounded by $w+2-{\mathchoice{\left|X_{s}\right|}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}}$.
\par\begin{lemma}Denote with $(S^{\prime},\mathcal{X^{\prime}})$ the tree-cut decomposition after blowing up $s$.
Then, $(S^{\prime},\mathcal{X}^{\prime})$ is nice, has width at most $\max(4,w)$, and ${\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp_{S^{\prime}}\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp_{S^{\prime}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{S^{\prime}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{S^{\prime}}(s)}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp_{S^{\prime}}\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp_{S^{\prime}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{S^{\prime}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{S^{\prime}}(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp_{S^{\prime}}\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp_{S^{\prime}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{S^{\prime}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{S^{\prime}}(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp_{S^{\prime}}\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp_{S^{\prime}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{S^{\prime}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{S^{\prime}}(s)}}|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|X_{s}\right|}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}}\leq w+2$.
\end{lemma}
\@proof Combined with \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:tcw-expanding-upper-nice-and-width}, we only need to show, that whenever ${\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp_{S}\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp_{S}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{S}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{S}(s)}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp_{S}\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp_{S}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{S}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{S}(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp_{S}\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp_{S}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{S}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{S}(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp_{S}\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp_{S}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{S}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{S}(s)}}|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|X_{s}\right|}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}}\geq w+3$, there are two $a,b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp_{S}\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp_{S}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{S}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{S}(s)}}$ with respect to which we can expand $s$.
Let $P=z_{top}z_{c_{1}}z_{c_{2}}\dots z_{c_{\ell}}$ be the path of vertices associated with the fake nodes in $H$ obtained from \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:tcw-fake-nodes-form-path}.
As ${\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp_{S}\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp_{S}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{S}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{S}(s)}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp_{S}\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp_{S}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{S}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{S}(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp_{S}\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp_{S}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{S}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{S}(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp_{S}\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp_{S}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{S}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{S}(s)}}|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|X_{s}\right|}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}}\geq w+3$, we have $\ell\geq 3$.
For $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[\ell-1\right]}{[\ell-1]}{[\ell-1]}{[\ell-1]}}_{0}$, denote with $m_{i}$ the multiplicity of the edge $z_{c_{i}}z_{c_{i+1}}$.
If $m_{1}=1$, we claim that $s$ can be expanded with respect to the nodes $c_{1}$ and $c_{2}$; otherwise, with respect to the nodes $c_{2}$ and $c_{3}$.
Assume, that $m_{1}=1$.
Then, ${\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(c_{1}\right)}{\adhesionOp(c_{1})}{\adhesionOp(c_{1})}{\adhesionOp(c_{1})}}={\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(c_{2}\right)}{\adhesionOp(c_{2})}{\adhesionOp(c_{2})}{\adhesionOp(c_{2})}}=3$ and ${\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(c_{1}\right)}{\adhesionOp(c_{1})}{\adhesionOp(c_{1})}{\adhesionOp(c_{1})}}+{\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(c_{2}\right)}{\adhesionOp(c_{2})}{\adhesionOp(c_{2})}{\adhesionOp(c_{2})}}-2m_{1}=4\geq 3$.
If $m_{1}\neq 1$, we have $m_{1}=2$ and ${\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(c_{2}\right)}{\adhesionOp(c_{2})}{\adhesionOp(c_{2})}{\adhesionOp(c_{2})}}+{\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(c_{3}\right)}{\adhesionOp(c_{3})}{\adhesionOp(c_{3})}{\adhesionOp(c_{3})}}-2m_{2}=m_{1}+{\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(c_{3}\right)}{\adhesionOp(c_{3})}{\adhesionOp(c_{3})}{\adhesionOp(c_{3})}}-m_{2}\geq 2+1=3$, meaning that $s$ can be expanded with respect to the nodes associated with $p_{2}$ and $p_{3}$.
\qed\end@proof
\par Finally, we note that the operation of blowing up $s$ can be implemented in linear time in size of the respective torso.
Additionally, the newly introduced nodes do not need to be altered again to make the tree-cut decomposition friendly.
These facts allow us to design an algorithm running in $\fpt$-linear time.
In fact, this algorithm always runs in at most quartic time in ${\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}$.
\par\begin{theorem}Given a nice tree-cut decomposition $(S,\mathcal{D})$ of width $w$, we can compute a friendly tree-cut decomposition of width at most $\max(4,w)$ in time ${\mathchoice{\OOp\left(w{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\right)}{\OOp(w{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}{\OOp(w{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}{\OOp(w{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}}$.
\end{theorem}
\@proof In time ${\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}})}}$, we can reduce the number of nodes in ${\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}$ to $2{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}$ while not increasing its width~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{GanianKS22}{}{}]}.
We assume, without loss of generality, that this operation has already been applied.
\par We can compute all torsos in time ${\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}}+\sum_{s\in V(S)}{\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(s\right)}{\adhesionOp(s)}{\adhesionOp(s)}{\adhesionOp(s)}}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}}+\sum_{s\in V(S)}{\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(s\right)}{\adhesionOp(s)}{\adhesionOp(s)}{\adhesionOp(s)}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}}+\sum_{s\in V(S)}{\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(s\right)}{\adhesionOp(s)}{\adhesionOp(s)}{\adhesionOp(s)}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}}+\sum_{s\in V(S)}{\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(s\right)}{\adhesionOp(s)}{\adhesionOp(s)}{\adhesionOp(s)}})}}={\mathchoice{\OOp\left(w{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}}\right)}{\OOp(w{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}})}{\OOp(w{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}})}{\OOp(w{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}})}}$.
For every $s\in V(S)$, we can now compute the 3-center and hence the fake bold children in time ${\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|H_{s}\right|}{{|H_{s}|}}{{|H_{s}|}}{{|H_{s}|}}}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|H_{s}\right|}{{|H_{s}|}}{{|H_{s}|}}{{|H_{s}|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|H_{s}\right|}{{|H_{s}|}}{{|H_{s}|}}{{|H_{s}|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|H_{s}\right|}{{|H_{s}|}}{{|H_{s}|}}{{|H_{s}|}}})}}$.
As expanding $s$ with respect to any two nodes only result in these nodes being contracted and by \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:tcw-fake-nodes-form-path} these nodes have degree at most 4, we can blowup $s$ in time ${\mathchoice{\OOp\left(\ell_{s}\right)}{\OOp(\ell_{s})}{\OOp(\ell_{s})}{\OOp(\ell_{s})}}={\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|H_{s}\right|}{{|H_{s}|}}{{|H_{s}|}}{{|H_{s}|}}}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|H_{s}\right|}{{|H_{s}|}}{{|H_{s}|}}{{|H_{s}|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|H_{s}\right|}{{|H_{s}|}}{{|H_{s}|}}{{|H_{s}|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|H_{s}\right|}{{|H_{s}|}}{{|H_{s}|}}{{|H_{s}|}}})}}$ as well, where $\ell_{s}$ denotes the number of fake bold children at $s$.
The newly introduced nodes all have exactly 2 bold children and so do not need to be blown up to make the tree-cut decomposition friendly.
Therefore, the running time to make the tree-cut decomposition friendly is ${\mathchoice{\OOp\left(\sum_{s\in V(S)}{\mathchoice{\left|H_{s}\right|}{{|H_{s}|}}{{|H_{s}|}}{{|H_{s}|}}}\right)}{\OOp(\sum_{s\in V(S)}{\mathchoice{\left|H_{s}\right|}{{|H_{s}|}}{{|H_{s}|}}{{|H_{s}|}}})}{\OOp(\sum_{s\in V(S)}{\mathchoice{\left|H_{s}\right|}{{|H_{s}|}}{{|H_{s}|}}{{|H_{s}|}}})}{\OOp(\sum_{s\in V(S)}{\mathchoice{\left|H_{s}\right|}{{|H_{s}|}}{{|H_{s}|}}{{|H_{s}|}}})}}={\mathchoice{\OOp\left(\sum_{s\in V(S)}{\mathchoice{\left|V(H_{s})\right|}{{|V(H_{s})|}}{{|V(H_{s})|}}{{|V(H_{s})|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|E(H_{s})\right|}{{|E(H_{s})|}}{{|E(H_{s})|}}{{|E(H_{s})|}}}\right)}{\OOp(\sum_{s\in V(S)}{\mathchoice{\left|V(H_{s})\right|}{{|V(H_{s})|}}{{|V(H_{s})|}}{{|V(H_{s})|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|E(H_{s})\right|}{{|E(H_{s})|}}{{|E(H_{s})|}}{{|E(H_{s})|}}})}{\OOp(\sum_{s\in V(S)}{\mathchoice{\left|V(H_{s})\right|}{{|V(H_{s})|}}{{|V(H_{s})|}}{{|V(H_{s})|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|E(H_{s})\right|}{{|E(H_{s})|}}{{|E(H_{s})|}}{{|E(H_{s})|}}})}{\OOp(\sum_{s\in V(S)}{\mathchoice{\left|V(H_{s})\right|}{{|V(H_{s})|}}{{|V(H_{s})|}}{{|V(H_{s})|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|E(H_{s})\right|}{{|E(H_{s})|}}{{|E(H_{s})|}}{{|E(H_{s})|}}})}}={\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|V(S)\right|}{{|V(S)|}}{{|V(S)|}}{{|V(S)|}}}+\sum_{s\in V(S)}{\mathchoice{\left|E(H_{s})\right|}{{|E(H_{s})|}}{{|E(H_{s})|}}{{|E(H_{s})|}}}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|V(S)\right|}{{|V(S)|}}{{|V(S)|}}{{|V(S)|}}}+\sum_{s\in V(S)}{\mathchoice{\left|E(H_{s})\right|}{{|E(H_{s})|}}{{|E(H_{s})|}}{{|E(H_{s})|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|V(S)\right|}{{|V(S)|}}{{|V(S)|}}{{|V(S)|}}}+\sum_{s\in V(S)}{\mathchoice{\left|E(H_{s})\right|}{{|E(H_{s})|}}{{|E(H_{s})|}}{{|E(H_{s})|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|V(S)\right|}{{|V(S)|}}{{|V(S)|}}{{|V(S)|}}}+\sum_{s\in V(S)}{\mathchoice{\left|E(H_{s})\right|}{{|E(H_{s})|}}{{|E(H_{s})|}}{{|E(H_{s})|}}})}}$.
\par Consider $s\in V(S)$ and denote with $r_{s}\coloneqq{\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}|}}}-\ell_{s}$ the number of non-fake bold children of $s$.
As all thin children have degree at most 2 and all vertices associated with fake node children have degree at most 4.
Thus, ${\mathchoice{\left|E(H_{s})\right|}{{|E(H_{s})|}}{{|E(H_{s})|}}{{|E(H_{s})|}}}\leq 4{\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\childrenOp\left(s\right)}{\childrenOp(s)}{\childrenOp(s)}{\childrenOp(s)}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\childrenOp\left(s\right)}{\childrenOp(s)}{\childrenOp(s)}{\childrenOp(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\childrenOp\left(s\right)}{\childrenOp(s)}{\childrenOp(s)}{\childrenOp(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\childrenOp\left(s\right)}{\childrenOp(s)}{\childrenOp(s)}{\childrenOp(s)}}|}}}+(1+{\mathchoice{\left|X_{s}\right|}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}}+r_{s})^{2}$ and $\sum_{s\in V(S)}{\mathchoice{\left|E(H_{s})\right|}{{|E(H_{s})|}}{{|E(H_{s})|}}{{|E(H_{s})|}}}\leq 4{\mathchoice{\left|V(S)\right|}{{|V(S)|}}{{|V(S)|}}{{|V(S)|}}}+\sum_{s\in V(S)}(1+{\mathchoice{\left|X_{s}\right|}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}}+r_{s})^{2}$.
As $(1+{\mathchoice{\left|X_{s}\right|}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}}+r_{s})^{2}$ is convex and since for all $s\in V(S)$, we have ${\mathchoice{\left|X_{s}\right|}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}}+r_{s}\leq w$, we conclude that for fixed $\sum_{s\in V(S)}{\mathchoice{\left|X_{s}\right|}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}}+r_{s}$ the value of $\sum_{s\in V(S)}(1+{\mathchoice{\left|X_{s}\right|}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}}+r_{s})^{2}$ is maximized, when the number of $s$ with ${\mathchoice{\left|X_{s}\right|}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}}+r_{s}=w$ is maximized.
As $\sum_{s\in V(S)}{\mathchoice{\left|X_{s}\right|}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}}+r_{s}={\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}})}}$, we conclude that $\sum_{s\in V(S)}(1+{\mathchoice{\left|X_{s}\right|}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}}+r_{s})^{2}\leq{\mathchoice{\OOp\left(\frac{{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}}{w}w^{2}+{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}\right)}{\OOp(\frac{{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}}{w}w^{2}+{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}})}{\OOp(\frac{{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}}{w}w^{2}+{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}})}{\OOp(\frac{{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}}{w}w^{2}+{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}})}}={\mathchoice{\OOp\left(w{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}\right)}{\OOp(w{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}})}{\OOp(w{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}})}{\OOp(w{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}})}}$.
\qed\end@proof
\par When creating $\fpt$ algorithms parameterized by tree-cut width, we can from now on assume that the number of bold children is bounded by $w+2$, or more concretely, that ${\mathchoice{\left|X_{s}\right|}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}|}}}\leq w+2$.
If, however, the algorithms only work for constant tree-cut width or more concretely up to tree-cut width at most 3, we can not make this simplifying assumption based on the arguments presented above.
\par We now sketch how to close this gap for tree-cut decompositions of width exactly 3.
For these, we expand a node $s$ with respect to fake nodes $a,b\in{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}$ if the multiplicity of the edge $z_{a}z_{b}$ is exactly 2.
One can verify, analogously to \lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{stmt:tcw-expanding-upper-nice-and-width} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{stmt:tcw-blowing-up-number-children}, that such a pair of fake nodes always exists if ${\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|X_{s}\right|}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}}\geq w+3$, but that contrary to the result of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:tcw-expanding-upper-nice-and-width} the width of the obtained tree-cut decomposition is bounded by 3, but the newly introduced node turns out to be a thin child of $s$ and so the tree-cut decomposition is not necessarily nice anymore.
Therefore, we can not use this operation as a post-processing step after having obtained a nice tree-cut decomposition.
\par To fix this, we take a closer look at the algorithm provided by Ganian et~al.~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{GanianKS22}{}{}]} to obtain a nice tree-cut decomposition from an arbitrary tree-cut decomposition.
Their algorithm works by repeatedly considering the thin node $p$ with parent $s$ of minimum depth that is bad (i.e.,~there is a $q\in{\mathchoice{\childrenOp\left(s\right)}{\childrenOp(s)}{\childrenOp(s)}{\childrenOp(s)}}$ with $N(p)\cap Y_{q}\neq\emptyset$).
Then, $p$ gets moved to be included in the sub-tree rooted at $q$.
The position to which $p$ gets moved is chosen in such a way as to ensure that the width of the tree-cut decomposition does not increase and that after at most $2{\mathchoice{\left|V(S)\right|}{{|V(S)|}}{{|V(S)|}}{{|V(S)|}}}$ many moving operations, the tree-cut decomposition is nice.
Notice that whenever at depth $t$ there is no bad node anymore, this fact is maintained throughout the algorithm.
\par The operation of expanding a node in a tree-cut decomposition of width 3 only introduces a bad node at a larger depth than at the expanded node.
Therefore, we consider the tree-cut decomposition depth layer by depth layer.
If at a depth layer, there is a bad node $p$, we apply the moving procedure described by Ganian et~al.~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{GanianKS22}{}{}]}.
If at a depth layer, there is no bad node, but a node $s$ with ${\mathchoice{\left|X_{s}\right|}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}|}}}\geq w+3$, we blow up $s$ using the modified expansion operation.
This ensures that after a layer is processed, there are no bad nodes on this or a lower layer and all such nodes $s$ have ${\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|X_{s}\right|}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}}\leq w+2$.
Additionally, per depth layer we apply at most $3{\mathchoice{\left|V(S^{\prime})\right|}{{|V(S^{\prime})|}}{{|V(S^{\prime})|}}{{|V(S^{\prime})|}}}$ many operations, where $S^{\prime}$ denotes the tree-cut decomposition when beginning to process the respective depth layer.
As all internal empty nodes have at least two children and we remove all empty leaves, the number of nodes in $S$ is bounded by $2{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}$~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{GanianKS22}{}{}]}.
Thus, we do at most $6{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}^{2}$ operations.
\par\begin{corollary}Given a nice tree-cut decomposition of width $w$, we can compute a friendly tree-cut decomposition of width at most $w$ in polynomial time.
\end{corollary}
\par\par\@@numbered@section{subsubsection}{toc}{Reducing \texorpdfstring{$\gstp$}{GSTP} by Augmented Tree-Cut Width to \texorpdfstring{$\gstp$}{GSTP} with a Simple Tree-Cut Decomposition}
In this Section, we present some reduction rules that, taken together, are enough to remove all nodes in the tree-cut decomposition that violate the condition for a simple tree-cut decomposition.
Some of the reduction rules have as a precondition that a specific sub-instance is positive.
So, they are not reduction rules in the classical sense, as they can not necessarily be applied in polynomial but rather only in $\fpt$-time.
\par For this Section, assume that $\mathcal{D}\coloneqq(S,\mathcal{X})$ is a friendly tree-cut decomposition of width $w$ for ${G}^{\mathcal{T}}$.
Set $X_{s}^{\prime}\coloneqq X_{s}\cap V(G)$ and let $\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\coloneqq\{X^{\prime}_{s}\}_{s\in V(S)}$ for all $s\in S$.
Then, $\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\coloneqq(S,\mathcal{X}^{\prime})$ is a tree-cut decomposition of $G$ with width at most $w$.
When we refer to a reduction rule presented in \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{sec:tcw-red-rules}, namely \lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{rr:connected-components}, \lx@cref{refnum}{rr:cross-link-demand-large} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{rr:adh-1}, we mean that they are applied with respect to $\mathcal{D}^{\prime}$ while keeping $\mathcal{D}$ in sync.
These reduction rules already bring us quite close to $\mathcal{D}^{\prime}$ being simple with respect to the nodes that are thin in $\mathcal{D}$.
\par\begin{lemma}After exhaustively applying \lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{rr:sensible-terminal-sets}, \lx@cref{refnum}{rr:connected-components} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{rr:adh-1} with respect to $\mathcal{D}^{\prime}$ and removing nodes that are empty in $\mathcal{D}$ and $\mathcal{D}^{\prime}$, we have for all $s\in V(S)\setminus\{r\}$ that are thin in $\mathcal{D}$ that ${\mathchoice{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}\left(s\right)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}}=2$ and ${\mathchoice{\crossLinkOp_{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}\left(s\right)}{\crossLinkOp_{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}(s)}{\crossLinkOp_{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}(s)}{\crossLinkOp_{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}(s)}}=\emptyset$.
In particular, ${\mathchoice{\cutEdgesOp_{{G}^{\mathcal{T}}}\left(Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s}\right)}{\cutEdgesOp_{{G}^{\mathcal{T}}}(Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s})}{\cutEdgesOp_{{G}^{\mathcal{T}}}(Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s})}{\cutEdgesOp_{{G}^{\mathcal{T}}}(Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s})}}$ does not contain an augmented and a non-augmented edge.
Additionally, the tree-cut decompositions can be maintained efficiently while not increasing the width of $\mathcal{D}$ and keeping $\mathcal{D}$ friendly if it was friendly before.
\end{lemma}
\@proof Let $s\in V(S)\setminus\{r\}$ be thin in $\mathcal{D}$.
Assume ${\mathchoice{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}\left(s\right)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}}<2$.
If ${\mathchoice{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}\left(s\right)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}}=0$, either $Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s}\supseteq Y^{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}_{s}$ is empty, which means that this node was removed, or $G$ is disconnected, which means that \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{rr:connected-components} would split this instance.
New let ${\mathchoice{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}\left(s\right)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}}=1$ and set $\{uv\}={\mathchoice{\cutEdgesOp\left(Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s}\right)}{\cutEdgesOp(Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s})}{\cutEdgesOp(Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s})}}$.
If $uv\in E(G)$, then ${\mathchoice{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}\left(s\right)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}(s)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}(s)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}(s)}}=1$ and \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{rr:adh-1} would remove $uv$.
So, $uv\notin E(G)$ and $uv$ is an augmented edge.
Let $T$ be the terminal set inducing $uv$.
As ${\mathchoice{\cutEdgesOp_{G}\left(Y_{s}^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}\right)}{\cutEdgesOp_{G}(Y_{s}^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}})}{\cutEdgesOp_{G}(Y_{s}^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}})}{\cutEdgesOp_{G}(Y_{s}^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}})}}$ is empty, either $Y^{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}_{s}$ or $V(G)\setminus Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}$ is empty or $G$ would be disconnected, which would violate that \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{rr:connected-components} has been applied exhaustively.
Thus, ${\mathchoice{\left|T\right|}{{|T|}}{{|T|}}{{|T|}}}\leq 1$ and $T$ should have been removed by \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{rr:sensible-terminal-sets} and we have ${\mathchoice{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}\left(s\right)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}}=2$.
\par Now, assume that there is a $T\in{\mathchoice{\crossLinkOp_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}\left(s\right)}{\crossLinkOp_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}(s)}{\crossLinkOp_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}(s)}{\crossLinkOp_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}(s)}}$.
This means that at least one edge of ${\mathchoice{\cutEdgesOp_{{G}^{\mathcal{T}}}\left(Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s}\right)}{\cutEdgesOp_{{G}^{\mathcal{T}}}(Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s})}{\cutEdgesOp_{{G}^{\mathcal{T}}}(Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s})}{\cutEdgesOp_{{G}^{\mathcal{T}}}(Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s})}}$ is augmented.
If both are augmented, either $Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}$ or $V(G)\setminus Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}$ is empty.
As $T\subseteq V(G)$, this means that either $T\cap Y_{s}^{\mathcal{D}}\neq\emptyset$ or $T\setminus Y_{s}^{\mathcal{D}}\neq\emptyset$ is empty, violating our assumption.
Thus, exactly one edge of ${\mathchoice{\cutEdgesOp_{{G}^{\mathcal{T}}}\left(Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s}\right)}{\cutEdgesOp_{{G}^{\mathcal{T}}}(Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s})}{\cutEdgesOp_{{G}^{\mathcal{T}}}(Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s})}{\cutEdgesOp_{{G}^{\mathcal{T}}}(Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s})}}$ is augmented and so ${\mathchoice{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}\left(s\right)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}(s)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}(s)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}(s)}}=1$, which means that \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{rr:adh-1} was applicable.
\par The only reduction rule, that might be hard to efficiently maintain and might increase the width of $\mathcal{D}$ is \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{rr:adh-1} in the case where the terminal sets are modified.
This case only applies when ${\mathchoice{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}\left(s\right)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}(s)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}(s)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}(s)}}=1$ and there is a $T\in\mathcal{T}$ with $T\cap Y_{s}^{\mathcal{D}}\neq\emptyset$ and $T\setminus Y_{s}^{\mathcal{D}}\neq\emptyset$.
Thus, ${\mathchoice{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}\left(s\right)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}}=2$.
Let $\{uv\}\coloneqq{\mathchoice{\cutEdgesOp_{G}\left(Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}\right)}{\cutEdgesOp_{G}(Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s})}{\cutEdgesOp_{G}(Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s})}{\cutEdgesOp_{G}(Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s})}}$ and $\{uv,xy\}\coloneqq{\mathchoice{\cutEdgesOp_{{G}^{\mathcal{T}}}\left(Y_{s}^{\mathcal{D}}\right)}{\cutEdgesOp_{{G}^{\mathcal{T}}}(Y_{s}^{\mathcal{D}})}{\cutEdgesOp_{{G}^{\mathcal{T}}}(Y_{s}^{\mathcal{D}})}{\cutEdgesOp_{{G}^{\mathcal{T}}}(Y_{s}^{\mathcal{D}})}}$ and, without loss of generality, assume $u\in Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}$, $v\notin Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}$, and $x=\aug{T}$.
Additionally, assume $x\in Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s}$, the case $x\notin Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s}$ follows analogously.
The augmented graph after removing $uv$ and $T$ is $G^{*}\coloneqq{G}^{\mathcal{T}}-uv-xy$.
\par Let $A$ be the component of $G^{*}$ containing $u$.
If before the reduction rule applied, we already had $(T\cap Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s})\cup\{u\}$ as a terminal set, $A={G}^{\mathcal{T}}[Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s}]$; so, a tree-cut decomposition of appropriate width and friendliness for this decomposition is $(S_{s},\{X_{s^{\prime}}\setminus\{u\}\mid s^{\prime}\in S_{s}\})$.
Now, assume that the terminal set $(T\cap Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s})\cup\{u\}$ was newly introduced.
Then, $A={G}^{\mathcal{T}}[Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s}]+xu$, where we identify $u$ with the augmented vertex of $(T\cap Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s})\cup\{u\}$.
Consider the tree-cut decomposition $\mathcal{D}_{A}\coloneqq(S_{s},\{X_{s^{\prime}}\}_{s^{\prime}\in S_{s}})$.
Note that $\mathcal{D}_{A}$ is friendly if $\mathcal{D}$ is friendly.
Let $s\in V(S_{s})\setminus\{s\}$.
We have $xy\in{\mathchoice{\cutEdgesOp_{{G}^{\mathcal{T}}}\left(Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s}\right)}{\cutEdgesOp_{{G}^{\mathcal{T}}}(Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s})}{\cutEdgesOp_{{G}^{\mathcal{T}}}(Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s})}{\cutEdgesOp_{{G}^{\mathcal{T}}}(Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s})}}$ if and only if $xu\in{\mathchoice{\cutEdgesOp_{A}\left(Y^{\mathcal{D}_{A}}_{s}\right)}{\cutEdgesOp_{A}(Y^{\mathcal{D}_{A}}_{s})}{\cutEdgesOp_{A}(Y^{\mathcal{D}_{A}}_{s})}{\cutEdgesOp_{A}(Y^{\mathcal{D}_{A}}_{s})}}$.
So, ${\mathchoice{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}\left(s\right)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}}={\mathchoice{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}_{A}}\left(s\right)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}_{A}}(s)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}_{A}}(s)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}_{A}}(s)}}$.
Additionally, the torso at $s$ does not change between $\mathcal{D}$ and $\mathcal{D}_{A}$.
Lastly, denote with $H_{s}$ and $H^{\prime}_{s}$ the torsos at $s$ with respect to $\mathcal{D}$ and $\mathcal{D}_{A}$, respectively.
If $x\in X_{s}$, $H^{\prime}_{s}=H_{s}-z_{top}+xu$, so the 3-center does not increase as we only introduce an edge between center vertices.
If $x\notin X_{s}$, denote with $z$ the vertex of $H_{s}$ whose associated sub-tree contains $x$.
Then, $H^{\prime}_{s}=H_{s}-z_{top}+uz$.
As ${\mathchoice{\degOp_{H_{s}}\left(z_{top}\right)}{\degOp_{H_{s}}(z_{top})}{\degOp_{H_{s}}(z_{top})}{\degOp_{H_{s}}(z_{top})}}={\mathchoice{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}\left(s\right)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}}=2$, the vertex $z_{top}$ is suppressible.
We have $N_{H_{s}}(z_{top})=\{u,z\}$.
Thus, $H^{\prime}_{s}$ is equal to $H_{s}$ after suppressing $z_{top}$ and their 3-centers are equal.
\par Let $B$ be the component of $G^{*}$ containing $v$.
If before the reduction rule applied, we already had $(T\setminus Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s})\cup\{v\}$ as a terminal set, $B={G}^{\mathcal{T}}[V({G}^{\mathcal{T}})\setminus Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s}]$; so, a tree-cut decomposition of appropriate width and friendliness for this decomposition is $(S-S_{s},\{X_{s^{\prime}}\}_{s^{\prime}\in V(S)-V(S_{s})})$.
Now, assume that the terminal set $(T\setminus Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s})\cup\{v\}$ was newly introduced.
Then, $B={G}^{\mathcal{T}}/Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s}$ and let $t$ be the augmented vertex of $(T\setminus Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s})\cup\{v\}$.
Denote with $p$ the parent of $s$ in $S$ and consider the tree-cut decomposition $\mathcal{D}_{B}$ induced by $S-S_{s}$, where we add a node $s^{\prime}$ with $X_{s^{\prime}}\coloneqq\{t\}$ as a child of $p$.
Note, that $\mathcal{D}_{B}$ is friendly if $\mathcal{D}$ was friendly and that the 3-center of the torso at $s^{\prime}$ is empty.
As above, the adhesion does not increase and the remaining torsos also do not change between $\mathcal{D}$ and $\mathcal{D}_{B}$.
To obtain a tree-cut decomposition for the whole graph, set $\mathcal{D}_{A}$ as a child of the root of $\mathcal{D}_{B}$.
\qed\end@proof
\par There are two task remaining for this Section.
First, we need to ensure for all thin nodes $s\in V(S)\setminus\{r\}$ that ${\mathchoice{\left|Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}\right|}{{|Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}|}}{{|Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}|}}{{|Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}|}}}\leq 1$.
We call nodes $s\in V(S)\setminus\{r\}$ that are thin in $\mathcal{D}$, but have ${\mathchoice{\left|Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}\right|}{{|Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}|}}{{|Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}|}}{{|Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}|}}}\geq 2$ \emph{cluttered}.
Second, we need to take care of all nodes that are bold in $\mathcal{D}$, but thin in $\mathcal{D}^{\prime}$.
The second task can be taken care of rather quickly by applying \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:almost-simple-tcd-made-simple}.
As $\mathcal{D}$ is friendly, for each $s\in V(S)$ the number of such children is bounded by the number of bold children, or more concretely, $w+2-{\mathchoice{\left|X_{s}\right|}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}}$.
Thus, we can just treat them like bold children.
\par\begin{lemma}Assume $\mathcal{D}$ is friendly, has no cluttered nodes, and that \lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{rr:sensible-terminal-sets}, \lx@cref{refnum}{rr:connected-components} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{rr:adh-1} were applied exhaustively.
We can compute in linear time an equivalent instance $(G^{\prime},\mathcal{T},d)$ and a simple tree-cut decomposition $\mathcal{C}$ of $G^{\prime}$ of width at most $w+5$.
\end{lemma}
\@proof To obtain $G^{\prime}$ and $\mathcal{C}$, we apply \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:almost-simple-tcd-made-simple} to $G$ and $\mathcal{D}^{\prime}$, where we remove all empty leaves.
It remains to bound the tree-cut width of $\mathcal{C}$.
Let $s\in V(S)$, we now need to bound $\Delta_{s}={\mathchoice{\left|N_{s}\right|}{{|N_{s}|}}{{|N_{s}|}}{{|N_{s}|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}(s)}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}(s)}}|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|X_{s}\right|}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}}-w-1$.
For this, we show that any $c\in{\mathchoice{\thinChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}}\left(s\right)}{\thinChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}{\thinChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}{\thinChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}}$ is simple in $\mathcal{D}^{\prime}$.
As $c$ is not cluttered, we have ${\mathchoice{\left|Y^{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}\right|}{{|Y^{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}|}}{{|Y^{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}|}}{{|Y^{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}|}}}\leq 1$ and since we remove empty leaves, we even have ${\mathchoice{\left|Y^{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}_{s}\right|}{{|Y^{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}_{s}|}}{{|Y^{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}_{s}|}}{{|Y^{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}_{s}|}}}=1$.
By \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:general-tcw-rr-almost-simple}, we have that ${\mathchoice{\adhesionOp\left(c\right)}{\adhesionOp(c)}{\adhesionOp(c)}{\adhesionOp(c)}}=2$ and ${\mathchoice{\crossLinkOp\left(c\right)}{\crossLinkOp(c)}{\crossLinkOp(c)}{\crossLinkOp(c)}}=\emptyset$, yielding that $c$ is simple.
Thus, $N_{s}\subseteq{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}}\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}}$ and, in particular, $N_{s}\cup{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}(s)}}\subseteq{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}}\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}{\boldChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}}$.
Combined with the fact that $\mathcal{D}$ is friendly, we get $\Delta_{s}\leq 1$ and the width of $\mathcal{C}$ is bounded by $w+5$.
\qed\end@proof
\par To tackle the cluttered nodes, we solve sub-instances of $\gstp$.
The reduction rules we present now, are no reduction rules in the classical sense (i.e.,~they do not necessarily run in polynomial time), but rather recursion rules.
We later show, how to apply these rules in a way, that we only solve simple sub-instances and we mostly preserves the running time obtained in \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{sec:tcw-simple}.
\par The crux of why this problem is $\fpt$ by the tree-cut width of the augmented graph, but $\wonehard$ by the tree-cut width of the host-graph~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{GanianOR20}{}{}]} lies in the fact, that for a cluttered node $s\in V(S)$, we have for all $T\in\mathcal{T}$ that either $T\cap Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}$ or $T\setminus Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}$ is empty.
This means that no terminal set crosses $Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}$.
Therefore, we can mostly disregard how the instance looks on $V(G)\setminus Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}$ for deciding how terminal set contained in $Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}$ are solved in a solution of the whole instance.
Let $u,x\in Y_{s}$ and $v,y\in V(G)\setminus Y_{s}$ be such, that $\{uv,xy\}={\mathchoice{\cutEdgesOp_{{G}^{\mathcal{T}}}\left(Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s}\right)}{\cutEdgesOp_{{G}^{\mathcal{T}}}(Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s})}{\cutEdgesOp_{{G}^{\mathcal{T}}}(Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s})}{\cutEdgesOp_{{G}^{\mathcal{T}}}(Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s})}}$, and let $\mathcal{U}\coloneqq\{T\in\mathcal{T}\mid T\subseteq Y_{s}\}$ be those terminal sets contained in $Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}$.
Note that $uv,xy\in E(G)$.
First, we consider the case, where we can satisfy the requirements of $\mathcal{U}$, while supplying an additional connection for $vy$ to $V(G)\setminus Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}$, while solving the requirements of $\mathcal{U}$.
\par\begin{reductionrule}Consider the instance $\mathscr{X}_{s}\coloneqq(G[Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}],\mathcal{U}\cup\{u,x\},d^{\prime})$ where $d^{\prime}$ is $d|_{\mathcal{U}\cup\{u,x\}}$ increased by one for the argument $\{u,x\}$.
If $\mathscr{X}_{s}$ is positive, remove all $\mathcal{U}$ from $\mathcal{T}$ and contract $Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}$ in the original instance $\mathscr{P}$.
\end{reductionrule}
\@proof Let $(\mathcal{F},\pi)$ be a solution for the original instance and denote with $\mathcal{Z}\coloneqq\pi^{-1}(\mathcal{T}\setminus\mathcal{U})$ all trees that are assigned to a terminal set whose vertices are not contained in $Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}\cap V(G)$.
Denote with $h$ the vertex to which $Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}$ gets contracted.
Consider any $Z\in\mathcal{Z}$, if $uv\in E(Z)$, replace $Z$ by $(Z-uv)+hv$ and if $xy\in E(Z)$, replace $Z$ by $(Z-xy)+hy$.
Denote with $\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}$ the set of all $\mathcal{Z}$ after applying the transformation.
Notice that all of them are connected, pairwise edge-disjoint, and contained in the reduced instance.
Assigning $Z\in\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}$ to the same terminal set as the original subgraphs yields a solution for the reduced instance.
\par Now, let $(\mathcal{F},\pi)$ be a solution for the reduced instance and $(\mathcal{H},\rho)$ be a solution to $\mathscr{X}_{s}$.
Without loss of generality, we assume that for all $T\in\mathcal{T}\setminus\mathcal{U}$, we have that all leaves of all trees in $\pi^{-1}(T)$ are contained in $T$.
Denote with $h$ the vertex to which $Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}$ gets contracted.
As $h$ is not contained in any terminal set and ${\mathchoice{\degOp\left(h\right)}{\degOp(h)}{\degOp(h)}{\degOp(h)}}=2$, there is at most one $F\in\mathcal{F}$ with $h\in V(F)$.
Let $P\in\rho^{-1}(\{u,x\})$ and set $F^{*}\coloneqq((F-h)+uv+xy)\cup P$.
Notice that $V(F^{*})\supseteq\pi(F)$, that $F^{*}$ is connected, and that edge-disjoint from all $\mathcal{H}\setminus P$ and $\mathcal{F}\setminus F$.
A solution for the original instance can be obtained by assigning all $F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}\setminus F$ to $\pi(F)$, all $H\in\mathcal{H}\setminus P$ to $\rho(H)$, and $F^{*}$ to $\pi(F)$.
\qed\end@proof
\par If \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{rr:tcw-thin-supply} is not applicable, we know that we cannot use $uv$ and $xy$ in a tree for terminals contained in $V(G)\setminus Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}$.
So, we check, whether the terminal sets $\mathcal{U}$ can be solved only using edges of $G[Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}]$.
\par\begin{reductionrule}Assume that \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{rr:tcw-thin-supply} is not applicable to $s$.
Consider the instance $\mathscr{Y}_{s}\coloneqq(G[Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}],\mathcal{U},d|_{\mathcal{U}})$.
If $\mathscr{Y}_{s}$ is positive, remove $\mathcal{U}$ from $\mathcal{T}$ and $Y_{s}^{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}$ from $G$ in the original instance $\mathscr{P}$.
\end{reductionrule}
\@proof Let $(\mathcal{F},\pi)$ be a solution for the original instance and denote with $\mathcal{Z}\coloneqq\pi^{-1}(\mathcal{T}\setminus\mathcal{U})$ all trees that are assigned to a terminal set whose vertices are not contained in $Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}\cap V(G)$.
Since $\mathscr{X}_{s}$ is negative, for all $Z\in\mathcal{Z}$ the set of vertices $V(Z)$ is disjoint from $Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}$.
So, $(\mathcal{Z},\pi|_{\mathcal{Z}})$ is a solution for the reduced instance.
\par Now, let $(\mathcal{F},\pi)$ be a solution for the reduced instance and $(\mathcal{H},\rho)$ be a solution to $\mathscr{Y}_{s}$.
Let $\mathcal{J}\coloneqq\mathcal{F}\cup\mathcal{H}$ be a set of edge-disjoint connected subgraphs of $G$ and denote with $\tau\colon\mathcal{J}\to\mathcal{T}$ the function satisfying $\tau|_{\mathcal{F}}=\pi$ and $\tau|_{\mathcal{H}}=\rho$.
Then, $(\mathcal{J},\tau)$ is a solution for the original instance.
\qed\end@proof
\par Finally, we need to take care of the case, where the terminal sets $\mathcal{U}$ cannot be solved using only edges of $G[Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}]$.
\par\begin{reductionrule}Assume both \lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{rr:tcw-thin-supply} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{rr:tcw-thin-independent} are not applicable to $s$.
Consider the instance $\mathscr{Z}_{s}\coloneqq(G/(V(G)\setminus Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}),\mathcal{U},d|_{\mathcal{U}})$.
If $\mathscr{Z}_{s}$ is positive, we remove $\mathcal{U}$ from $\mathcal{T}$, $Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}$ from $G$, and add 1 demand to the terminal set $\{v,y\}$ in the remaining instance.
Otherwise, output a trivial negative instance.
\end{reductionrule}
\@proof Let $(\mathcal{F},\pi)$ be a solution for the original instance and denote with $\mathcal{H}\coloneqq\pi^{-1}(\mathcal{U})$ all trees that are assigned to a terminal set whose vertices are contained in $Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}\cap V(G)$.
Denote with $z$ the vertex of $G/(V(G)\setminus Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s})$ to which the vertices $V(G)\setminus Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}$ are contracted.
For all subgraphs $H\in\mathcal{H}$, if $uv\in E(H)$ replace $H$ by $(H-uv)+uz$, and if $xy\in E(Y)$ replace $H$ by $(H-xy)+xz$.
Denote with $\mathcal{H^{\prime}}$ the set of all $\mathcal{H}$ after applying the modification.
Notice that they are connected and pairwise edge-disjoint, and contained in $G/(V(G)\setminus Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s})$.
Assigning each subgraph of $\mathcal{H}^{\prime}$ to the original terminal pair, we obtain a solution for $\mathscr{Z}_{s}$.
So, rejecting the instance if $\mathscr{Z}_{s}$ is negative is correct.
\par Additionally, since $\mathscr{Y}_{s}$ is negative, we know that there is a $H\in\mathcal{H}$ with $V(H)\setminus Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}\neq\emptyset$.
In particular, $\{uv,xy\}\subseteq E(H)$.
Let $P\coloneqq H-Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}$ be the part of $H$ outside $Y_{s}$ and let $\mathcal{J}\coloneqq\pi^{-1}(\mathcal{T}\setminus\mathcal{U})$.
Note that $P$ is connected, edge-disjoint from all $\mathcal{J}$, contained in the reduced instance and $V(P)\supseteq\{v,y\}$.
To obtain a solution for the reduced graph, we assign all subgraphs in $\mathcal{J}$ to their respective terminal set and $P$ to $\{v,y\}$.
\par Now, let $(\mathcal{F},\pi)$ be a solution for the reduced instance and $(\mathcal{H},\rho)$ be a solution to $\mathscr{Z}_{s}$.
Since $\mathscr{Y}_{s}$ is negative, there is a unique $H\in\mathcal{H}$ such that $V(H)\setminus Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}\neq\emptyset$.
Let $z$ denote the graph to which $V(G)\setminus Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}$ are contracted in the host-graph of $\mathscr{Z}_{s}$.
Then, $\{uz,xz\}\subseteq E(H)$.
Let $P\in\pi^{-1}(\{v,y\})$ and denote with $H^{*}\coloneqq((H-z)+uv+xy)\cup P$.
Notice that $V(H^{*})\supseteq\rho(H)$, that $H^{*}$ is connected, and that $H^{*}$ is edge-disjoint from all subgraphs in $\mathcal{H}\setminus H$ and $\mathcal{F}\setminus P$.
A solution for the original instance can be obtained by assigning all $F\in\mathcal{F}\setminus P$ to $\pi(F)$, all $H^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}\setminus H$ to $\rho(H^{\prime})$, and $H^{*}$ to $\rho(H)$.
\qed\end@proof
\par Additionally, the tree-cut width of the augmented graphs of $\mathscr{X}_{s},\mathscr{Y}_{s}$, and $\mathscr{Z}_{s}$ is bounded by ${\mathchoice{\tcwOp\left({G}^{\mathcal{T}}\right)}{\tcwOp({G}^{\mathcal{T}})}{\tcwOp({G}^{\mathcal{T}})}{\tcwOp({G}^{\mathcal{T}})}}$ and applying any of the previous three reduction rules does not increase the tree-cut width of the augmented graph.
\par\begin{lemma}We can obtain a tree-cut decomposition for the augmented graphs of $\mathscr{X}_{s}$, $\mathscr{Y}_{s}$, and $\mathscr{Z}_{s}$ of at most the same width as the tree-cut decomposition of ${G}^{\mathcal{T}}$ in linear time.
Additionally, we can obtain tree-cut decomposition of the augmented graphs after applying any of \lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{rr:tcw-thin-supply}, \lx@cref{refnum}{rr:tcw-thin-independent} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{rr:tcw-thin-demand} in linear time as well, while not increasing their width.
\end{lemma}
\@proof Let $H\coloneqq{G}^{\mathcal{T}}/(V(G)\setminus Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s})$ be the augmented graph of the instance with all but the vertices in $Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}$ contracted.
Notice that $H$ is the augmented graph of $\mathscr{Z}_{s}$, and if $\{u,x\}\notin\mathcal{U}$ it is also the augmented graph of $\mathscr{X}_{s}$.
Denote with $h$ the vertex to which $V({G}^{\mathcal{T}})\setminus Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}$ were contracted.
As the tree-cut decomposition for this graph we choose the tree-cut decomposition induced by $S_{s}$ on $\mathcal{D}$ where we add a node $p$ containing only $h$ as the parent of $s$.
Denote this tree-cut decomposition with $\mathcal{D}_{H}$.
We have ${\mathchoice{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}_{H}}\left(s\right)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}_{H}}(s)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}_{H}}(s)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}_{H}}(s)}}=2={\mathchoice{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}\left(s\right)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}{\adhesionOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}}$, and the 3-center of the torso at $p$ in $\mathcal{D}_{H}$ is the empty graph, while the torso at $s$ is the same with respect to $\mathcal{D}_{H}$ and $\mathcal{D}$.
Thus, the width of the $\mathcal{D}_{H}$ is bounded by the width of $\mathcal{D}$.
\par Let $H^{\prime}\coloneqq{G}^{\mathcal{T}}[Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}]$ be the augmented graph of the instance with all but the vertices in $Y^{\mathcal{D^{\prime}}}_{s}$ removed.
Notice that $H^{\prime}$ is the augmented graph of $\mathscr{Y}_{s}$ and if $\{u,x\}\in\mathcal{U}$ it is also the augmented graph of $\mathscr{X}_{s}$.
As tree-cut decomposition for $H^{\prime}$ we choose the tree-cut decomposition induced by $S_{s}$ on $\mathcal{D}_{H}$.
\par The augmented graph of the reduced instance is either $I\coloneqq{G}^{\mathcal{T}}-Y_{s}$ or $J\coloneqq{G}^{\mathcal{T}}/Y_{s}$.
We obtain a tree-cut decomposition for $I$ by removing $S_{s}$ from $\mathcal{D}$.
Denote with $z$ the vertex in $J$ to which the vertices in $Y_{s}$ are contracted.
For $J$, we obtain a tree-cut decomposition by replacing $S_{s}$ by a node associated with a bag containing only $z$.
\qed\end@proof
\par Together \lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{rr:tcw-thin-supply}, \lx@cref{refnum}{rr:tcw-thin-independent} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{rr:tcw-thin-demand}, can be used to remove a cluttered node—or at least make it non-cluttered.
We now show how to apply these rules to solve $\gstp$ parameterized by tree-cut width of the augmented graph.
To do so, we solve multiple sub-instances of $\gstp$ with respect to simple tree-cut decompositions.
\par\begin{theorem}Assume $\gstp$ can be solved in time $r(g,k)$, given a graph of size at most $g$ and a simple tree-cut decomposition of width at most $k$.
Then, $\gstp$ for the instance $\mathscr{P}\coloneqq(G,\mathcal{T},d)$ given a tree-cut decomposition of width $w$ for ${G}^{\mathcal{T}}$ can be solved in time ${\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|\mathscr{P}\right|}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}^{7}+{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}^{2}r(3{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}},w+12)\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|\mathscr{P}\right|}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}^{7}+{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}^{2}r(3{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}},w+12))}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|\mathscr{P}\right|}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}^{7}+{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}^{2}r(3{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}},w+12))}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|\mathscr{P}\right|}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}^{7}+{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}^{2}r(3{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}},w+12))}}={\mathchoice{\OOp^{\ast}\left(r({\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}},w+12)\right)}{\OOp^{\ast}(r({\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}},w+12))}{\OOp^{\ast}(r({\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}},w+12))}{\OOp^{\ast}(r({\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}},w+12))}}$.
\end{theorem}
\@proof First, we exhaustively apply \lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{rr:sensible-terminal-sets} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{rr:degree-negative-instance} in time ${\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|\mathscr{P}\right|}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|\mathscr{P}\right|}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|\mathscr{P}\right|}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|\mathscr{P}\right|}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}{{|\mathscr{P}|}}})}}$.
After this ${\mathchoice{\left|T\right|}{{|T|}}{{|T|}}{{|T|}}}\leq 2{\mathchoice{\left|E(G)\right|}{{|E(G)|}}{{|E(G)|}}{{|E(G)|}}}$, implying ${\mathchoice{\left|V({G}^{\mathcal{T}})\right|}{{|V({G}^{\mathcal{T}})|}}{{|V({G}^{\mathcal{T}})|}}{{|V({G}^{\mathcal{T}})|}}}\leq 3{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}}$.
\par Now, we exhaustively apply \lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{rr:connected-components} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{rr:adh-1}.
This can be done in time ${\mathchoice{\OOp\left(w{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}}\right)}{\OOp(w{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}})}{\OOp(w{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}})}{\OOp(w{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}})}}$.
Now, we make the tree-cut decomposition nice in cubic time~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{GanianKS22}{}{}]}.
Then, we make it friendly using \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:tcw-blowing-up-running-time} in time ${\mathchoice{\OOp\left(w{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}}\right)}{\OOp(w{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}})}{\OOp(w{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}})}{\OOp(w{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}})}}$, which might increase the tree-cut width to $\max(4,w)\leq w+4$.
We continue with this until none of \lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{rr:connected-components} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{rr:adh-1} is applicable anymore.
Overall, this might take ${\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|E(G)\right|}{{|E(G)|}}{{|E(G)|}}{{|E(G)|}}}(w{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}^{3}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|E(G)\right|}{{|E(G)|}}{{|E(G)|}}{{|E(G)|}}}(w{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}^{3})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|E(G)\right|}{{|E(G)|}}{{|E(G)|}}{{|E(G)|}}}(w{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}^{3})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|E(G)\right|}{{|E(G)|}}{{|E(G)|}}{{|E(G)|}}}(w{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}^{3})}})$.
Additionally, these algorithms ensure that each leave is not empty and all empty nodes have at least two children.
\par If there is no cluttered node, we solve the instance in time $r({\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}},w+9)$ using \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:no-cluttered}.
So, let $s$ be the cluttered node with largest depth, resolving ties arbitrarily.
We want to apply \lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{rr:tcw-thin-supply}, \lx@cref{refnum}{rr:tcw-thin-independent} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{rr:tcw-thin-demand}.
To not increase the running-time too much, when applying one of these rules, we first ensure $2\leq{\mathchoice{\left|Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s}\right|}{{|Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s}|}}{{|Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s}|}}{{|Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s}|}}}<\frac{{\mathchoice{\left|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}\right|}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}}}{2}$.
\par We know, that $2\leq{\mathchoice{\left|Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s}\right|}{{|Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s}|}}{{|Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s}|}}{{|Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s}|}}}\leq{\mathchoice{\left|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}\right|}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}}-1$.
Let $p$ denote the parent of $t$.
If ${\mathchoice{\left|Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s}\right|}{{|Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s}|}}{{|Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s}|}}{{|Y^{\mathcal{D}}_{s}|}}}={\mathchoice{\left|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}\right|}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}}-1$, we notice that by choice of $s$, it is the only cluttered node in $T$.
We now merge $X_{p}$ into $X_{s}$ and remove—the now empty node—$p$ from the tree-cut decomposition.
Call this tree-cut decomposition $\mathcal{D}_{s}$.
Since $\mathcal{D}$ is friendly, so is $\mathcal{D}_{s}$ and the width of $\mathcal{D}_{s}$ is bounded by $(w+4)+3$.
Additionally, since $s$ was the only cluttered node in $\mathcal{D}$, there is no cluttered node in $\mathcal{D}_{s}$.
Using this, we solve the instance in running time $r({\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}},w+12)$ using \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:no-cluttered}.
Note that in this case we are done now; so, the increase in width is negligible.
\par If $\frac{{\mathchoice{\left|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}\right|}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}}}{2}\leq{\mathchoice{\left|Y_{s}\right|}{{|Y_{s}|}}{{|Y_{s}|}}{{|Y_{s}|}}}<{\mathchoice{\left|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}\right|}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}}-1$, we re-root $S$ to $s$, this might make $S$ not-nice, but we do not need that at this stage anymore.
Notice that $p$ is cluttered in the modified tree-cut decomposition and that ${\mathchoice{\left|Y_{s}\right|}{{|Y_{s}|}}{{|Y_{s}|}}{{|Y_{s}|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|Y_{p}\right|}{{|Y_{p}|}}{{|Y_{p}|}}{{|Y_{p}|}}}={\mathchoice{\left|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}\right|}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}}$.
Thus, in the modified tree-cut decomposition $2\leq{\mathchoice{\left|Y_{p}\right|}{{|Y_{p}|}}{{|Y_{p}|}}{{|Y_{p}|}}}<\frac{{\mathchoice{\left|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}\right|}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}}}{2}$.
So, we assume, without loss of generality, that we choose a cluttered node $s^{\prime}$ with $2\leq{\mathchoice{\left|Y_{s^{\prime}}\right|}{{|Y_{s^{\prime}}|}}{{|Y_{s^{\prime}}|}}{{|Y_{s^{\prime}}|}}}<\frac{{\mathchoice{\left|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}\right|}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}}}{2}$.
\par We now construct the tree-cut decompositions for $\mathscr{X}_{s^{\prime}}$, $\mathscr{Y}_{s^{\prime}}$, and $\mathscr{Z}_{s^{\prime}}$ in linear time using \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:rr-tcw-thin-decomposition-sensible} and recursively check which of $\mathscr{X}_{s^{\prime}}$, $\mathscr{Y}_{s^{\prime}}$, and $\mathscr{Z}_{s^{\prime}}$ are positive instances.
Based on this information, we apply the appropriate rule out of \lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{rr:tcw-thin-supply}, \lx@cref{refnum}{rr:tcw-thin-independent} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{rr:tcw-thin-demand}, which takes at most ${\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}\right|}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}\right|}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}\right|}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}\right|}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}{{|{G}^{\mathcal{T}}|}}})}}={\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}})}}$ time and recursively solve the remaining instance.
\par To bound the running time, we calculate how many uncluttered instances will be solved.
Let $i(n)$ denote the maximum number of uncluttered instances solved, if the augmented graph contains $n$ vertices.
Assume that we consider a cluttered node $s$.
The augmented graphs of the sub-instances have at most ${\mathchoice{\left|Y_{s}\right|}{{|Y_{s}|}}{{|Y_{s}|}}{{|Y_{s}|}}}+1\leq\frac{n}{2}$ vertices.
After applying the reduction rules, the vertices in $Y_{s}$ get contracted to a single vertex.
Thus, ${\mathchoice{\left|Y_{s}\right|}{{|Y_{s}|}}{{|Y_{s}|}}{{|Y_{s}|}}}-1$ vertices are removed from the instance.
So, for large enough $n$, the function $i$ satisfies $i(n)\leq\max_{2\leq k<\frac{n}{2}}3i(k+1)+i(n-k+1)$.
Using this, one can prove inductively that there is a $c\in\R$ such that for all $n\in\N$ we have $i(n)\leq c(n-3)^{2}+c={\mathchoice{\OOp\left(n^{2}\right)}{\OOp(n^{2})}{\OOp(n^{2})}{\OOp(n^{2})}}$.
Thus, we solve at most ${\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}^{2}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}^{2})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}^{2})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}^{2})}}$ uncluttered instances, each of which takes at most time $r({\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}},w+12)$.
\par Now, we only need to analyze the additional cost.
