In each recursion step, we do at most do at most ${\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|E(G)\right|}{{|E(G)|}}{{|E(G)|}}{{|E(G)|}}}(w{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}^{3})\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|E(G)\right|}{{|E(G)|}}{{|E(G)|}}{{|E(G)|}}}(w{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}^{3}))}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|E(G)\right|}{{|E(G)|}}{{|E(G)|}}{{|E(G)|}}}(w{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}^{3}))}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|E(G)\right|}{{|E(G)|}}{{|E(G)|}}{{|E(G)|}}}(w{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}^{3}))}}$ additional work.
Therefore, the overall amount of additional work is ${\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|E(G)\right|}{{|E(G)|}}{{|E(G)|}}{{|E(G)|}}}(w{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}^{3})i({\mathchoice{\left|V({G}^{\mathcal{T}})\right|}{{|V({G}^{\mathcal{T}})|}}{{|V({G}^{\mathcal{T}})|}}{{|V({G}^{\mathcal{T}})|}}})\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|E(G)\right|}{{|E(G)|}}{{|E(G)|}}{{|E(G)|}}}(w{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}^{3})i({\mathchoice{\left|V({G}^{\mathcal{T}})\right|}{{|V({G}^{\mathcal{T}})|}}{{|V({G}^{\mathcal{T}})|}}{{|V({G}^{\mathcal{T}})|}}}))}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|E(G)\right|}{{|E(G)|}}{{|E(G)|}}{{|E(G)|}}}(w{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}^{3})i({\mathchoice{\left|V({G}^{\mathcal{T}})\right|}{{|V({G}^{\mathcal{T}})|}}{{|V({G}^{\mathcal{T}})|}}{{|V({G}^{\mathcal{T}})|}}}))}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|E(G)\right|}{{|E(G)|}}{{|E(G)|}}{{|E(G)|}}}(w{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}^{3})i({\mathchoice{\left|V({G}^{\mathcal{T}})\right|}{{|V({G}^{\mathcal{T}})|}}{{|V({G}^{\mathcal{T}})|}}{{|V({G}^{\mathcal{T}})|}}}))}}={\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}^{7}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}^{7})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}^{7})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}^{7})}}$.
\qed\end@proof
\par Kim et~al.~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{KimOPST18}{}{}]} proved that for all $k\in\N$ in time $2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left(k^{2}\log w\right)}{\OOp(k^{2}\log w)}{\OOp(k^{2}\log w)}{\OOp(k^{2}\log w)}}{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}^{2}$ we can either compute a tree-cut decomposition of width $2k$, or we can certify that no tree-cut decomposition of width $k$ exists.
Combined with \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:fpt-simple-tcw}, we know that $\gstp$ is FPT by the tree-cut width of the augmented graph.
\par\begin{corollary}Let $(G,\mathcal{T},d)$ be an instance of $\gstp$ and set $k\coloneqq{\mathchoice{\tcwOp\left({G}^{\mathcal{T}}\right)}{\tcwOp({G}^{\mathcal{T}})}{\tcwOp({G}^{\mathcal{T}})}{\tcwOp({G}^{\mathcal{T}})}}$.
We can decide whether this instance is positive in time $\OOp^{\ast}\left(2^{2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left(k^{8}\right)}{\OOp(k^{8})}{\OOp(k^{8})}{\OOp(k^{8})}}}\right)$; so, $\gstp$ is $\fpt$ by the tree-cut width of the augmented graph.
\end{corollary}
\par
\par\@@numbered@section{section}{toc}{\texorpdfstring{$\stp$}{STP} is \texorpdfstring{$\fpt$}{FPT} by the Tree-Cut Width}
In this Chapter, we show that $\stp$ is FPT by the tree-cut width of the host graph.
As $\stp$ is a special case of $\gstp$, where we only have a single terminal set.
This means that we can apply the results derived in the previous Chapters for $\gstp$ to $\stp$.
However, the previous are not strong enough to directly show that $\stp$ is $\fpt$ by the tree-cut width of the host-graph.
Mainly the case when the terminal set is large, but the demand is low is still missing.
\par Let $(G,T,d)$ be an instance of $\stp$ and let $\mathcal{D}=(S,\mathcal{X})$ be a friendly tree-cut decomposition of $G$ with width $w$.
If ${\mathchoice{\left|T\right|}{{|T|}}{{|T|}}{{|T|}}}\leq w$, we interpret the instance as an instance of $\gstp$.
We can find a tree-cut decomposition $\mathcal{D}^{\prime}=(S^{\prime},\mathcal{X}^{\prime})$ for $G^{\{T\}}$ by adding a new root $r^{\prime}$ with the bag $\{\aug{T}\lx@text@rbrace$ to $S$ and making the old root a child of $r^{\prime}$.
The adhesion of $\mathcal{D}^{\prime}$ is bounded by $2w$.
Let $s\in V(S)$.
Any $t\in{\mathchoice{\thinChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}}\left(s\right)}{\thinChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}{\thinChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}{\thinChildrenOp_{\mathcal{D}}(s)}}$ with $Y_{t}^{\mathcal{D}}\cap T\neq\emptyset$ is also a thin child of $s^{\prime}$ in $\mathcal{D}^{\prime}$.
Thus, the size of the 3-center of the torso at $s$ in $\mathcal{D}^{\prime}$ is bounded by $1+{\mathchoice{\left|T\right|}{{|T|}}{{|T|}}{{|T|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\boldChildrenOp\left(s\right)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}{\boldChildrenOp(s)}}|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|X_{s}\right|}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}{{|X_{s}|}}}\leq 2w+3$.
Note that the torso at $r^{\prime}$ in $\mathcal{D}^{\prime}$ consists of 2 vertices.
Thus, the width of $\mathcal{D}^{\prime}$ is bounded by $2w+3$ and we can use \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:gstp-fpt-tcw} to decide whether the instance is positive in $\fpt$-time.
\par The same approach does not work for the case ${\mathchoice{\left|T\right|}{{|T|}}{{|T|}}{{|T|}}}>w$, as in this case, the tree-cut width of the augmented graph is not necessarily bounded by a function of the tree-cut width of the host graph.
For this consider as the host graph a path of length $n$ where we attach to each vertex of the path 3 leaves.
This graph is a tree, has tree-cut width 1, and is depicted in \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{fig:unlimited-tcw-stp-before}.
Let the set of terminals be the set of all leaves.
The augmented graph, which is depicted in \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{fig:unlimited-tcw-stp-after}, contains the graph $S_{3,n}$ (i.e.,~the star graph with $n$ leaves where each leaf has 3 parallel edges to the center) as an immersion.
This graph has the wall $H_{\lfloor\sqrt{n}\rfloor}$ as an immersion~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Wollan15}{}{}]}.
Therefore, $H_{\lfloor\sqrt{n}\rfloor}$ has an immersion into the augmented graph.
For each $r\in\N$, let $G^{\prime}$ be a graph for which there is an immersion from $H_{2r^{2}}$ into $G$.
Thus, $G$ has tree-cut width at least $r$~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Wollan15}{}{}]} and the augmented graph has tree-cut width at least $\Omega(\sqrt[4]{n})$ (more careful calculations show the tree-cut width to be $\Theta(\sqrt{n})$), proving that the tree-cut width can grow without bound in the tree-cut width of the host graph.
\par\begin{remark}There exists a family of $\stp$ instances such that the host-graph of every instance has tree-cut width 1, but the tree-cut width of the augmented graph of the instances is not bounded.
\end{remark}
\par\begin{figure}\subfigure{0.49}
\includegraphics[width=345.0pt]{unlimited-tree-cut-width-before-augmentation.pdf}
\@@toccaption{{\lx@tag[ ]{{10}}{The host graph is a path of length $n$ with 3 leaves attached to each vertex.\newline
{}}}}\@@caption{{\lx@tag[: ]{{Figure 10}}{The host graph is a path of length $n$ with 3 leaves attached to each vertex.\newline
{}}}}
\subfigure{0.49}
\includegraphics[width=345.0pt]{unlimited-tree-cut-width-after-augmentation.pdf}
\@@toccaption{{\lx@tag[ ]{{11}}{The augmented graph with the terminal set being the set of all leaves. Augmented edges and vertices are drawn in orange.}}}\@@caption{{\lx@tag[: ]{{Figure 11}}{The augmented graph with the terminal set being the set of all leaves. Augmented edges and vertices are drawn in orange.}}}
\@@toccaption{{\lx@tag[ ]{{12}}{A family of host graphs with a terminal set increasing the tree-cut width of the augmented graph without bound.}}}\@@caption{{\lx@tag[: ]{{Figure 12}}{A family of host graphs with a terminal set increasing the tree-cut width of the augmented graph without bound.}}}\end{figure}
\par In this case (i.e.,~${\mathchoice{\left|T\right|}{{|T|}}{{|T|}}{{|T|}}}>w$), we observe that $T$ is not contained in a single bag.
Let $s,s^{\prime}\in V(S)$ be such that $X_{s}\cap T\neq\emptyset$, $X_{s^{\prime}}\cap T\neq\emptyset$, and $s^{\prime}\notin V(S_{s})$ that is $s^{\prime}$ is not a descendend of $s$.
Then, $T\in{\mathchoice{\crossLinkOp\left(s\right)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}{\crossLinkOp(s)}}$ and applying \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{rr:cross-link-demand-large}, we know that if $d(T)>w$, we get a trivial negative instance.
So, we can assume from now on that $d(T)\leq w$.
\par Ganian et~al.~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{GanianKS22}{}{}]} claimed that from a tree-cut decomposition, we can find a tree decomposition of width at most $2{w}^{2}+3w$.
Their argument relies on the claim that in a nice tree-cut decomposition, the number of bold children is bounded by $w+1$.
In \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:nice-tree-cut-unlimited-bold} we refute this claim.
However, their argument can easily be adapted to a friendly tree-cut decomposition.
Yielding a nice tree decomposition of width $2{w}^{2}+4w$; thus, ${\mathchoice{\twOp\left(G\right)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}}\leq 2w^{2}+4w$.
That is, in the missing case ${\mathchoice{\twOp\left(G\right)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}}+d(T)$ is bounded by a function of $w$.
The remainder of this Chapter is dedicated to proving the following theorem.
\par\begin{theorem}Let $(G,\mathcal{T},d)$ be an instance of the $\gstp$ problem and set ${\Sigma_{d}}\coloneqq\sum_{T\in\mathcal{T}}d(T)$.
In time ${\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\Sigma_{d}}{\mathchoice{\twOp\left(G\right)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}}\log{\mathchoice{\twOp\left(G\right)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}}\right)}{\OOp({\Sigma_{d}}{\mathchoice{\twOp\left(G\right)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}}\log{\mathchoice{\twOp\left(G\right)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}})}{\OOp({\Sigma_{d}}{\mathchoice{\twOp\left(G\right)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}}\log{\mathchoice{\twOp\left(G\right)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}})}{\OOp({\Sigma_{d}}{\mathchoice{\twOp\left(G\right)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}}\log{\mathchoice{\twOp\left(G\right)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}})}}$, we can decide whether this instance is positive.
The $\gstp$ problem is $\fpt$ by the parameter ${\mathchoice{\twOp\left(G\right)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}}+{\Sigma_{d}}$.
\end{theorem}
\par This is a generalization of the case missing to solve $\stp$ parameterized by tree-cut width.
Combined with the case distinction presented above, \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:gstp-fpt-tcw}, and the fact that there is a $2$-approximation for tree-cut width running in time $2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\tcwOp\left(G\right)}{\tcwOp(G)}{\tcwOp(G)}{\tcwOp(G)}}^{2}\log{\mathchoice{\tcwOp\left(G\right)}{\tcwOp(G)}{\tcwOp(G)}{\tcwOp(G)}}\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\tcwOp\left(G\right)}{\tcwOp(G)}{\tcwOp(G)}{\tcwOp(G)}}^{2}\log{\mathchoice{\tcwOp\left(G\right)}{\tcwOp(G)}{\tcwOp(G)}{\tcwOp(G)}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\tcwOp\left(G\right)}{\tcwOp(G)}{\tcwOp(G)}{\tcwOp(G)}}^{2}\log{\mathchoice{\tcwOp\left(G\right)}{\tcwOp(G)}{\tcwOp(G)}{\tcwOp(G)}})}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\tcwOp\left(G\right)}{\tcwOp(G)}{\tcwOp(G)}{\tcwOp(G)}}^{2}\log{\mathchoice{\tcwOp\left(G\right)}{\tcwOp(G)}{\tcwOp(G)}{\tcwOp(G)}})}}{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}^{2}$~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{KimOPST18}{}{}]}, we get that $\stp$ is $\fpt$ by the tree-cut width of the host graph.
\par\begin{corollary}Let $(G,T,d)$ be an instance of $\stp$ and set $k\coloneqq{\mathchoice{\tcwOp\left(G\right)}{\tcwOp(G)}{\tcwOp(G)}{\tcwOp(G)}}$.
In time $\OOp^{\ast}\left(2^{2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left(k^{8}\right)}{\OOp(k^{8})}{\OOp(k^{8})}{\OOp(k^{8})}}}\right)$, we can decide whether this instance is positive.
So, $\stp$ is $\fpt$ by the parameter ${\mathchoice{\tcwOp\left(G\right)}{\tcwOp(G)}{\tcwOp(G)}{\tcwOp(G)}}$.
\end{corollary}
\par\par\@@numbered@section{subsection}{toc}{\texorpdfstring{$\gstp$}{GSTP} is \texorpdfstring{$\fpt$}{FPT} by Treewidth + Sum of Demands}
\par Let $\mathscr{P}\coloneqq(G,\mathcal{T},d)$ be an instance of $\gstp$ with ${\Sigma_{d}}\coloneqq\sum_{T\in\mathcal{T}}d(T)$.
Let $\mathcal{D}\coloneqq(S,\mathcal{X})$ be a nice tree decomposition of width $w$.
We see, that for each ${\Sigma_{d}}$ we can compute a $\mathrm{MSO}_{2}$ formula for this problem.
Using Courcelles theorem~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{BoriePT92}{}{}]}, we see that this problem is FPT by $w+{\Sigma_{d}}$.
However, the running time using this meta theorem is horrendous.
So, we additionally provide a more or less standard dynamic program by treewidth.
In this dynamic program, we essentially save for each tree all connectivity information below the current bag.
\par For $\edp$ an $\fpt$-algorithm parameterized by $w+{\Sigma_{d}}$ is known~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{ZhouTN00}{}{}]}.
The algorithm runs in ${\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}(({\Sigma_{d}}+w^{2}){\Sigma_{d}}^{w(w+1)/2}+{\Sigma_{d}}(w+4)^{2(w+4){\Sigma_{d}}+3})\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}(({\Sigma_{d}}+w^{2}){\Sigma_{d}}^{w(w+1)/2}+{\Sigma_{d}}(w+4)^{2(w+4){\Sigma_{d}}+3}))}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}(({\Sigma_{d}}+w^{2}){\Sigma_{d}}^{w(w+1)/2}+{\Sigma_{d}}(w+4)^{2(w+4){\Sigma_{d}}+3}))}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}(({\Sigma_{d}}+w^{2}){\Sigma_{d}}^{w(w+1)/2}+{\Sigma_{d}}(w+4)^{2(w+4){\Sigma_{d}}+3}))}}={\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}(2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left(w^{2}\log{\Sigma_{d}}\right)}{\OOp(w^{2}\log{\Sigma_{d}})}{\OOp(w^{2}\log{\Sigma_{d}})}{\OOp(w^{2}\log{\Sigma_{d}})}}+2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\Sigma_{d}}w\log w\right)}{\OOp({\Sigma_{d}}w\log w)}{\OOp({\Sigma_{d}}w\log w)}{\OOp({\Sigma_{d}}w\log w)}})$.
Our algorithm generalizes this result significantly, while improving the running time to ${\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\Sigma_{d}}w\log w\right)}{\OOp({\Sigma_{d}}w\log w)}{\OOp({\Sigma_{d}}w\log w)}{\OOp({\Sigma_{d}}w\log w)}}$.
We conjecture that our result is a stepping stone towards applying the Cut\&Count technique introduced by Cygan et~al.~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{CyganNPPRW11}{}{}]} to obtain an algorithm running in time ${\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\Sigma_{d}}w\right)}{\OOp({\Sigma_{d}}w)}{\OOp({\Sigma_{d}}w)}{\OOp({\Sigma_{d}}w)}}$.
\par Consider a solution $(\mathcal{F},\pi)$ to $\mathscr{P}$ and a $s\in V(S)$.
There are three types of subgraphs in $\mathcal{F}$.
First, there are the subgraphs $F\in\mathcal{F}$ with $V(F)\subseteq Y_{s}\setminus X_{s}$, that is all vertices of these subgraphs have already been forgotten at $s$.
Second, there are the subgraphs $F\in\mathcal{F}$ with $V(F)\cap X_{s}\neq\emptyset$, that is the subgraphs cross the bag $s$.
Finally, there are the subgraphs $F\in\mathcal{F}$ with $V(F)\cap Y_{s}=\emptyset$, that is the subgraphs that are completely contained disjoint from $Y_{s}$.
In the dynamic program, we store for each $s\in V(S)$, $T\in\mathcal{T}$, and $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[d(T)\right]}{[d(T)]}{[d(T)]}{[d(T)]}}$ in which type the corresponding solution subgraphs fall and for each $F\in\mathcal{F}$ with $V(F)\cap X_{s}\neq\emptyset$, we additionally store for each $K\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left(F[Y_{s}]-E(G[X_{s}])\right)}{\compOp(F[Y_{s}]-E(G[X_{s}]))}{\compOp(F[Y_{s}]-E(G[X_{s}]))}{\compOp(F[Y_{s}]-E(G[X_{s}]))}}$ the set $K\cap X_{s}$.
\par Formally, let $\enumerateTerminalOp\colon{\mathchoice{\left[{\Sigma_{d}}\right]}{[{\Sigma_{d}}]}{[{\Sigma_{d}}]}{[{\Sigma_{d}}]}}\to\mathcal{T}$ be such that for all $T\in\mathcal{T}$, we have ${\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}}|}}}=d(T)$.
This gives indices to all solution subgraphs such that we only need to find for each $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[{\Sigma_{d}}\right]}{[{\Sigma_{d}}]}{[{\Sigma_{d}}]}{[{\Sigma_{d}}]}}$ a solution subgraph $F_{i}$ with ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\subseteq V(F_{i})$.
To define the dynamic program at a node $s\in V(S)$, partition ${\mathchoice{\left[{\Sigma_{d}}\right]}{[{\Sigma_{d}}]}{[{\Sigma_{d}}]}{[{\Sigma_{d}}]}}$ into three parts $I^{\bot}$, $I^{\times}$, and $I^{\top}$ representing the indices of the three types of solutions subgraphs with respect to $s$.
We allow any of $I^{\bot}$, $I^{\times}$, and $I^{\top}$ to be empty.
Finally, consider for all $i\in I^{\times}$ a partitioning $\mathcal{P}_{i}\coloneqq\{P_{i,j}\}_{j\in{\mathchoice{\left[{\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{P}_{i}\right|}{{|\mathcal{P}_{i}|}}{{|\mathcal{P}_{i}|}}{{|\mathcal{P}_{i}|}}}\right]}{[{\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{P}_{i}\right|}{{|\mathcal{P}_{i}|}}{{|\mathcal{P}_{i}|}}{{|\mathcal{P}_{i}|}}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{P}_{i}\right|}{{|\mathcal{P}_{i}|}}{{|\mathcal{P}_{i}|}}{{|\mathcal{P}_{i}|}}}]}{[{\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{P}_{i}\right|}{{|\mathcal{P}_{i}|}}{{|\mathcal{P}_{i}|}}{{|\mathcal{P}_{i}|}}}]}}}$ of a set $U$ with $\emptyset\neq U\subseteq X_{s}$.
Intuitively, these partitions describe which vertices of $X_{s}$ are already connected in the subgraph below $s$.
The crucial change to obtain the speed-up over the state-of-the-art algorithm is to, ensure the connections are realized without edges inside $X_{s}$.
This allows the sub-solutions in join nodes to be treated independently.
The entries of the dynamic program $D(s)$ are tuples of the form $\tau\coloneqq(I^{\bot},I^{\times},I^{\top},(\mathcal{P}_{i})_{i\in I^{\times}})$.
We also refer to $I^{\bot}$ as $I^{\bot}_{\tau}$ and similarly for $I^{\times}$ as well as $I^{\top}$ and to $\mathcal{P}_{i}$ as $\mathcal{P}_{\tau,i}$.
\par To formally define which entries $\tau$ should be included in the dynamic program, we define an instance of $\gstp$ based on the corresponding tuple.
Let $G_{s}\coloneqq G[Y_{s}]-E(G[X_{s}])$ be the graph induced by all edges adjacent to a node already forgotten at $s$.
For each $i\in I^{\times}$ add a vertex $q_{i}$ to $G_{s}$ adjacent to all $X_{s}$.
Call the obtained graph $G_{s,\tau}$.
This is the host-graph of the corresponding instance.
To define the terminal sets, let $i\in I^{\bot}$.
Then ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}$ is a terminal set with demand ${\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}}\cap I^{\bot}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}}\cap I^{\bot}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}}\cap I^{\bot}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}}\cap I^{\bot}|}}}$, that is the demand is equal to the number of indices of solution subgraphs corresponding to the terminal set ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}$ that are completed at $s$.
Additionally, let $i\in I^{\times}$ and set $Q_{s,i}\coloneqq\{q_{i}\}\cup({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\cap Y_{s})$ to be a terminal set with demand 1.
So, the solution subgraph assigned to $Q_{s,i}$ is marked with including $q_{i}$ and can use $q_{i}$ to simulate that connections outside $G_{s}$ are made for this solution subgraph.
Call the obtained instance $\mathscr{D}_{s,\tau}$.
\par\begin{definition}The dynamic programming table $D(s)$ contains exactly the tuples $\tau\coloneqq(I^{\bot},I^{\times},I^{\top},(\mathcal{P}_{i})_{i\in I^{\times}})$ such that
\begin{enumerate}\enumerate@item@for all $T\in\mathcal{T}$ with $T\cap Y_{s}\neq\emptyset$, we have ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}}\subseteq I^{\bot}\cup I^{\times}$,
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@there is a solution $(\mathcal{F},\pi)$ to the instance $\mathscr{D}_{s,\tau}$ such that
\begin{enumerate}\enumerate@item@for all $i\in I^{\bot}$ and $F\in\pi^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})$, the set $V(F)$ is disjoint from $X_{s}$ and $\{q_{i}\}_{i\in I^{\times}}$,
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@for all $i\in I^{\times}$ and $\{F\}\coloneqq\pi^{-1}(Q_{s,i})$, the solution subgraph $F$ is the unique $F\in\mathcal{F}$ with $q_{i}\in V(F)$, and $\mathcal{P}_{i}=\{V(K)\cap X_{s}\mid K\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left(F-q_{i}\right)}{\compOp(F-q_{i})}{\compOp(F-q_{i})}{\compOp(F-q_{i})}}\}$.\end{enumerate}\end{enumerate}\end{definition}
\par With \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-started}, we ensure that all indices referring to solution subgraphs starting at or below $s$ are actually included the index sets that refer to solution subgraphs that are already started.
Using \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-solution}, we ensure that the solution $(\mathcal{F},\pi)$ to $\mathscr{D}_{s,\tau}$ witnessing $\tau\in D(s)$ actually fulfills some consistency requirements.
Namely, we ensure with \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-bot-are-complete} that the vertices $\{q_{i}\}_{i\in I^{\times}}$, which can be used to simulate connections outside $G_{s}$, are not used for solution subgraphs that are marked as complete in the tuple.
Finally, we ensure two properties using \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-crossing}.
First, we ensure that the $\{q_{i}\}_{i\in I^{\times}}$ are only used by the $i$-th solution subgraph, which means that these are used as markers.
Secondly, we make sure that the partitionings $(\mathcal{P}_{i})_{i\in I^{\times}}$ conform to our interpretation presented above.
\par We now show, that this dynamic program can be used to determine whether the instance is positive.
\par\begin{lemma}Let $r$ be the root of $S$.
The instance $\mathscr{P}$ is positive if and only if $$({\mathchoice{\left[{\Sigma_{d}}\right]}{[{\Sigma_{d}}]}{[{\Sigma_{d}}]}{[{\Sigma_{d}}]}},\emptyset,\emptyset,())\in D(r).$$\end{lemma}
\@proof Let $\tau\coloneqq({\mathchoice{\left[{\Sigma_{d}}\right]}{[{\Sigma_{d}}]}{[{\Sigma_{d}}]}{[{\Sigma_{d}}]}},\emptyset,\emptyset,())$.
Notice that $\mathcal{D}$ is nice and therefore $X_{r}=\emptyset$.
Thus, that $\mathscr{D}_{t,\tau}=\mathscr{P}$.
Additionally, the conditions laid forth in \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw} are satisfied by every solution to $\mathscr{D}_{s,\tau}$, proving the statement.
\qed\end@proof
\par Now, all that remains is to show, how to compute this dynamic program on a nice tree decomposition.
That is, we need to show how to compute the dynamic program for leaf, introduce, join, and forget nodes given that the dynamic program of all children is already computed.
\par\par\@@unnumbered@section{paragraph}{Leaf node} Let $s\in V(S)$ be a leaf node.
Since $\mathcal{D}$ is nice, $X_{s}=\emptyset$.
Observe that $Y_{s}=\emptyset$.
Thus, $I^{\bot}_{\tau}=\emptyset$.
We can verify that for all $S\subseteq{\mathchoice{\left[{\Sigma_{d}}\right]}{[{\Sigma_{d}}]}{[{\Sigma_{d}}]}{[{\Sigma_{d}}]}}$, we have $(\emptyset,S,{\mathchoice{\left[{\Sigma_{d}}\right]}{[{\Sigma_{d}}]}{[{\Sigma_{d}}]}{[{\Sigma_{d}}]}}\setminus S,(\emptyset)_{i\in S})\in D(s)$; so, $D(s)=\bigcup_{S\subseteq{\mathchoice{\left[{\Sigma_{d}}\right]}{[{\Sigma_{d}}]}{[{\Sigma_{d}}]}{[{\Sigma_{d}}]}}}\{(\emptyset,S,{\mathchoice{\left[{\Sigma_{d}}\right]}{[{\Sigma_{d}}]}{[{\Sigma_{d}}]}{[{\Sigma_{d}}]}}\setminus S,(\emptyset)_{i\in S})\}$, showing that these kinds of nodes can be computed efficiently.
\par\par\@@unnumbered@section{paragraph}{Introduce node} Let $s\in V(S)$ be an introduce node, that is it has exactly one child $c$ and there is a $v\in V(G)\setminus X_{c}$ with $X_{s}=X_{c}\cup\{v\}$.
Let $\tau\in D(c)$.
Note that $G_{c}+v=G_{s}$.
Consider how a solution $(\mathcal{F},\pi)$ to $\mathscr{D}_{c,\tau}$ can be extended using this new vertex $v$.
As $(S,\mathcal{X})$ is a tree decomposition, we know that $N(v)$ is disjoint from all already forgotten nodes $Y_{s}\setminus X_{s}$.
So, no solution subgraph associated with an index in $I^{\bot}_{\tau}$ can be extended to include $v$ without changing the state regarding $X_{c}$.
Any $F\in\pi^{-1}(\{Q_{s,i}\}_{i\in I^{\times}_{\tau}})$ can be extended to include $v$ by adding the edge $vq_{i}$ to $F$.
For an $i\in I^{\top}_{\tau}$, we can also introduce a new solution subgraph $G[vq_{i}]$ only containing the edges $vq_{i}$.
To formally capture this, let $S\subseteq I^{\times}_{\tau}\cup I^{\top}_{\tau}$.
For all $i\in I^{\times}\cap S$, set $\mathcal{P}_{i}^{\prime}\coloneqq\mathcal{P}_{\tau,i}\cup\{\{v\}\}$, for all $i\in S\setminus I^{\times}$, set $\mathcal{P}_{i}^{\prime}\coloneqq\{\{v\}\}$ for all $i\in I^{\times}\setminus S$, set $\mathcal{P}_{i}^{\prime}\coloneqq\mathcal{P}_{\tau,i}$.
Now, we define the function ${\mathchoice{\extendOp\left(\tau,S\right)}{\extendOp(\tau,S)}{\extendOp(\tau,S)}{\extendOp(\tau,S)}}\coloneqq(I^{\bot}_{\tau},I^{\times}_{\tau}\cup S,I^{\top}_{\tau}\setminus S,(\mathcal{P}_{i}^{\prime})_{i\in I^{\times}\cup S})$.
Intuitively, ${\mathchoice{\extendOp\left(\tau,S\right)}{\extendOp(\tau,S)}{\extendOp(\tau,S)}{\extendOp(\tau,S)}}$ is the state obtained from $\tau$, when all solution subgraphs associated with indices in $S$ are extended to include $v$.
\par\begin{lemma}Let $L\coloneqq\{i\in{\mathchoice{\left[{\Sigma_{d}}\right]}{[{\Sigma_{d}}]}{[{\Sigma_{d}}]}{[{\Sigma_{d}}]}}\mid v\in{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\}$.
Then, $$D(s)=\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tau\in D(c),\\
L\subseteq S\subseteq I^{\times}_{\tau}\cup I^{\top}_{\tau}\end{subarray}}\{{\mathchoice{\extendOp\left(\tau,S\right)}{\extendOp(\tau,S)}{\extendOp(\tau,S)}{\extendOp(\tau,S)}}\}.$$\end{lemma}
\par\@proof Let $\tau\in D(s)$ and let $(\mathcal{F},\pi)$ be a solution to $\mathscr{D}_{s,\tau}$ such that the additional restrictions of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-bot-are-complete} in \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw} are satisfied.
We now show, that there is are $\gamma\in D(c)$ and $L\subseteq S\subseteq I^{\top}_{\gamma}\cup I^{\times}_{\gamma}$ with ${\mathchoice{\extendOp\left(\gamma,S\right)}{\extendOp(\gamma,S)}{\extendOp(\gamma,S)}{\extendOp(\gamma,S)}}=\tau$.
For this, we first define $\gamma$.
Set $I^{\bot}_{\gamma}\coloneqq I^{\bot}_{\tau}$.
For all $i\in I^{\times}_{\tau}$, we set $\mathcal{P}_{i}^{\prime}\coloneqq\mathcal{P}_{i}\setminus\{\{v\}\}$, that is we remove from $\mathcal{P}_{i}$ the partition only containing $v$.
We set $I^{\times}_{\gamma}\coloneqq\{i\in I^{\times}_{\tau}\mid\mathcal{P}_{i}^{\prime}\neq\emptyset\}$ to be the indices that use vertices of $X_{s}$ in $\tau$ apart from $v$, and we set for all $i\in I^{\times}_{\gamma}$ that $\mathcal{P}_{\gamma,i}\coloneqq\mathcal{P}_{i}^{\prime}$.
Finally, set $I^{\top}_{\gamma}$ to be the remaining indices ${\mathchoice{\left[{\Sigma_{d}}\right]}{[{\Sigma_{d}}]}{[{\Sigma_{d}}]}{[{\Sigma_{d}}]}}\setminus(I^{\bot}_{\gamma}\cup I^{\times}_{\gamma})$.
Additionally, set $S\coloneqq\{i\in I^{\times}_{\tau}\mid v\in\bigcup\mathcal{P}_{\tau,i}\}$ to be the set of indices using $v$ in the solution.
One can verify that ${\mathchoice{\extendOp\left(\gamma,S\right)}{\extendOp(\gamma,S)}{\extendOp(\gamma,S)}{\extendOp(\gamma,S)}}=\tau$.
\par It remains to show that $L\subseteq S$ and that $\gamma\in D(c)$.
We first show $L\subseteq S$.
Let $i\in L$, then $i\in I^{\bot}_{\tau}\cup I^{\times}_{\tau}$.
Assume $i\in I^{\bot}_{\tau}$.
Then, the demand of ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}$ is at least 1 in $\mathscr{D}_{s,\tau}$; so, let $F\in\pi^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})$.
As $v\in{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\subseteq V(F)$, this violates \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-bot-are-complete} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw}.
Therefore, $i\in I^{\times}_{\tau}$.
Let $\{F\}\coloneqq\pi^{-1}(Q_{s,i})$.
As $v\in{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\subseteq V(F)$, we have, by \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-crossing} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw}, that $i\in S$.
\par To show $\gamma\in D(c)$, we verify that $\gamma$ satisfies \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-started} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw}.
Let $T\in\mathcal{T}$ with $\emptyset\neq T\cap Y_{c}\subseteq T\cap Y_{s}$.
By \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-started} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw}, we have ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}}\subseteq I^{\bot}_{\tau}\cup I^{\times}_{\tau}$.
We know $I^{\bot}_{\tau}=I^{\bot}_{\gamma}$ and $\{i\in I^{\times}_{\tau}\mid\mathcal{P}_{i}^{\prime}\neq\emptyset\}=I^{\times}_{\gamma}$.
So, it suffices to show for all $i\in{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}}$ with $i\in I^{\times}_{\tau}$ that $\mathcal{P}_{i}^{\prime}\neq\emptyset$.
Assume the contrary, that is there is a $i\in{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}}$ with $\mathcal{P}_{i}^{\prime}=\emptyset$.
Then, $\mathcal{P}_{i}=\{\{v\}\}$.
Let $u\in T\cap Y_{c}$ and consider a $vu$-path $Q$ in $F$.
As $N_{G}(v)$ is disjoint from $Y_{s}\setminus X_{s}$ and since there are no edges among vertices in $X_{s}$ in $G_{s}$, there is an $x\in X_{s}\setminus\{v\}$ with $Q=vq_{i}x\dots u$.
Thus, $x\in V(F)\cap X_{s}$.
By \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-crossing}, we have $x\in\bigcup\mathcal{P}_{i}$ and violating $\mathcal{P}_{i}=\{\{v\}\}$; so, $\gamma$ satisfies \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-started} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw}.
\par Finally, we provide a solution to $\mathscr{D}_{c,\gamma}$ satisfying the additional requirements of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-solution} in \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw}.
For this, let $i\in I^{\bot}_{\gamma}$.
As $I^{\bot}_{\gamma}=I^{\bot}_{\tau}$, there is a $F\in\pi^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})$.
By \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-bot-are-complete} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw}, $F$ is fully contained in $G[Y_{s}\setminus X_{s}]=G[Y_{c}\setminus X_{c}]$.
So, we assign all such $F$ to ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}$ in our solution to $\mathscr{D}_{c,\gamma}$ and since $I^{\bot}_{\gamma}=I^{\bot}_{\tau}$, this satisfies the demand of ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}$ while satisfying \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-bot-are-complete} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw}.
Now, let $i\in I^{\times}_{\gamma}\subseteq I^{\times}_{\tau}$ and let $\{F_{i}\}\coloneqq\pi^{-1}(Q_{s,i})$.
The graph $F^{*}_{i}\coloneqq F-v$ is contained in $G_{c,\rho}$ and we see $Q_{c,i}=q_{i}\cup({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\cap Y_{c})=(q_{i}\cup({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\cap Y_{s}))\setminus\{v\}=Q_{s,i}\setminus\{v\}\subseteq V(F^{*}_{i})$.
Consequently, we assign $F^{*}_{i}$ to $Q_{c,i}$ in $\mathscr{D}_{c,\gamma}$, satisfying its demands.
We can verify that this solution satisfies \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-bot-are-complete} and that for all $i\in I^{\times}_{\gamma}$, the solution subgraph $F^{*}_{i}$ is the unique solution subgraph containing $q_{i}$.
Finally, consider $K\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left(F_{i}-q_{i}\right)}{\compOp(F_{i}-q_{i})}{\compOp(F_{i}-q_{i})}{\compOp(F_{i}-q_{i})}}$.
Assume $v\in V(K)$.
As $N_{G_{s}}(v)=\{q_{i}\}$, we have $V(K)=\{v\}$.
So, if $v\notin V(K)$, there is a $K^{\prime}\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left(F^{*}_{i}-q_{i}\right)}{\compOp(F^{*}_{i}-q_{i})}{\compOp(F^{*}_{i}-q_{i})}{\compOp(F^{*}_{i}-q_{i})}}$ with $K=K^{\prime}$ and vice versa, concluding the proof that $\gamma\in D(c)$.
\par Now, let $\gamma\in D(c)$ and $L\subseteq S\subseteq I^{\times}_{\gamma}\cup I^{\top}_{\gamma}$ be given.
We now show that $\tau\coloneqq{\mathchoice{\extendOp\left(\gamma,S\right)}{\extendOp(\gamma,S)}{\extendOp(\gamma,S)}{\extendOp(\gamma,S)}}\in D(s)$ by verifying \lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-started} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{item:tw-dp-solution} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw} one after the other.
Let $T\in\mathcal{T}$ with $T\cap Y_{s}\neq\emptyset$.
If $T\cap Y_{c}\neq\emptyset$, by \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-started} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw}, we have ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}}\subseteq I^{\bot}_{\gamma}\cup I^{\times}_{\gamma}\subseteq I^{\bot}_{\tau}\cup I^{\times}_{\tau}$.
Otherwise, $v\in T$.
Thus, ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}}\subseteq L\subseteq S\subseteq I^{\times}_{\tau}$, showing that \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-started} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw} is satisfied.
\par To construct a solution to $\mathscr{D}_{s,\tau}$ satisfying the additional requirements of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-solution} in \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw}, let $(\mathcal{F},\pi)$ be a solution to $\mathscr{D}_{c,\gamma}$ satisfying these additional requirements.
First, let $i\in I^{\bot}_{\tau}=I^{\bot}_{\gamma}$ and consider $F\in\pi^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})$.
By \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-bot-are-complete}, we have ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\subseteq V(F)\subseteq Y_{c}\setminus X_{c}=Y_{s}\setminus X_{s}$; so, we assign each such $F$ to ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}$ satisfying its demand in $\mathscr{D}_{s,\tau}$.
Now, let $i\in I^{\times}_{\gamma}\subseteq I^{\times}_{\tau}$ and let $\{F\}\coloneq\pi^{-1}(Q_{c,i})$.
If $i\in S$, we consider the subgraph $F^{*}_{i}\coloneqq F+q_{i}v$ of $G_{s,\tau}$.
As $q_{i}\in V(F)$, we know that $F^{*}_{i}$ is connected.
Furthermore, $Q_{s,i}=\{q_{i}\}\cup({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\cap Y_{s})\subseteq\{q_{i},v\}\cup({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\cap Y_{c})=Q_{c,i}\cup\{v\}\subseteq V(F^{*}_{i})$.
So, we assign $F^{*}_{i}$ to $Q_{s,i}$ in our solution, satisfying its demand.
Now, let $i\in I^{\times}_{\tau}\setminus I^{\times}_{\gamma}$.
By \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-started}, we know that ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\cap Y_{c}=\emptyset$.
Thus, $Q_{s,i}=\{q_{i}\}\cup({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\cap Y_{s})\subseteq\{q_{i},v\}$.
We assign the subgraph $F^{*}_{i}\coloneqq G_{s,\tau}[q_{i}v]$, that is the subgraph induced by the edge $q_{i}v$, to the terminal set $Q_{s,i}$, completing the description of the solution to $\mathscr{D}_{s,\tau}$.
We can verify that this solution additionally satisfies \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-solution} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw}.
\qed\end@proof
\par This gives us an easy way to compute the dynamic program for introduce nodes.
\par\begin{corollary}The dynamic program for an introduce node with child $c$ can be computed in time ${\mathchoice{\OOp\left(1+({\Sigma_{d}}+w)2^{\Sigma_{d}}{\mathchoice{\left|D(c)\right|}{{|D(c)|}}{{|D(c)|}}{{|D(c)|}}}\right)}{\OOp(1+({\Sigma_{d}}+w)2^{\Sigma_{d}}{\mathchoice{\left|D(c)\right|}{{|D(c)|}}{{|D(c)|}}{{|D(c)|}}})}{\OOp(1+({\Sigma_{d}}+w)2^{\Sigma_{d}}{\mathchoice{\left|D(c)\right|}{{|D(c)|}}{{|D(c)|}}{{|D(c)|}}})}{\OOp(1+({\Sigma_{d}}+w)2^{\Sigma_{d}}{\mathchoice{\left|D(c)\right|}{{|D(c)|}}{{|D(c)|}}{{|D(c)|}}})}}$ given that $D(c)$ is provided.
\end{corollary}
\par\par\@@unnumbered@section{paragraph}{Join node} Let $s\in V(S)$ be a join node with children $a,b$ such that $X_{s}=X_{a}=X_{b}$.
Notice that $Y_{a}\setminus X_{s}$ and $Y_{b}\setminus X_{s}$ are disjoint, which means that $G_{a}$ and $G_{b}$ are edge-disjoint, since vertices in $X_{s}$ are not adjacent.
Let $\alpha\in D(a)$ and $\beta\in D(b)$.
Consider solutions $(\mathcal{F}_{a},\pi_{a})$ and $(\mathcal{F}_{b},\pi_{b})$ to $\mathscr{D}_{a,\alpha}$ and $\mathscr{D}_{b,\beta}$, respectively.
Then, every edge in $(\bigcup\mathcal{F}_{a})\cap(\bigcup\mathcal{F}_{b})$ is adjacent to a vertex in $\{q_{i}\}_{i\in I^{\times}_{\alpha}\cup I^{\times}_{\beta}}$.
Meaning that these solutions can be easily combined.
Assume that $I^{\times}_{\alpha}=I^{\times}_{\beta}$, and let $i\in I^{\times}_{\alpha}$ and set $\mathcal{E}_{i}\coloneqq\mathcal{P}_{\alpha,i}\cup\mathcal{P}_{\beta,i}$ to be the sets of vertices in $X_{s}$ that are connected inside $G_{a}$ or $G_{b}$ in the solution subgraphs associated with index $i$.
Consider the hypergraph $H_{i}\coloneqq(\bigcup\mathcal{E}_{i},\mathcal{E}_{i})$ and set $\mathcal{K}_{i}\coloneqq\{V(K)\mid K\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left(H_{i}\right)}{\compOp(H_{i})}{\compOp(H_{i})}{\compOp(H_{i})}}\}$.
Let $I^{\bot}\coloneqq I^{\bot}_{\alpha}\cup I^{\bot}_{\beta}$ be all indices of subgraphs that are associated with a complete solution subgraph in either $\alpha$ or $\beta$.
We now define the function ${\mathchoice{\mergeOp\left(\alpha,\beta\right)}{\mergeOp(\alpha,\beta)}{\mergeOp(\alpha,\beta)}{\mergeOp(\alpha,\beta)}}\coloneqq(I^{\bot},I^{\times}_{\alpha},I^{\top}_{\alpha}\setminus I^{\bot}_{\beta},(\mathcal{K}_{i})_{i\in I^{\times}_{\alpha}})$.
Intuitively, after merging $\alpha$ and $\beta$, all indices which were completed in either $\alpha$ or $\beta$ are completed after the merge and all solution subgraphs that are associated with an index crossing $X_{s}$ can use connections made in either $G_{a}$ and $G_{b}$.
\par\begin{lemma}We have $$D(s)=\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in D(a),\beta\in D(b)\!\colon\\
I^{\times}_{\alpha}=I^{\times}_{\beta}\end{subarray}}\{{\mathchoice{\mergeOp\left(\alpha,\beta\right)}{\mergeOp(\alpha,\beta)}{\mergeOp(\alpha,\beta)}{\mergeOp(\alpha,\beta)}}\}$$\end{lemma}
\par\@proof Let $\tau\in D(s)$ and let $(\mathcal{F},\pi)$ be a solution to $\mathscr{D}_{s,\tau}$ satisfying the additional requirements of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-solution} in \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw}.
We now show, that there is are $\alpha\in D(a)$ and $\beta\in D(b)$ with $I^{\times}_{\alpha}=I^{\times}_{\beta}$ such that ${\mathchoice{\mergeOp\left(\alpha,\beta\right)}{\mergeOp(\alpha,\beta)}{\mergeOp(\alpha,\beta)}{\mergeOp(\alpha,\beta)}}=\tau$.
For this, we first define $\alpha$ and $\beta$.
Set $I^{\times}_{\alpha}\coloneqq I^{\times}_{\tau}$ and $I^{\times}_{\beta}\coloneqq I^{\times}_{\tau}$.
Additionally, set $I^{\bot}_{\alpha}\coloneqq\{i\in I^{\bot}_{\tau}\mid{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\subseteq Y_{a}\}$ to be the indices of solution subgraphs that are fully contained in $G_{a}$.
Analogously set $I^{\bot}_{\beta}$ and choose $I^{\top}_{\alpha}$ and $I^{\top}_{\beta}$ accordingly.
Consider $i\in I^{\times}_{\alpha}$ and let $\{F\}\coloneqq\pi^{-1}(Q_{s,i})$.
Set $\mathcal{P}_{\alpha,i}\coloneqq\{V(K)\cap X_{a}\mid K\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left(F-q_{i}-(Y_{b}\setminus X_{s})\right)}{\compOp(F-q_{i}-(Y_{b}\setminus X_{s}))}{\compOp(F-q_{i}-(Y_{b}\setminus X_{s}))}{\compOp(F-q_{i}-(Y_{b}\setminus X_{s}))}}\}$ to be the partitions of $X_{a}$ connected inside $G_{b}$ and set $\mathcal{P}_{\beta,i}$ analogously.
\par We now show that ${\mathchoice{\mergeOp\left(\alpha,\beta\right)}{\mergeOp(\alpha,\beta)}{\mergeOp(\alpha,\beta)}{\mergeOp(\alpha,\beta)}}=\tau$.
First, we show $I^{\bot}_{\alpha}\cup I^{\bot}_{\beta}=I^{\bot}_{\tau}$.
As $I^{\bot}_{\alpha}$ and $I^{\bot}_{\beta}$ are subsets of $I^{\bot}_{\tau}$, is suffices to show $I^{\bot}_{\alpha}\cup I^{\bot}_{\beta}\supseteq I^{\bot}_{\tau}$.
Assume there is a $i\in I^{\bot}_{\tau}\setminus(I^{\bot}_{\alpha}\cup I^{\bot}_{\beta})$.
As ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}$ is a terminal set in $\mathscr{D}_{s,\tau}$ with positive demand and this instance is positive, we have ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\subseteq Y_{s}$.
Since $i\notin I^{\bot}_{\alpha}$, there is a $u\in{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\setminus Y_{a}={\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\cap(Y_{b}\setminus X_{s})$.
Similarly, there is a $v\in{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\cap(Y_{a}\setminus X_{s})$.
Let $F\in\pi^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})$ and consider a $uv$ path $P$ in $F$.
As we are considering a tree decomposition, we have $N_{G}(Y_{a})\subseteq X_{s}$ and $N_{G}(Y_{b})\subseteq X_{s}$.
Thus, the path $P$ contains a vertex of $X_{s}$ violating \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-bot-are-complete} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw}.
\par To show ${\mathchoice{\mergeOp\left(\alpha,\beta\right)}{\mergeOp(\alpha,\beta)}{\mergeOp(\alpha,\beta)}{\mergeOp(\alpha,\beta)}}=\tau$, it remains to show that for all $i\in I^{\times}_{\tau}$, we have $\mathcal{P}_{\tau,i}=\mathcal{P}_{{\mathchoice{\mergeOp\left(\alpha,\beta\right)}{\mergeOp(\alpha,\beta)}{\mergeOp(\alpha,\beta)}{\mergeOp(\alpha,\beta)}},i}$.
First, we note that $\bigcup\mathcal{P}_{\tau,i}=\bigcup\mathcal{P}_{\alpha,i}=\bigcup\mathcal{P}_{\beta,i}=\mathcal{P}_{{\mathchoice{\mergeOp\left(\alpha,\beta\right)}{\mergeOp(\alpha,\beta)}{\mergeOp(\alpha,\beta)}{\mergeOp(\alpha,\beta)}},i}$.
Let $\{F\}\coloneqq\pi^{-1}(Q_{s,i})$, $P\in\mathcal{P}_{\tau,i}$, and $u,v\in\mathcal{P}_{\tau,i}$.
There is a simple $uv$-path $R$ in $F-q_{i}$.
Let $x_{1},x_{2},\dots,x_{\ell}$ be the sequence of vertices in $X_{s}$ on $R$.
For all $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[\ell-1\right]}{[\ell-1]}{[\ell-1]}{[\ell-1]}}$, the sub-path in $R$ starting at $x_{i}$ and ending at $x_{i+1}$ only contains inner vertices in either $Y_{a}\setminus X_{s}$ or $Y_{b}\setminus X_{s}$.
Thus, there is a $P\in\mathcal{P}_{\alpha,i}\cup\mathcal{P}_{\beta,i}=\mathcal{E}_{i}$ with $x_{i},x_{i+1}\in P$.
Therefore, in the hypergraph $H_{i}$ there is a $uv$-path and there is a $P^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{{\mathchoice{\mergeOp\left(\alpha,\beta\right)}{\mergeOp(\alpha,\beta)}{\mergeOp(\alpha,\beta)}{\mergeOp(\alpha,\beta)}},i}$ with $u,v\in P^{\prime}$.
Applying this logic in reverse, we get that for all $P\in\mathcal{P}_{{\mathchoice{\mergeOp\left(\alpha,\beta\right)}{\mergeOp(\alpha,\beta)}{\mergeOp(\alpha,\beta)}{\mergeOp(\alpha,\beta)}},i}$ and $u,v\in P$, there is a $P^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{\tau,i}$ with $u,v\in P^{\prime}$.
This shows that $\mathcal{P}_{\tau,i}=\mathcal{P}_{{\mathchoice{\mergeOp\left(\alpha,\beta\right)}{\mergeOp(\alpha,\beta)}{\mergeOp(\alpha,\beta)}{\mergeOp(\alpha,\beta)}},i}$.
\par We now show that $\alpha\in D(a)$.
The proof that $\beta\in D(b)$ can be done analogously.
We now show that \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-started} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw} is satisfied for $\alpha$.
Let $T\in\mathcal{T}$ with $\emptyset\neq T\cap Y_{a}\subseteq T\cap Y_{s}$.
Let $i\in{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}}$.
By \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-started} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw}, we have $i\in I^{\bot}_{\tau}\cup I^{\times}_{\tau}$.
If $i\in I^{\bot}_{\tau}$, we know that $i\in I^{\bot}_{\alpha}\cup I^{\bot}_{\beta}$.
If $i\in I^{\bot}_{\alpha}$, we are done, so assume $i\in I^{\bot}_{\beta}$.
Then, ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\subseteq Y_{b}$.
As $T\cap Y_{a}\neq\emptyset$, we have $T\cap X_{s}\neq\emptyset$.
This is however not possible by \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-bot-are-complete} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw} and since $I^{\times}_{\tau}=I^{\times}_{\alpha}$, we have ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}}\subseteq I^{\bot}_{\alpha}\cup I^{\times}_{\alpha}$.
Hence, \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-started} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw} is satisfied.
\par Now, we provide a solution to $\mathscr{D}_{a,\alpha}$ satisfying the additional requirements of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-solution} in \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw}.
Let $i\in I^{\bot}_{\alpha}\subseteq I^{\bot}_{\tau}$ and consider $F\in\pi^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})$.
As ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\subseteq Y_{a}$, since $V(F)$ is disjoint from $X_{s}$ by \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-bot-are-complete} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw}, and since $N_{G_{s}}(Y_{a})\subseteq X_{s}$, we have $V(F)\subseteq Y_{a}\setminus X_{s}$.
So, we assign $F$ to ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}$ in our solution to $\mathscr{D}_{a,\alpha}$ satisfying the demand of this terminal set as well as \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-bot-are-complete} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw}.
Now, let $i\in I^{\times}_{\alpha}=I^{\times}_{\tau}$ and consider $\{F\}\coloneqq\pi^{-1}(Q_{s,i})$.
Set $F^{*}_{i}$ equal to $F-(Y_{b}\setminus X_{s})+\{q_{i}x\}_{x\in V(F)\cap X_{s}}$, that is, we remove all vertices not contained in $Y_{a}\cup\{q_{i}\}$ and add all edges between $q_{i}$ and vertices of the bag used in $F$.
To see that $F^{*}_{i}$ is connected, note that all $X_{s}\cap V(F^{*}_{i})$ are adjacent to $q_{i}$.
For any $v\in V(F^{*}_{i})\setminus(X_{s}\cup\{q_{i}\})$ there is a $vq_{i}$-path $P$ in $F$.
As $N(q_{i})\subseteq X_{s}$, there is an $x\in X_{s}$ that appears first in $P$.
As $v\in Y_{a}$ and $N_{G_{a,\alpha}}(Y_{a}\setminus X_{s})\subseteq X_{s}$, there is no vertex contained in $Y_{b}\setminus X_{s}$ between $v$ and $x$ on $P$.
Thus, there is a $vx$-path in $F^{*}_{i}$ and $v$ is in the same connected component as $x$ and by extension $q_{i}$, proving that $F^{*}_{i}$ has exactly one connected component.
Furthermore, $Q_{a,i}=Q_{s,i}\setminus(Y_{b}\setminus X_{s})\subseteq V(F^{*}_{i})$.
Consequently, we assign $F^{*}_{i}$ to $Q_{a,i}$ in our solution to $\mathscr{D}_{a,\alpha}$, satisfying the demand of $Q_{a,\alpha}$ and concluding our solution to $\mathscr{D}_{a,\alpha}$.
\par That this solution satisfies \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-bot-are-complete} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw} follows directly from the fact that $(\mathcal{F},\pi)$ satisfies this condition.
Also, for each $i\in I^{\times}_{\alpha}$, it is clear that the solution subgraph $F^{*}_{i}$ is the unique solution subgraph containing $q_{i}$.
Additionally, we have $F^{*}_{i}-q_{i}=F-q_{i}-(Y_{b}\setminus X_{s})$ which shows that \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-crossing} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw} is satisfied, showing that $\alpha\in D(a)$.
\par Now, let $\alpha\in D(a)$ and $\beta\in D(b)$ with $I^{\times}_{\alpha}=I^{\times}_{\beta}$ be given.
We now show that $\tau\coloneqq{\mathchoice{\mergeOp\left(\alpha,\beta\right)}{\mergeOp(\alpha,\beta)}{\mergeOp(\alpha,\beta)}{\mergeOp(\alpha,\beta)}}\in D(s)$.
First, we show that \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-bot-are-complete} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw} is satisfied.
Let $T\in\mathcal{T}$ with $T\cap Y_{s}\neq\emptyset$ and consider $i\in{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}}$.
If $T\cap Y_{a}\neq\emptyset$, we have $i\in I^{\bot}_{\alpha}\cup I^{\times}_{\alpha}\subseteq I^{\bot}_{\tau}\cup I^{\times}_{\tau}$ since $\alpha\in D(a)$.
Otherwise, $T\cap Y_{a}=\emptyset$.
In this case, $T\cap Y_{b}\neq\emptyset$ and $i\in I^{\bot}_{\beta}\cup I^{\times}_{\beta}\subseteq I^{\bot}_{\tau}\cup I^{\times}_{\tau}$, showing that \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-bot-are-complete} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw} is satisfied.
\par Next, we construct a solution to $\mathscr{D}_{s,\tau}$ satisfying the additional requirements of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-solution} in \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw}.
For this, let $(\mathcal{F}_{a},\pi_{a})$ and $(\mathcal{F}_{b},\pi_{b})$ be solutions to $\mathscr{D}_{a,\alpha}$ and $\mathscr{D}_{b,\beta}$ satisfying the additional requirements of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-solution} in \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw}, respectively.
Let $i\in I^{\bot}_{\tau}=I^{\bot}_{\alpha}\cup I^{\bot}_{\beta}$.
Assume without loss of generality that $i\in I^{\bot}_{\alpha}$ and let $F\in\pi^{-1}_{a}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})$.
As $F$ is completely contained in $G_{a}[Y_{a}\setminus X_{s}]$, it is also completely contained in $G_{s}[Y_{s}\setminus X_{s}]$ and we assign all such $F$ to ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}$ in $\mathscr{D}_{s,\tau}$.
To see that this already satisfies the demand of ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}$, note that the demand of ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}$ in $\mathscr{D}_{s,\tau}$ is ${\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}}\cap I^{\bot}_{\tau}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}}\cap I^{\bot}_{\tau}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}}\cap I^{\bot}_{\tau}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}}\cap I^{\bot}_{\tau}|}}}\leq{\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}}\cap I^{\bot}_{\alpha}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}}\cap I^{\bot}_{\alpha}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}}\cap I^{\bot}_{\alpha}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}}\cap I^{\bot}_{\alpha}|}}}+{\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}}\cap I^{\bot}_{\beta}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}}\cap I^{\bot}_{\beta}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}}\cap I^{\bot}_{\beta}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}}\cap I^{\bot}_{\beta}|}}}$ and we assign ${\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}}\cap I^{\bot}_{\alpha}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}}\cap I^{\bot}_{\alpha}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}}\cap I^{\bot}_{\alpha}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}}\cap I^{\bot}_{\alpha}|}}}$ solution subgraphs.
As ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\subseteq V(F)\subseteq Y_{a}\setminus X_{a}$, we have ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\cap Y_{b}\setminus X_{b}=\emptyset$.
Thus, ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(i)}}\cap I^{\bot}_{\beta}=\emptyset$ and the demand of ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}$ is satisfied in our solution.
Apply the analogous argument if $i\in I^{\bot}_{\beta}$ to see that for all $i\in I^{\bot}_{\tau}$ the demand of ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}$ is satisfied.
Note that this construction directly satisfies \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-bot-are-complete} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw} for our solution.
\par Let $i\in I^{\times}_{\tau}=I^{\times}_{\alpha}=I^{\times}_{\beta}$, $\{F_{a,i}\}\coloneqq\pi^{-1}_{a}(Q_{a,i})$, and $\{F_{b,i}\}\coloneqq\pi^{-1}_{b}(Q_{b,i})$.
Consider $F^{*}_{i}\coloneqq F_{a}\cup F_{b}$.
As both $F_{a}$ and $F_{b}$ are connected and contain $q_{i}$, $F^{*}_{i}$ is connected as well.
Additionally, $Q_{s,i}=\{q_{i}\}\cup({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\cap Y_{s})=\{q_{i}\}\cup({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\cap Y_{a})\cup({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\cap Y_{b})=Q_{a,i}\cup Q_{b,i}\subseteq V(F^{*}_{i})$.
So, we assign $F^{*}_{i}$ to $Q_{s,i}$ in our solution to $\mathscr{D}_{s,\tau}$, satisfying the demand of $Q_{s,i}$, and concluding the description of our solution to $\mathscr{D}_{s,\tau}$.
\par It remains to show that \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-crossing} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw} is satisfied.
For this, let $i\in I^{\times}_{\tau}$.
For each $P\in\mathcal{P}_{\alpha,i}$ the vertices in $P$ are in a connected component of $F_{a}$ and for each $P\in\mathcal{P}_{\beta,i}$ the vertices in $P$ are in a connected component of $F_{b}$.
Thus, if there is a path in $H_{i}$ between two vertices in $X_{s}$, there is a path in $F^{*}_{i}$ between these vertices.
Now, let $K\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left(F^{*}_{i}-q_{i}\right)}{\compOp(F^{*}_{i}-q_{i})}{\compOp(F^{*}_{i}-q_{i})}{\compOp(F^{*}_{i}-q_{i})}}$, $u,v\in V(K)\cap X_{s}$, and $P$ be a $uv$-path in $K$.
Consider the sequence of $x_{1},x_{2},\dots,x_{\ell}$ of vertices in $X_{s}$ on $P$.
For each $i\in{\mathchoice{\left[\ell-1\right]}{[\ell-1]}{[\ell-1]}{[\ell-1]}}$, there is a component in either $F^{*}_{i}-q_{i}-(Y_{b}\setminus X_{s})=F_{a,i}-q_{i}$ of $F^{*}_{i}-q_{i}-(Y_{a}\setminus X_{s})=F_{b,i}-q_{i}$ containing both $x_{i}$ and $x_{i+1}$.
That is, there is a $R\in\mathcal{E}_{i}$ with $x_{i},x_{i+1}\in R$.
Therefore, $x_{i}$ and $x_{i+1}$ are in the same component of $H_{i}$; in particular, $u$ and $v$ are in the same component of $H_{i}$, showing that \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-crossing} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw} is satisfied and that $\tau\in D(s)$.
\qed\end@proof
\par This gives us an easy and efficient way to compute the dynamic program for join nodes.
\par\begin{corollary}The dynamic program for a join node with children $a$ and $b$ can be computed in time ${\mathchoice{\OOp\left(1+({\Sigma_{d}}+w){\mathchoice{\left|D(a)\right|}{{|D(a)|}}{{|D(a)|}}{{|D(a)|}}}{\mathchoice{\left|D(b)\right|}{{|D(b)|}}{{|D(b)|}}{{|D(b)|}}}\right)}{\OOp(1+({\Sigma_{d}}+w){\mathchoice{\left|D(a)\right|}{{|D(a)|}}{{|D(a)|}}{{|D(a)|}}}{\mathchoice{\left|D(b)\right|}{{|D(b)|}}{{|D(b)|}}{{|D(b)|}}})}{\OOp(1+({\Sigma_{d}}+w){\mathchoice{\left|D(a)\right|}{{|D(a)|}}{{|D(a)|}}{{|D(a)|}}}{\mathchoice{\left|D(b)\right|}{{|D(b)|}}{{|D(b)|}}{{|D(b)|}}})}{\OOp(1+({\Sigma_{d}}+w){\mathchoice{\left|D(a)\right|}{{|D(a)|}}{{|D(a)|}}{{|D(a)|}}}{\mathchoice{\left|D(b)\right|}{{|D(b)|}}{{|D(b)|}}{{|D(b)|}}})}}$ given that $D(a)$ and $D(b)$ are provided.
\end{corollary}
\par\par\@@unnumbered@section{paragraph}{Forget node} Let $s\in V(S)$ be a forget node, that is it has a child $c$ and there is a $v\in X_{c}$ with $X_{s}=X_{c}\setminus\{v\}$.
Let $E_{v}\coloneqq\{vx\}_{x\in N(v)\cap X_{s}}$ to be the set of edges incident to $v$ and a vertex in $X_{s}$.
Then, we have that $G_{s}=G_{c}+E_{v}$.
Let $\gamma\in D(c)$.
Consider a solution $(\mathcal{F},\pi)$ to $\mathscr{D}_{c,\gamma}$ satisfying the additional requirements of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-solution} in \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw}.
We need to distribute the additional edges among the solution subgraphs which cross $X_{c}$.
Consider a function $\lambda\colon E_{v}\to I^{\times}_{\gamma}\cup\{\blockedIndex\}$ where $\blockedIndex$ is a symbol indicating that the edge is not used and for all $i\in I^{\times}_{\gamma}$, we add the edges $\lambda^{-1}(i)$ to $\pi^{-1}(Q_{c,i})$.
\par To see how this effects the solution state, let $i\in I^{\times}_{\tau}$ and $\mathcal{E}_{i}\coloneqq\lambda^{-1}(i)\cup\mathcal{P}_{i}$.
Consider the hypergraph $H_{i}\coloneqq(\{v\}\cup\mathcal{E}_{i},\mathcal{E}_{i})$ this is, intuitively speaking, the hypergraph on the vertices of $X_{c}$ with the edges incident to $v$ designated for this solution subgraph combined with the edges that correspond to connections already completed in $G_{c}$.
Now, we set $\mathcal{P}_{i}^{\prime}\coloneqq\{V(K)\cap X_{s}\mid K\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left(H_{i}\right)}{\compOp(H_{i})}{\compOp(H_{i})}{\compOp(H_{i})}}\}$ to be the vertex sets of the components of $H_{i}$ with $v$ removed.
Set $C_{\gamma,\lambda}\coloneqq\{i\in I^{\times}_{\gamma}\mid\mathcal{P}_{i}^{\prime}=\{\emptyset\}\}$ to be the indices that cross $X_{c}$ but not $X_{s}$ under this edge distribution.
We now define the function ${\mathchoice{\distributeOp\left(\gamma,\lambda\right)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\lambda)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\lambda)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\lambda)}}\coloneqq(I^{\bot}_{\gamma}\cup C_{\gamma,\lambda},I^{\times}_{\gamma}\setminus C_{\gamma,\lambda},I^{\top}_{\gamma},(\mathcal{P}_{i}^{\prime})_{i\in I^{\times}_{\gamma}\setminus C_{\gamma,\lambda}})$.
This function describes the state of the dynamic program after forgetting $v$ and distributing the newly available edges among the solution subgraphs according to $\lambda$.
When distributing edges, we must watch out not to disconnect solution subgraphs that are not yet finished.
\par\begin{lemma}We have $$D(s)=\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in D(c),\\
\lambda\colon E_{v}\to I^{\times}_{\gamma}\cup\{\blockedIndex\}\!\colon\\
\forall i\in C_{\gamma,\lambda}\colon{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\subseteq Y_{c}\end{subarray}}\{{\mathchoice{\distributeOp\left(\gamma,\lambda\right)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\lambda)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\lambda)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\lambda)}}\}.$$\end{lemma}
\@proof Let $\tau\in D(s)$ and let $(\mathcal{F},\pi)$ be a solution to $\mathscr{D}_{s,\tau}$ satisfying the additional requirements of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-solution} in \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw}.
We now show that there is are $\gamma\in D(c)$ and $\lambda\colon E_{v}\to I^{\times}_{\gamma}\cup\{\blockedIndex\}$ such that ${\mathchoice{\distributeOp\left(\gamma,\lambda\right)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\lambda)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\lambda)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\lambda)}}=\tau$ and that for all $i\in C_{\gamma,\lambda}$, we have ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\subseteq Y_{c}$.
For this, we first define $\gamma$ and $\lambda$.
\par Denote with $\mathcal{F}_{v}\coloneqq\{F\in\mathcal{F}\mid\{v\}=V(F)\cap X_{c}\}$ the set of solution subgraphs that contain $v$ but none of $X_{s}$.
For all $F\in\mathcal{F}_{v}$, the demand of $\pi(F)$ is exactly ${\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left(\pi(F)\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(\pi(F))}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(\pi(F))}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(\pi(F))}}\cap I^{\bot}_{\tau}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left(\pi(F)\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(\pi(F))}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(\pi(F))}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(\pi(F))}}\cap I^{\bot}_{\tau}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left(\pi(F)\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(\pi(F))}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(\pi(F))}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(\pi(F))}}\cap I^{\bot}_{\tau}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left(\pi(F)\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(\pi(F))}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(\pi(F))}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(\pi(F))}}\cap I^{\bot}_{\tau}|}}}$.
So, we are able to choose a function $\sigma\colon\mathcal{F}_{v}\to I^{\bot}_{\tau}$ such that for all $F\in\mathcal{F}_{v}$, we have $\pi(F)={\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(\sigma(F)\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(\sigma(F))}{\enumerateTerminalOp(\sigma(F))}{\enumerateTerminalOp(\sigma(F))}}$.
We now set $I^{\bot}_{\gamma}\coloneqq I^{\bot}_{\tau}\setminus{\mathchoice{\imageOp\left(\sigma\right)}{\imageOp(\sigma)}{\imageOp(\sigma)}{\imageOp(\sigma)}}$, $I^{\times}_{\gamma}\coloneqq I^{\times}_{\tau}\cup{\mathchoice{\imageOp\left(\sigma\right)}{\imageOp(\sigma)}{\imageOp(\sigma)}{\imageOp(\sigma)}}$, and $I^{\top}_{\gamma}\coloneqq I^{\top}_{\tau}$.
Let $i\in I^{\times}_{\tau}$.
We set $\mathcal{P}_{\gamma,i}\coloneqq\{V(K)\cap X_{c}\mid K\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left(\pi^{-1}(Q_{s,i})-E_{v}-q_{i}\right)}{\compOp(\pi^{-1}(Q_{s,i})-E_{v}-q_{i})}{\compOp(\pi^{-1}(Q_{s,i})-E_{v}-q_{i})}{\compOp(\pi^{-1}(Q_{s,i})-E_{v}-q_{i})}}\}$ to be the connected components of the solution subgraph assigned to $Q_{s,i}$ with $q_{i}$ and all edges adjacent to $v$ removed.
For $i\in{\mathchoice{\imageOp\left(\sigma\right)}{\imageOp(\sigma)}{\imageOp(\sigma)}{\imageOp(\sigma)}}$, we set $\mathcal{P}_{\gamma,i}\coloneqq\{\{v\}\}$ to be the partitioning of $X_{c}$ only containing the partition $\{v\}$.
To define $\alpha$, consider any $e\in E_{v}$.
If there is a $F\in\mathcal{F}_{v}$ with $e\in E(F)$, we set $\alpha(e)\coloneqq\sigma(e)$.
If there is a $F\in\mathcal{F}\setminus\mathcal{F}_{v}$ with $e\in E(F)$, let $i\in I^{\times}_{\tau}$ be the unique $i$ with $q_{i}\in V(F)$.
We set $\alpha(e)\coloneqq i$.
Otherwise, we set $\alpha(e)\coloneqq\blockedIndex$.
\par To show ${\mathchoice{\distributeOp\left(\gamma,\lambda\right)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\lambda)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\lambda)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\lambda)}}=\tau$, we first note that $C_{\gamma,\lambda}={\mathchoice{\imageOp\left(\sigma\right)}{\imageOp(\sigma)}{\imageOp(\sigma)}{\imageOp(\sigma)}}$.
With this knowledge, we see that $I^{\bot}_{{\mathchoice{\distributeOp\left(\gamma,\lambda\right)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\lambda)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\lambda)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\lambda)}}}=I^{\bot}_{\tau}$, $I^{\times}_{{\mathchoice{\distributeOp\left(\gamma,\lambda\right)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\lambda)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\lambda)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\lambda)}}}=I^{\times}_{\tau}$, and $I^{\top}_{{\mathchoice{\distributeOp\left(\gamma,\lambda\right)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\lambda)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\lambda)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\lambda)}}}=I^{\top}_{\tau}$.
Now, let $i\in I^{\times}_{\tau}$.
By definition, we know that $\bigcup\mathcal{P}_{{\mathchoice{\distributeOp\left(\gamma,\lambda\right)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\lambda)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\lambda)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\lambda)}},i}=V(\pi^{-1}(Q_{s,i}))\cap X_{s}$; by \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-crossing} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw}, we know $V(\pi^{-1}(Q_{s,i}))\cap X_{s}=\bigcup\mathcal{P}_{\tau,i}$, yielding $\bigcup\mathcal{P}_{{\mathchoice{\distributeOp\left(\gamma,\lambda\right)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\lambda)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\lambda)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\lambda)}},i}=\bigcup\mathcal{P}_{\tau,i}$.
It remains to show for $x,y\in\bigcup\mathcal{P}_{\tau,i}$ that there is a $P\in\mathcal{P}_{\tau,i}$ with $x,y\in P$ if and only if there is a $P^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{{\mathchoice{\distributeOp\left(\gamma,\lambda\right)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\lambda)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\lambda)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\lambda)}},i}$ with $x,y\in P^{\prime}$.
Assume there is a $P\in\mathcal{P}_{\tau,i}$ with $x,y\in P$.
By \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-crossing} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw}, this is exactly the case if there is a $K\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left(\pi^{-1}(Q_{s,i})-q_{i}\right)}{\compOp(\pi^{-1}(Q_{s,i})-q_{i})}{\compOp(\pi^{-1}(Q_{s,i})-q_{i})}{\compOp(\pi^{-1}(Q_{s,i})-q_{i})}}$ with $x,y\in V(K)\cap X_{s}$.
So, there is a path $xy$-path $P$ in $\pi^{-1}(Q_{s,i})$ not containing the vertex $q_{i}$.
Consider the vertices $q_{1},q_{2},\dots,q_{\ell}$ of $P$ in $X_{c}$ in order.
For all $k\in{\mathchoice{\left[\ell-1\right]}{[\ell-1]}{[\ell-1]}{[\ell-1]}}$, if $q_{k}$ and $q_{k+1}$ are directly connected by an edge, then, as $X_{s}$ is an independent set in $G_{s}$, either $q_{k}=v$ or $q_{k+1}=v$.
By definition of $\alpha$, we have $\alpha(q_{k}q_{k+1})=i$; meaning $\{q_{k},q_{k+1}\}\in\mathcal{E}_{i}$.
Otherwise, $q_{k}$ and $q_{k+1}$ are connected by a path in $\pi^{-1}(Q_{s,i})-q_{i}-E_{v}$; so, there is a $K\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left(\pi^{-1}(Q_{s,i})-q_{i}-E_{v}\right)}{\compOp(\pi^{-1}(Q_{s,i})-q_{i}-E_{v})}{\compOp(\pi^{-1}(Q_{s,i})-q_{i}-E_{v})}{\compOp(\pi^{-1}(Q_{s,i})-q_{i}-E_{v})}}$ with $q_{k},q_{k+1}\in V(K)\cap X_{c}$.
As $V(K)\cap X_{c}\in\mathcal{E}_{i}$, in either case $q_{k}$ and $q_{k+1}$ are adjacent by a hyperedge in $\mathcal{E}_{i}$.
Thus, $x$ and $y$ are in the same connected component of $H_{i}$ and there is a $P^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{{\mathchoice{\distributeOp\left(\gamma,\alpha\right)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\alpha)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\alpha)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\alpha)}},i}$ with $x,y\in P^{\prime}$.
Applying these arguments in reverse shows the claimed equivalence and, by extension, ${\mathchoice{\distributeOp\left(\gamma,\alpha\right)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\alpha)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\alpha)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\alpha)}}=\tau$.
\par Now, let $i\in C_{\gamma,\lambda}={\mathchoice{\imageOp\left(\sigma\right)}{\imageOp(\sigma)}{\imageOp(\sigma)}{\imageOp(\sigma)}}$.
If $i\in I^{\times}_{\tau}$, let $\{F\}\coloneqq\pi^{-1}(Q_{s,i})$.
We have that $v\in V(F)$ and that $q_{i}\in V(F)$.
Additionally, $V(F)$ contains no vertex in $X_{s}$.
As $N_{G_{s,\tau}}(q_{i})=X_{s}$, there is no $vq_{i}$-path contained in $F$ and so $F$ is disconnected, which is a contradiction.
Thus, $i\in I^{\bot}_{\tau}$ and ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\subseteq V(G_{s})=Y_{s}=Y_{c}$.
\par It remains to show that $\gamma\in D(c)$.
We now show \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-started} and \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-solution} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw} separately for $\gamma$.
First, consider any $T\in\mathcal{T}$ with $T\cap Y_{c}\neq\emptyset$.
We know that $Y_{c}=Y_{s}$; so, by \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-started} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw}, we know that ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}}\subseteq I^{\bot}_{\tau}\cup I^{\times}_{\tau}$.
Noting that $I^{\bot}_{\gamma}\cup I^{\times}_{\gamma}=I^{\bot}_{\tau}\cup I^{\times}_{\tau}$, we get ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}}\subseteq I^{\bot}_{\gamma}\cup I^{\times}_{\gamma}$. Hence \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-started} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw} is satisfied for $\gamma$.
\par To show \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-solution} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw} for $\gamma$, we construct a solution to $\mathscr{D}_{c,\gamma}$ satisfying \lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-bot-are-complete} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{item:tw-dp-crossing} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw}.

I^{\bot}_{\tau}|}}}-{\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}}\cap{\mathchoice{\imageOp\left(\sigma\right)}{\imageOp(\sigma)}{\imageOp(\sigma)}{\imageOp(\sigma)}}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}}\cap{\mathchoice{\imageOp\left(\sigma\right)}{\imageOp(\sigma)}{\imageOp(\sigma)}{\imageOp(\sigma)}}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTermina
