Additionally, $\sigma^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})\subseteq{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}}$ implies that $\sigma^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})={\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}}\cap{\mathchoice{\imageOp\left(\sigma\right)}{\imageOp(\sigma)}{\imageOp(\sigma)}{\imageOp(\sigma)}}$.
Thus, the demand of ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}$ is satisfied by assigning all such $F$.
\par Now, let $i\in I^{\times}_{\gamma}$.
If $i\in{\mathchoice{\imageOp\left(\sigma\right)}{\imageOp(\sigma)}{\imageOp(\sigma)}{\imageOp(\sigma)}}$.
Let $\{F\}\coloneqq\sigma^{-1}(i)$ and set $F^{*}_{i}\coloneqq F+vq_{i}$, which is connected as $v\in V(F)$.
By choice of $\sigma$, we have $\pi(F)={\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}$.
Thus, $Q_{c,i}=\{q_{i}\}\cup({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\cap Y_{c})\subseteq\{q_{i}\}\cup\pi(F)\subseteq V(F^{*}_{i})$.
Consequently, we assign $F^{*}_{i}$ to $Q_{c,i}$ in our solution.
If $i\notin{\mathchoice{\imageOp\left(\sigma\right)}{\imageOp(\sigma)}{\imageOp(\sigma)}{\imageOp(\sigma)}}$, we have $i\in I^{\times}_{\tau}$.
Let $\{F\}\coloneqq\pi^{-1}(Q_{s,i})$.
We set $F^{*}_{i}\coloneqq F-E_{v}+\{xq_{i}\mid x\in X_{c}\}$.
If $F-E_{v}$ is disconnected, for every $K\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left(F-E_{v}\right)}{\compOp(F-E_{v})}{\compOp(F-E_{v})}{\compOp(F-E_{v})}}$ there is an $x\in V(K)$ that incident to an edge $e\in E_{v}$.
As $E_{v}$ only contains edges inside $X_{c}$, we have $x\in X_{c}$.
Thus, $V(K)\cap X_{c}\neq\emptyset$ and in $F^{*}_{i}$, every connected component contains $q_{i}$ showing that $F^{*}_{i}$ is connected.
By \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-solution} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw}, this $F^{*}_{i}$ is edge-disjoint from all $\{F^{*}_{j}\}_{j\in I^{\times}_{\gamma}}$ and all other already assigned solution subgraphs.
Additionally, notice that $Q_{c,i}\subseteq Q_{s,i}\subseteq V(F)\subseteq V(F^{*}_{i})$; thus, we assign $F^{*}_{i}$ to $Q_{c,i}$ in our solution to $\mathscr{D}_{c,\gamma}$ completing the description of our solution description.
\par We finally show that \lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-bot-are-complete} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{item:tw-dp-crossing} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw} are satisfied.
That \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-bot-are-complete} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw} is satisfied directly follows from the fact that $(\mathcal{F},\pi)$ satisfies this condition with respect to $\tau$ and $s$.
Now, let $i\in I^{\times}_{\gamma}$.
It follows by construction and by the fact that $\tau$ satisfies \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-crossing} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw}, that $F^{*}_{i}$ is the unique solution subgraph containing the vertex $q_{i}$.
It remains to show that $\mathcal{P}_{\gamma,i}=\{V(K)\cap X_{c}\mid K\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left(F^{*}_{i}-q_{i}\right)}{\compOp(F^{*}_{i}-q_{i})}{\compOp(F^{*}_{i}-q_{i})}{\compOp(F^{*}_{i}-q_{i})}}\}$.
If $i\in{\mathchoice{\imageOp\left(\sigma\right)}{\imageOp(\sigma)}{\imageOp(\sigma)}{\imageOp(\sigma)}}$, let $\{F\}\coloneqq\sigma^{-1}(i)$.
As $F\in\mathcal{F}_{v}$, we have $V(F)\cap X_{c}=\{v\}$.
So, $\{V(K)\cap X_{c}\mid K\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left(F^{*}_{i}-q_{i}\right)}{\compOp(F^{*}_{i}-q_{i})}{\compOp(F^{*}_{i}-q_{i})}{\compOp(F^{*}_{i}-q_{i})}}\}=\{\{v\}\}=\mathcal{P}_{\gamma,i}$.
If $i\notin{\mathchoice{\imageOp\left(\sigma\right)}{\imageOp(\sigma)}{\imageOp(\sigma)}{\imageOp(\sigma)}}$, we have that $i\in I^{\times}_{\tau}$.
By definition, we have $\mathcal{P}_{\gamma,i}=\{V(K)\cap X_{c}\mid K\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left(F^{*}_{i}-E_{v}-q_{i}\right)}{\compOp(F^{*}_{i}-E_{v}-q_{i})}{\compOp(F^{*}_{i}-E_{v}-q_{i})}{\compOp(F^{*}_{i}-E_{v}-q_{i})}}\}$.
Since, $F^{*}_{i}=F-E_{v}+\{xq_{i}\mid x\in X_{c}\}$ there is no edge of $E_{v}$ contained in $F^{*}_{i}$; meaning that $F^{*}_{i}-E_{v}-q_{i}=F^{*}_{i}-q_{i}$, which shows the desired equality and that $\gamma\in D(c)$.
\par Now, let $\gamma\in D(c)$ and $\alpha\colon E_{v}\to I^{\times}_{\gamma}\cup\{\blockedIndex\}$ be given, such that for all $i\in C_{\gamma,\lambda}$ we have that ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\subseteq Y_{c}$.
To show $\tau\coloneqq{\mathchoice{\distributeOp\left(\gamma,\alpha\right)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\alpha)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\alpha)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\alpha)}}\in D(s)$, we show \lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-started} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{item:tw-dp-solution} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw} separately.
First, let $T\in\mathcal{T}$ with $T\cap Y_{s}\neq\emptyset$ be given.
By \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-started} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw} applied to $\gamma$, we know that ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}}\subseteq I^{\bot}_{\gamma}\cup I^{\times}_{\gamma}$.
As $I^{\bot}_{\gamma}\cup I^{\times}_{\gamma}=I^{\bot}_{\tau}\cup I^{\times}_{\tau}$, this shows ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left(T\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}(T)}}\subseteq I^{\bot}_{\tau}\cup I^{\times}_{\tau}$.
\par Now, we provide a solution to $\mathscr{D}_{s,\tau}$ satisfying the additional requirements of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-solution} in \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw}.
For this, let $(\mathcal{F},\pi)$ be a solution to $\mathscr{D}_{c,\gamma}$ satisfying the additional requirements of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-solution} in \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw}.
First, let $i\in I^{\bot}_{\gamma}$ and consider $F\in\pi^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})$.
As $F$ is completely contained in $G_{c}$, it is also completely contained in $G_{s}$ and we assign $F$ to ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}$ in our solution for $\mathscr{D}_{s,\tau}$.
Now, let $i\in C_{\gamma,\lambda}$ and set $\{F\}\coloneqq\pi^{-1}(Q_{c,i})$.
By \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-crossing} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw} and choice of $C_{\gamma,\lambda}$, we know that $V(F)$ is disjoint from $X_{s}$.
Thus, $F-q_{i}$ is contained in $G_{s}$.
Since ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\subseteq Y_{c}$, we have ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}={\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\cap Y_{c}=Q_{c,i}\setminus\{q_{i}\}\subseteq V(F-q_{i})$.
Consequently, we assign $F-q_{i}$ to ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}$.
\par Now, let $i\in I^{\bot}_{\tau}$.
We assign $d_{\mathscr{D}_{c,\gamma}}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})+{\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}}\cap C_{\gamma,\lambda}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}}\cap C_{\gamma,\lambda}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}}\cap C_{\gamma,\lambda}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}}\cap C_{\gamma,\lambda}|}}}$ solution subgraphs to ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}$ in our solution to $\mathscr{D}_{s,\tau}$.
As $d_{\mathscr{D}_{c,\gamma}}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})={\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}}\cap I^{\bot}_{\gamma}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}}\cap I^{\bot}_{\gamma}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}}\cap I^{\bot}_{\gamma}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}}\cap I^{\bot}_{\gamma}|}}}$ and since $I^{\bot}_{\gamma}$ is disjoint from $C_{\gamma,\lambda}$, we assign ${\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}}\cap(I^{\bot}_{\gamma}\cup C_{\gamma,\lambda})\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}}\cap(I^{\bot}_{\gamma}\cup C_{\gamma,\lambda})|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}}\cap(I^{\bot}_{\gamma}\cup C_{\gamma,\lambda})|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}}\cap(I^{\bot}_{\gamma}\cup C_{\gamma,\lambda})|}}}={\mathchoice{\left|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}}\cap I^{\bot}_{\tau}\right|}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}}\cap I^{\bot}_{\tau}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}}\cap I^{\bot}_{\tau}|}}{{|{\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}\left({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}{\enumerateTerminalOp^{-1}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})}}\cap I^{\bot}_{\tau}|}}}=d_{\mathscr{D}_{s,\tau}}({\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}})$ solution subgraphs to ${\mathchoice{\enumerateTerminalOp\left(i\right)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}{\enumerateTerminalOp(i)}}$ satisfying its demand.
\par Finally, consider $i\in I^{\times}_{\tau}=I^{\times}_{\gamma}\setminus C_{\gamma,\lambda}$.
Let $\{F\}\coloneqq\pi^{-1}(Q_{c,i})$ and set $F^{*}_{i}\coloneqq F+\lambda^{-1}(i)+\{xq_{i}\mid x\in X_{s}\}$.
Since $i\notin C_{\gamma,\lambda}$, there is an $x\in X_{s}\cap V(F)$ and so $F^{*}_{i}$ is connected.
By \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-crossing} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw}, we know that all $\{F^{*}_{j}\}_{j\in I^{\times}_{\tau}}$ are edge-disjoint from each other and all previously assigned solution subgraphs.
Additionally, $Q_{c,i}=Q_{s,i}\subseteq V(F)\subseteq V(F^{*}_{i})$; so, we assign $F^{*}_{i}$ to the terminal set $Q_{s,i}$ concluding the description of our solution to $\mathscr{D}_{s,\tau}$.
By construction this solution already satisfies \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-bot-are-complete} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw}.
\par We also see that $F^{*}_{i}$ is the unique solution subgraph containing $q_{i}$.
It remains to show that $\{V(K)\cap X_{s}\mid K\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left(F^{*}_{i}-q_{i}\right)}{\compOp(F^{*}_{i}-q_{i})}{\compOp(F^{*}_{i}-q_{i})}{\compOp(F^{*}_{i}-q_{i})}}\}=\mathcal{P}_{{\mathchoice{\distributeOp\left(\gamma,\alpha\right)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\alpha)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\alpha)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\alpha)}},i}$.
First, we see that $\bigcup_{K\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left(F^{*}_{i}-q_{i}\right)}{\compOp(F^{*}_{i}-q_{i})}{\compOp(F^{*}_{i}-q_{i})}{\compOp(F^{*}_{i}-q_{i})}}}V(K)\cap X_{s}=V(F^{*}_{i})\cap X_{s}$ and that $\bigcup\mathcal{P}_{{\mathchoice{\distributeOp\left(\gamma,\alpha\right)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\alpha)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\alpha)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\alpha)}},i}=\{V(K)\cap X_{s}\mid K\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left(H_{i}\right)}{\compOp(H_{i})}{\compOp(H_{i})}{\compOp(H_{i})}}\}=X_{s}\cap(\{v\}\cup\{x\mid vx\in\lambda^{-1}(i)\}\cup\bigcup\mathcal{P}_{\gamma,i})$.
The vertices $V(F^{*}_{i})\cap X_{s}$ are exactly the endpoints of all $\lambda^{-1}(i)$ which are not $v$ and the vertices $V(F)\cap X_{s}$; which, by \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{item:tw-dp-crossing} of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{def:dp-tw}, are exactly $\bigcup\mathcal{P}_{\gamma,i}$.
Therefore, $V(F^{*}_{i})\cap X_{s}=\bigcup\mathcal{P}_{{\mathchoice{\distributeOp\left(\gamma,\alpha\right)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\alpha)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\alpha)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\alpha)}},i}$.
We now show that for all $x,y\in V(F^{*}_{i})\cap X_{s}$ there is a $K\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left(F^{*}_{i}-q_{i}\right)}{\compOp(F^{*}_{i}-q_{i})}{\compOp(F^{*}_{i}-q_{i})}{\compOp(F^{*}_{i}-q_{i})}}$ with $x,y\in V(K)$ if and only if there is a $P\in\mathcal{P}_{{\mathchoice{\distributeOp\left(\gamma,\alpha\right)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\alpha)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\alpha)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\alpha)}},i}$ with $x,y\in P$, completing this proof.
Consider $x,y\in V(F^{*}_{i})\cap X_{s}$ such that there is a $K\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left(F^{*}_{i}-q_{i}\right)}{\compOp(F^{*}_{i}-q_{i})}{\compOp(F^{*}_{i}-q_{i})}{\compOp(F^{*}_{i}-q_{i})}}$ with $x,y\in V(K)$.
This is exactly the case when there is a simple $xy$-path $P$ in $F^{*}_{i}$ not containing $q_{i}$.
Denote with $x_{1},x_{2},\dots,x_{\ell}$ the vertices of $X_{c}$ on $P$ in order.
For all $k\in{\mathchoice{\left[\ell-1\right]}{[\ell-1]}{[\ell-1]}{[\ell-1]}}$, either $x_{k}$ and $x_{k+1}$ are directly connected by an edge in $\lambda^{-1}(i)$, or there is a path connecting $x_{k}$ and $x_{k+1}$ in $F$.
Therefore, $x_{k}$ and $x_{k+1}$ are adjacent in $H_{i}$, meaning that $x,y$ are in the same connected component of $H_{i}$.
Applying, this reasoning in reverse shows that $\{V(K)\cap X_{s}\mid K\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left(F^{*}_{i}-q_{i}\right)}{\compOp(F^{*}_{i}-q_{i})}{\compOp(F^{*}_{i}-q_{i})}{\compOp(F^{*}_{i}-q_{i})}}\}=\mathcal{P}_{{\mathchoice{\distributeOp\left(\gamma,\alpha\right)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\alpha)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\alpha)}{\distributeOp(\gamma,\alpha)}},i}$.
\qed\end@proof
\par The last lemma allows us to easily and efficiently compute $D(s)$ based on $D(c)$.
\par\begin{corollary}The dynamic program for a forget node with child $c$ can be computed in time ${\mathchoice{\OOp\left(1+({\Sigma_{d}}+w)({\Sigma_{d}}+1)^{w}{\mathchoice{\left|D(c)\right|}{{|D(c)|}}{{|D(c)|}}{{|D(c)|}}}\right)}{\OOp(1+({\Sigma_{d}}+w)({\Sigma_{d}}+1)^{w}{\mathchoice{\left|D(c)\right|}{{|D(c)|}}{{|D(c)|}}{{|D(c)|}}})}{\OOp(1+({\Sigma_{d}}+w)({\Sigma_{d}}+1)^{w}{\mathchoice{\left|D(c)\right|}{{|D(c)|}}{{|D(c)|}}{{|D(c)|}}})}{\OOp(1+({\Sigma_{d}}+w)({\Sigma_{d}}+1)^{w}{\mathchoice{\left|D(c)\right|}{{|D(c)|}}{{|D(c)|}}{{|D(c)|}}})}}$ given that $D(c)$ is provided.
\end{corollary}
\par Based on \lx@cref{creftypepluralcap~refnum}{stmt:tw-dp-introduce-running-time}, \lx@cref{refnum}{stmt:tw-dp-join-running-time} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{stmt:tw-dp-forget-running-time} we are able to compute the dynamic program for the whole tree decomposition.
To be able to easily compute leaf nodes, we modify the given tree decomposition by extending leaves that are not empty to actually introduce one of its vertices.
We do this until all leaf nodes are empty.
Finally, we extend the root node to forget its nodes until the root node is empty.
Using \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:tw-dp-output-correct}, this allows to decide whether the instance is positive.
We note that for all $s\in V(S)$, we have ${\mathchoice{\left|D(s)\right|}{{|D(s)|}}{{|D(s)|}}{{|D(s)|}}}\leq 2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\Sigma_{d}}w\log w\right)}{\OOp({\Sigma_{d}}w\log w)}{\OOp({\Sigma_{d}}w\log w)}{\OOp({\Sigma_{d}}w\log w)}}$.
This allows us to bound the running time.
\par\begin{corollary}Given a nice tree decomposition of width $w$, we can compute whether the instance is positive in time ${\mathchoice{\left|V(S)\right|}{{|V(S)|}}{{|V(S)|}}{{|V(S)|}}}2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\Sigma_{d}}w\log w\right)}{\OOp({\Sigma_{d}}w\log w)}{\OOp({\Sigma_{d}}w\log w)}{\OOp({\Sigma_{d}}w\log w)}}$.
\end{corollary}
\par For each $k\in\N$, we can either find in time ${\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}2^{\mathchoice{\OOp\left(k\right)}{\OOp(k)}{\OOp(k)}{\OOp(k)}}$ a tree decomposition of width at most $5k+4$, or conclude that ${\mathchoice{\twOp\left(G\right)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}}>k$~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{BodlaenderDDFLP16}{}{}]}.
Additionally, any tree decomposition $(S^{\prime},\mathcal{X}^{\prime})$ of width $w$ can be made nice in time ${\mathchoice{\OOp\left(w^{2}\max({\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}},{\mathchoice{\left|V(S^{\prime}\right|}{{|V(S^{\prime}|}}{{|V(S^{\prime}|}}{{|V(S^{\prime}|}}})\right)}{\OOp(w^{2}\max({\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}},{\mathchoice{\left|V(S^{\prime}\right|}{{|V(S^{\prime}|}}{{|V(S^{\prime}|}}{{|V(S^{\prime}|}}}))}{\OOp(w^{2}\max({\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}},{\mathchoice{\left|V(S^{\prime}\right|}{{|V(S^{\prime}|}}{{|V(S^{\prime}|}}{{|V(S^{\prime}|}}}))}{\OOp(w^{2}\max({\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}},{\mathchoice{\left|V(S^{\prime}\right|}{{|V(S^{\prime}|}}{{|V(S^{\prime}|}}{{|V(S^{\prime}|}}}))}}$ while using at most $w{\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}$ nodes~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Kloks94}{}{}]}.
Combined with \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:tw-dp-running-time}, this concludes the proof of \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:gstp-fpt-tw-D}.
\par
\par\@@numbered@section{section}{toc}{Conclusion and Outlook}
In this paper, we provide the first fixed-parameter tractable algorithm for {Steiner Tree Packing} ($\stp$) parameterized by a structural parameter.
Concretely, we show that $\stp$ is $\fpt$ when parameterized by fracture number as well as tree-cut width.
This significantly extends the number of instances for which we know an exact polynomial time algorithm.
Previously known polynomial time algorithms are typically based on heuristics or approximations.
In case of the result that $\stp$ is $\fpt$ by ${\mathchoice{\left|T\right|}{{|T|}}{{|T|}}{{|T|}}}+d$, we do not even know a concrete algorithm, but only that one exists~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{RobertsonS95b}{}{}]}.
\par To achieve this goal, we generalize the notion of the augmented graph from {Edge-Disjoint Paths} ($\edp$) to {Generalized Steiner Tree Packing} ($\gstp$) and $\stp$.
This is the first result utilizing this tool on a problem where the terminals are arbitrary sets and not pairs of vertices.
The notion of augmentation has been used extensively for $\edp$, but was originally introduced for {Multicut}~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{GottlobL07}{}{}]}.
Despite the fact that many parameterized complexity results for the generalized version of this problem ({Steiner Multicut}) are known~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{BringmannHML15}{}{}]}, the augmented graph has not yet been considered in this setting.
We think that augmentation will also prove to be a valuable tool for {Steiner Multicut} and other similar problems in future research.
\par Further, we extend all known $\fpt$ algorithms for $\edp$ parameterized by a structural parameter to $\gstp$.
In addition, we provide a novel $\fpt$ algorithm for $\gstp$ parameterized by the tree-cut width of the augmented graph.
This settles whether $\gstp$ is $\fpt$ or $\wonehard$ parameterized by all eight commonly used structural parameters described in \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{sec:prelims} with respect to the augmented graph as well as the host graph.
As all these results coincide between $\edp$ and $\gstp$, this also completes such a complexity classification for $\edp$, where previously the result that $\edp$ is $\fpt$ by the tree-cut width of the augmented graph was not known.
\par For $\stp$ the established results are almost as complete.
We prove for six of these eight parameters that $\stp$ is $\fpt$.
It is known, that $\stp$ is $\wonehard$ parameterized by treewidth, even if ${\mathchoice{\left|T\right|}{{|T|}}{{|T|}}{{|T|}}}=3$~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{BodlaenderMOPL23,AazamiCJ12}{}{}]}.
So, the only question remaining here is whether $\stp$ is $\fpt$ parameterized by feedback vertex set number.
As $\edp$ is $\wonehard$ parameterized by the feedback vertex set number of the augmented graph~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{GanianOR21}{}{}]}, the approach employed in this paper—generalizing results from $\edp$ to $\gstp$ and applying them to $\stp$—is not suited to decide this questions.
Also, the techniques used by Bodlaender et~al.~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{BodlaenderMOPL23}{}{}]} to obtain the $\wonehard$ness result for {Integer 2-Commodity Flow} parameterized by treewidth, which generalizes to $\stp$ with ${\mathchoice{\left|T\right|}{{|T|}}{{|T|}}{{|T|}}}=3$~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{AazamiCJ12}{}{}]}, do not easily apply with respect to the feedback vertex set number.
We leave answering this question to future research.
\par Additionally, we improve upon the known running times for $\edp$ parameterized by the fracture number of the augmented graph and the sum of treewidth and number of terminal pairs.
For the former algorithm, we do not see a clear path to improving the running time to sub-doubly-exponential and we would not be surprised, if this result is conditionally optimal up to improvements in the polynomial.
For the later algorithm, we think that our algorithm is a stepping stone towards further improvement.
In fact, we conjecture that an algorithm building on our idea running in time ${\mathchoice{\OOp^{\ast}\left(2^{{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\twOp\left(G\right)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}}\sum_{T\in\mathcal{T}}d(T)\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\twOp\left(G\right)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}}\sum_{T\in\mathcal{T}}d(T))}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\twOp\left(G\right)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}}\sum_{T\in\mathcal{T}}d(T))}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\twOp\left(G\right)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}}\sum_{T\in\mathcal{T}}d(T))}}}\right)}{\OOp^{\ast}(2^{{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\twOp\left(G\right)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}}\sum_{T\in\mathcal{T}}d(T)\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\twOp\left(G\right)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}}\sum_{T\in\mathcal{T}}d(T))}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\twOp\left(G\right)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}}\sum_{T\in\mathcal{T}}d(T))}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\twOp\left(G\right)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}}\sum_{T\in\mathcal{T}}d(T))}}})}{\OOp^{\ast}(2^{{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\twOp\left(G\right)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}}\sum_{T\in\mathcal{T}}d(T)\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\twOp\left(G\right)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}}\sum_{T\in\mathcal{T}}d(T))}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\twOp\left(G\right)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}}\sum_{T\in\mathcal{T}}d(T))}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\twOp\left(G\right)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}}\sum_{T\in\mathcal{T}}d(T))}}})}{\OOp^{\ast}(2^{{\mathchoice{\OOp\left({\mathchoice{\twOp\left(G\right)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}}\sum_{T\in\mathcal{T}}d(T)\right)}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\twOp\left(G\right)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}}\sum_{T\in\mathcal{T}}d(T))}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\twOp\left(G\right)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}}\sum_{T\in\mathcal{T}}d(T))}{\OOp({\mathchoice{\twOp\left(G\right)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}{\twOp(G)}}\sum_{T\in\mathcal{T}}d(T))}}})}}$ can be obtained using the Cut\&Count technique introduced by Cygan et~al.~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{CyganNPPRW11}{}{}]}.

\par\lx@newpage\par\@@numbered@section{appendix}{toc}{Finding a Fracture Modulator is in FPT}
In this Chapter, we first examine why the known algorithm to find a fracture modulator in FPT-time in the size of the modulator is inaccurate.
Then, we present a fix to this algorithm.
This algorithm first appeared in the context of the incidence graph of ILP-instances~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{DvorakEGKO21}{}{}]} and was later republished for general graphs~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{GanianOR21}{}{}]}.
The algorithm is a given a graph $G$ and an integer $k$ and should now, decide whether is a fracture modulator in $G$ of size $k$.
It is based on two main ideas, which together yield a branching algorithm with a bounded search tree.
\par\begin{claim}\begin{enumerate}\enumerate@item@For every $U\subseteq V(G)$ with ${\mathchoice{\left|U\right|}{{|U|}}{{|U|}}{{|U|}}}=k+1$, if $G[U]$ is connected then at least one vertex of $U$ is contained in any fracture modulator of size $k$.
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@Assume $G$ is disconnected and let $S$ be a fracture modulator of $G$. Then, for every $C\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left(G\right)}{\compOp(G)}{\compOp(G)}{\compOp(G)}}$, the set $S\cap V(C)$ is a fracture modulator for $C$.
\end{enumerate}\end{claim}
\par To see the inaccuracy, consider the graph $P_{5}$.
We can verify that no set of size $1$ is a fracture modulator in $P_{5}$.
Now, let $H$ be a graph that consists of two disconnected $P_{5}$ graphs and denote with $S^{*}$ the set of the two central vertices of each of the $P_{5}$.
In $H-S^{*}$ every connected component has at most two vertices.
Thus, $S^{*}$ is a fracture modulator for $H$, which uses exactly one vertex of each of the disconnected $P_{5}$ graphs.
This refutes the claim by Dvorak et~al.~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{DvorakEGKO21}{}{}]}.
\par To fix this problem, we solve a slightly more general problem.
We call it {$(k,d)$-Fracture Deletion}.
In it we are given a graph $G$ and two natural number $k,d\in\N$.
The task is now to decide, whether there is a set $S\subseteq V(G)$ with ${\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}=d$ such that every connected component of $G-S$ has at most $k$ vertices.
We call $S$ a \emph{$k$-fracture deletion set} of size $d$.
We see that checking whether a fracture modulator of size $k^{\prime}\in\N$ exists is equivalent to deciding {$(k^{\prime},k^{\prime})$-Fracture Deletion}.
We can adopt \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{claim:fracture-modulator-observations} to this modified definition.
\par\begin{lemma}Let $k,d\in\N$, $G$ be a graph, and $S$ be a $k$-fracture deletion with ${\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}=d$.
Then,
\begin{enumerate}\enumerate@item@for all $U\subseteq V(G)$ with $k<{\mathchoice{\left|U\right|}{{|U|}}{{|U|}}{{|U|}}}$ such that $G[U]$ is connected, we have $U\cap S\neq\emptyset$
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@for all $C\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left(G\right)}{\compOp(G)}{\compOp(G)}{\compOp(G)}}$, the set $S\cap V(C)$ is a $k$-fracture deletion set of $C$.
\end{enumerate}\end{lemma}
\@proof\begin{enumerate}\enumerate@item@Let $U\subseteq V(G)$ with $k<{\mathchoice{\left|U\right|}{{|U|}}{{|U|}}{{|U|}}}$ be given such that $G[U]$ is connected and that $U\cap S=\emptyset$.
Then, $U$ is contained in a single connected component $C$ of $G-S$.
As $U\subseteq V(C)$, we have $k<{\mathchoice{\left|V(C)\right|}{{|V(C)|}}{{|V(C)|}}{{|V(C)|}}}$ which violates that $S$ is a $k$-fracture deletion set.
So, such an $U$ can not exist.
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@Now, let $C\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left(G\right)}{\compOp(G)}{\compOp(G)}{\compOp(G)}}$.
For all $D\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left(C-(S\cap V(C))\right)}{\compOp(C-(S\cap V(C)))}{\compOp(C-(S\cap V(C)))}{\compOp(C-(S\cap V(C)))}}$, we also have $D\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left(G-S\right)}{\compOp(G-S)}{\compOp(G-S)}{\compOp(G-S)}}$.
Thus, $V(D)\leq k$ and $S\cap V(C)$ is a $k$-fracture deletion set of $C$. \qed\end{enumerate}
\end@proof
\par Based on this, we can give a simple branching algorithm with a bounded search tree.
We distinguish three cases.
First, if ${\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}<d$, we report that no $k$-fracture deletion set of size $d$ exists.
Second, if $G$ is connected; find any $U\subseteq V(G)$ with ${\mathchoice{\left|U\right|}{{|U|}}{{|U|}}{{|U|}}}=k+1$ and $G[U]$ connected.
We can find $U$ using a breadth- or depth-first-search.
Now, we branch on every $u\in U$, whether to include it in the solution.
We recursively decide whether $G-u$ is a positive instance of {$(k,d-1)$-Fracture Deletion} and output that $G$ is a positive instance of {$(k,d)$-Fracture Deletion} if and only if at least one subinstance is positive.
Finally, assume $G$ is disconnected.
Let $\mathcal{C}\coloneqq\{C\in{\mathchoice{\compOp\left(G\right)}{\compOp(G)}{\compOp(G)}{\compOp(G)}}\mid k<{\mathchoice{\left|V(C)\right|}{{|V(C)|}}{{|V(C)|}}{{|V(C)|}}}\}$ be the components of $G$ that need to be broken up.
If ${\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{C}\right|}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}}=0$, we report that $G$ is a positive instance of {$(k,d)$-Fracture Deletion}.
If ${\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{C}\right|}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}}=1$, let $\{C\}\coloneqq\mathcal{C}$ and we report that $G$ is a positive instance of {$(k,d)$-Fracture Deletion} if and only if $C$ is a positive instance of {$(k,d)$-Fracture Deletion}.
If ${\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{C}\right|}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}}>d$, we report that there is no $k$-fracture deletion set of size $d$.
Otherwise, for each $C\in\mathcal{C}$, we search recursively for the smallest $d_{C}\in{\mathchoice{\left[d-1\right]}{[d-1]}{[d-1]}{[d-1]}}$ such that a $k$-fracture deletion set of size $d_{C}$ exists in $C$.
If there is no such $d_{C}$, report that there is no $k$-fracture deletion set in $G$.
Finally, we output that $G$ is a positive instance of {$(k,d)$-Fracture Deletion} if and only if $\sum_{C\in\mathcal{C}}d_{C}\leq d$.
\par\begin{theorem}Let $k,d\in\N^{+}$, and $G$ be a graph.
The presented algorithm correctly determines whether $G$ is a positive instance of {$(k,d)$-Fracture Deletion} and can be implemented in time ${\mathchoice{\OOp\left(\max(k+1,2d-1)^{d}{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}}\right)}{\OOp(\max(k+1,2d-1)^{d}{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}})}{\OOp(\max(k+1,2d-1)^{d}{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}})}{\OOp(\max(k+1,2d-1)^{d}{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}})}}$.
The algorithm can be modified to report a $k$-fracture deletion set of size $d$ in the same time, if one exists.
\end{theorem}
\@proof We first focus on the correctness in case of termination, and after that bound the running time which proves termination.
\par Using \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:k-d-fracture-deletion-helper}, we see that the first two cases are correct.
Now, we focus on the third case.
When ${\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{C}\right|}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}}=0$, any subset of size $d$ of $V(G)$ is a $k$-fracture deletion set.
As ${\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}\geq d$, such a set exists and returning that the instance is positive in this case is correct.
If ${\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{C}\right|}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}}=1$, let $\{C\}\coloneqq\mathcal{C}$.
Consider any $k$-fracture deletion set $S$ of size $d$ in $G$.
We know that $S\cap V(C)$ is a $k$-fracture deletion set in $C$ and as ${\mathchoice{\left|S\cap V(C)\right|}{{|S\cap V(C)|}}{{|S\cap V(C)|}}{{|S\cap V(C)|}}}\leq d\leq{\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{C}\right|}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}}$, we know that we can extend $S\cap V(C)$ to a $k$-fracture deletion set of size $d$ in $C$.
Additionally, any $k$-fracture deletion set of size $d$ in $C$ is a $k$-fracture deletion set of size $d$ in $G$.
So, the algorithm is correct in this case as well.
Regarding the case ${\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{C}\right|}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}}>d$; we know from \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:k-d-fracture-deletion-helper}, that for each $k$-fracture deletion set $S$ and $C\in\mathcal{C}$, we have $S\cap V(C)\neq\emptyset$.
Thus, any $k$-fracture deletion set has size at least ${\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{C}\right|}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}}$; so, returning that the instance is negative when ${\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{C}\right|}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}}>d$ is correct.
\par Now, for each $C\in\mathcal{C}$ denote with $S_{C}$ a $k$-fracture deletion set of size $d_{C}$ and set $S\coloneqq\bigcup_{C\in\mathcal{C}}S_{C}$.
We can verify that $S$ is a $k$-fracture deletion set in $G$ with ${\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}=\sum_{C\in\mathcal{C}}d_{C}\leq d$.
To obtain a $k$-fracture deletion set of size $d$, we add arbitrary vertices of $G$ to $S$ until ${\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}=d$.
This again works as ${\mathchoice{\left|V(G)\right|}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}{{|V(G)|}}}\geq d$.
Assume that there is a $k$-fracture deletion set $S$ of size $d$.
By \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:k-d-fracture-deletion-helper}, we have ${\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{C}\right|}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}}\leq d$.
For each $C\in\mathcal{C}$, we have $d_{C}\leq{\mathchoice{\left|S\cap V(C)\right|}{{|S\cap V(C)|}}{{|S\cap V(C)|}}{{|S\cap V(C)|}}}={\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}-{\mathchoice{\left|S\setminus V(C)\right|}{{|S\setminus V(C)|}}{{|S\setminus V(C)|}}{{|S\setminus V(C)|}}}\leq d-1$.
Additionally, $\sum_{C\in\mathcal{C}}d_{C}\leq\sum_{C\in\mathcal{C}}{\mathchoice{\left|S\cap V(C)\right|}{{|S\cap V(C)|}}{{|S\cap V(C)|}}{{|S\cap V(C)|}}}\leq{\mathchoice{\left|S\right|}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}{{|S|}}}=d$ and the algorithm correctly reports that there is no $k$-fracture deletion set of size $d$ if $\sum_{C\in\mathcal{C}}d_{C}>d$.
\par To bound the running time, we bound the number of nodes in the recursion tree.
The first case does not make any recursive calls, while the second case makes at most $k+1$ recursive calls each decreasing $d$ by one.
Now, consider the third case.
If ${\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{C}\right|}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}}=1$, we make one recursive call that does not decrease $d$.
However, the instance $C$, on which we recurse, is connected.
Thus, we make at most $k+1$ recursive calls directly on $C$, which all decrease $d$ by at least one.
If ${\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{C}\right|}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}}\geq 2$, we make recursive calls which all decrease $d$ by at least one.
To bound, how many such recursive calls we make, we first need to specify how the search for $d_{C}$ is carried out.
We choose to select each $C\in\mathcal{C}$ after another and linearly search from 1 to $d_{C}$.
Additionally, we abort when it is clear that the sum of $d_{C}$ will exceed $d$.
Each call to a negative instance increases the minimum sum by $1$.
As this sum starts at ${\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{C}\right|}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}}$, we do at most $d-{\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{C}\right|}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}}+1$ calls to instances which turn out to be negative.
Additionally, since we stop the search for each $C\in\mathcal{C}$ once we recursively call a positive instance, we do at most $d$ such calls.
Therefore, we do at most $2d-{\mathchoice{\left|\mathcal{C}\right|}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}{{|\mathcal{C}|}}}+1\leq 2d-1$ such recursive calls.
This means, the branching factor is bounded by $\max(k+1,2d-1)$ and the branching depth is bounded by $d$, which combined with the fact that at each recursion step we do at most a linear amount of additional work, yields the desired result.
\qed\end@proof
\par We can combine \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{stmt:k-d-fracture-deletion-algo} with the fact that for each $k\in\N$ a fracture modulator of size $k$ is a $k$-fracture deletion set of size $k$ to obtain an $\fpt$-algorithm for finding minimum fracture modulator.
\par\begin{corollary}Let $G$ be a non-empty graph and $k\coloneqq{\mathchoice{\fracNumOp\left(G\right)}{\fracNumOp(G)}{\fracNumOp(G)}{\fracNumOp(G)}}$ be the fracture number of $G$.
There is an algorithm that finds a fracture modulator of size $k$ for $G$ in time ${\mathchoice{\OOp\left((2k-1)^{k}{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}}\right)}{\OOp((2k-1)^{k}{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}})}{\OOp((2k-1)^{k}{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}})}{\OOp((2k-1)^{k}{\mathchoice{\left|G\right|}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}{{|G|}}})}}$ if one exists.
\end{corollary}
